Categories
See Also
A category represents a type of information you want to store. For
example, if you want to track information about people, you would
use the Person/Company category for storing the data you enter
in Commence.
 
Categories consist of named fields. For example, the
Person/Company category consists of fields such as Name,
Address, and Phone. When you enter data into Commence, you
enter data into these fields just as you would enter data into a
paper form. There are different types of fields, similar to ones you
would find on a paper form. Unlike a paper form, Commence's
field types also give you:
 
a smart selection capability, called filtering, that lets you
set different types of selection criteria based on field type.
For example, if you are viewing the Notes/History
category, you can create a filter that displays only the
letters that were sent to the Acme Company during
February, 2006. (For more information, see Customize-
Filter View).
other special capabilities based on field type. For
example, you can dial a telephone field, perform
calculations on number fields, access external files, etc.
 
You use a screen called a detail form to enter data into
Commence. This form identifies the fields in an item that you
want displayed and lets you enter data values. You also use a
detail form when you edit data. Commence comes with at least
one pre-defined form for every category. You can edit these forms
at any time to change the arrangement of the fields, their fonts,
which fields appear in the form, and so on. You can also add
more forms for existing or new categories.
 
When you enter data into Commence using a form and save it,
you are creating an item. Before you can save an item, you must
enter a value in the name field. This special field type is the only
required field type in Commence. Commence uses it to locate
your data items, since every item in every category has one name
field value.
Views and View Types
See Also
A view displays the items stored in one or more categories.
Commence comes with one or more pre-defined views for each
category.
 
A view is made up of one or more fields, such as Name, Address,
Phone and Email. When you display a view, Commence gets the
field values for each item in the category and displays the values
in the view. Therefore, even if you defined a view weeks ago, it
will always display up-to-date data.
 
How the view appears and what special capabilities it has are
determined by the view type. Commence provides you with four
different view types:
 
Book Displays up to four fields with alphabetical book tabs for
quickly locating names.
Calendar Displays the names of items by day and time slot and
displays a busy bar that summarizes the entire day. Provides
navigational controls for moving quickly and easily to different
days. Lets you color code your appointments based on data in
the category, such as the type of appointment (meeting, phone
call, etc).
Gantt Chart Displays the names of items as a Gantt chart
showing the start and end dates of items in a category such as
Task. Lets you color code your tasks to indicate, for example, the
status of each task.
Grid Displays fields from one or more categories in a row/column
format. It can be used in two modes: read-only and update mode.
Document Displays a Web page as a view window on the
Commence desktop. The HTML view displays HTML content
directly from the Commence database or from a remote URL
address by hosting the Internet Explorer Web browser within
Commence.
Multi-View Displays a set of related views in one window frame.
The view is split to show the primary and secondary views.
Report Displays fields from one or more categories in a row-
column format. Optionally displays the total and averages of
certain field types. This is the default view type.
Report Viewer Displays data from your Commence database in
WYSIWYG format as a print preview. Data can be viewed,
printed, and exported to various file formats.
 
When you define a view, you always select a view type as well as
a category. Commence comes with a number of pre-defined
views. Some of these can be seen on the View bar to the left of
the Commence screen, and at the bottom of the View menu. The
entire set of pre-defined views in the active database can be seen
and opened using the View-Open/Manage Views command. From
this dialog box, you can add, edit, or delete a view at any time.
Editing the view lets you change the name of a view and decide
whether you want it to appear on the view bar and/or the Views
menu. You can also create a new view for displaying your data.
 
You can use the Customize-Sort View command to change the
sort order of the items displayed in report and Gantt view types.
 
You can view a subset of the items in a category by selecting
Customize-Filter View.
 
See the Customize menu topic for a description of these and
other commands that change the active view.
 
Changing the attributes of a view is temporary unless you save
the view definition changes using View-Save or View-Save View
As.
Connections
See Also
A connection ties together, or links, two related items. A
connection definition is a link between two categories. It creates
relationships between items in the same or different categories.
To add a connection definition to a database, select Customize ->
Database, followed by the Connection tab.
 
On a detail form, Commence provides you with a special three-
button control for each connection definition called a connection
control. This control lets you connect and disconnect items easily.
If you use this control to add a new item in the connected
category, Commence will automatically connect it to your source
item. Double clicking on the item name field in the control lets you
navigate to the detail form of the connected item.
 
You can easily add or rename a connection definition at any time
without affecting your data. You can also delete a connection
definition you don't want. Commence automatically updates your
detail forms when you add, edit and delete connection definitions.
It automatically updates saved views when you edit and delete
connection definitions.
Agents
See Also
An agent lets you instruct Commence to automatically perform
tasks for you.
 
A simple agent consists of a trigger and an action. The trigger
defines the event or time that will cause the agent to operate, and
the action determines what task will be performed. For example,
an agent could trigger every Friday at 9 am and print a list of
Calendar items for today.
 
There are four classes of triggers: timer, manual, data item and
external. A Timer trigger can be set to occur periodically or can
be set to occur only once at a given date and time. The manual
triggers (Menu Pick, Tool Bar Pick, and Keyboard Hotkey) fire
when you select the trigger from the Tools menu or the tool bar,
respectively. The Save Item trigger fires when you add and/or
edit an item, while the Item Alarm trigger fires based on the date
and time in an item. The external trigger (DDE) fires when
Commence receives a specified DDE message from another
Windows application.
 
Agents can also depend on one or two optional conditions. These
conditions can be used to further define the scope and operation
of the agent. For example, a condition can filter for a particular
subset of items in a category, such as all the Client type items in
the Person/Company category.
 
Up to four actions can be included in a single agent. You can
choose from four classes of actions: alerts, data
modification/display, Commence commands, and external
commands. The alerting actions that can be used as warnings,
reminders or notifications. The data modification/display actions
let you display views or let you add and edit data items. The
Commence command actions, such as Print Letter and Use
Telephone let you automatically perform certain menu commands
from within Commence. External command actions let you launch
an application or send a DDE message to another application.
 
See the Summary Tables, for a complete summary of all the
triggers, conditions, and actions supplied with Commence.
 
For detailed information about how to create new agents or
modify existing ones, see the Customize-Database-Agent
command.
Databases
See Also
A database is a set of categories, views and agents that you want
to store in Commence. Commence provides you with a pre-
defined database which is a set of simple categories, views and
agents that support customer service, project management and
contact management. This database has the same category, view
and agent definitions that you see when you use the Commence
tutorial. You can use the database as-is or you can customize it to
suit your needs. Or you can create a custom database from
scratch.
 
On your hard drive, each database is stored in its own directory
and, because Commence is based on a proprietary database,
you will see many separate files on disk within that directory.
Each file, no matter how small, serves a unique and important
purpose. Deleting or renaming Commence data files can result in
an unrecoverable loss of data.
 
Use the Open/Manage Database to open a different database; to
add, delete or rename databases; or to backup or restore a
database.
 
Regardless of the database you have open, you have access to
all of the capabilities that Commence provides: view types,
sorting, filtering, dialing, creating letters, etc.
 
You can move data between Commence databases or to other
database applications using the Tools-Export-By Category, Tools-
Export-By View, and Tools-Import commands. You can move
category, view, and agent definitions (and optionally, data)
between Commence databases using the Tools-Transfer Out and
Tools-Transfer In commands.
 
You can synchronize databases on different PCs using the Tools-
Sync Link Out and Tools-Sync Link In commands.
 
If you use more than one database, you will need to customize
each database environment separately. See the various
Preferences commands on the Customize menu for more
information.
Drag and Drop Operations
Some view types allow you to use your mouse to pick up an item
and perform one of the following drag and drop operations:
Print the item
Delete the item
Dial a phone number in the item
Send a letter using field values in the item
Move the item to a new date or time in a calendar view
Copy the item to another category
Reset the field values of an item based on the filter in the
destination view
You can also use drag and drop to re-structure filters and agents
by dragging the filter, agent condition, or agent action and then
dropping it to the new location.
 
To drag an item, click the left mouse button on the item and hold
the button down while you move the mouse pointer. The icon
changes to a sheet of paper (with a plus sign if it is a copy
operation) when you are over a "droppable" location. If you are
over an invalid location, the icon changes to a circle with a line
through it.
 
To drop the item, simply release the mouse button.
 
If the destination view is not visible, you can drop the selected
item on the magnet on the tool bar, open a different view, and
then drag the item from the magnet to its new location.
Copy an Item
You can copy an item by dragging the item to the magnet, then
dropping it back onto the same view, or by dragging an item to a
different view of the same category. To copy an item from one
category to another, drop the item on an open view for the other
category. If any field in both categories have identical field
names, those field values will also be copied to the new item. The
same applies to connections between the two categories.
Whenever possible, Commence changes the values of the new
item to match the values in the destination view's filter (if any).
Note: If you are dragging an item from a Calendar view, the
default drag and drop operation is move. You can force a copy
operation by holding down the Ctrl key while dragging the item.
If you drop the item on a calendar view, Commence will set the
date and, if possible, the start time, depending on where the item
was dropped.
Move an Item
You can move an item by holding down the Shift key while
dragging the item and dropping it onto a different view of the
same category. To move an item from one category to another,
drop the item on a view for a the other category. Move works the
same way as copy does with one major difference; the original
item is deleted after the move operation. Field values, connected
items and filters are handled in a move the same as with a copy.
 
Move an Item within the Calendar View
Since rescheduling appointments is the most common drag and
drop operation done in the Calendar view, move is the default
drag and drop operation. So is not necessary to hold down the
Shift key in this case. (See the note above for information about
forcing a copy operation.)
You can reschedule an item by dragging it to a new location on
the calendar or the mini-calendars. If you want to change
calendar dates or modes, you can drop the selected item on the
magnet on the command bar, change your date or mode, and
then drag your item from the magnet to its new date/time.
 
Reset Field Values Based on the Destination Filter
When you drop an item onto a filtered view, Commence tries to
make the item qualify for the view.
 
Note: If your filter specifies an indeterminate value, such as a
date range, Commence will not be able to set a value that
qualifies your item for the filter, so it will not appear in the view.
Caution: If an item seems to disappear after using drag and
drop, check the state of the Except checkbox in your filter. When
the except checkbox is used to invert a filter, drag and drop will
reset the field values of the item to match the filter but will
disregard the except setting. This will prevent the item from
appearing in the view.
Prefix Matching on the Name field
See Also
Prefix matching allows you to find a particular item in a long list of
items in a report view, Gantt chart view, grid view Book view, or
connection definition. For example: You are looking at a report
view of people from your Person/Company category. You need to
find the item for Paul Smith among the 30 other names starting
with Smith in the category. Type "smith, p" at your keyboard. The
characters typed are echoed in the status bar at the bottom of the
screen (these characters are the current prefix string).
 
As you type the prefix string, the highlight in the view moves to
the first item in the list whose Name field contains the prefix
string. Use the Crtl + N (hold down the Control key and then
press the "N" key) to move to the next prefix match. If there is no
match for a character that you type, you will hear a beep and the
character does not appear on the status bar.
 
Use the Backspace key to cancel the last character typed, and
return the highlight to the previous matching item (i.e., the first
item in the list that begins with the remaining characters on the
status bar).
 
The current prefix string remains in effect (and is displayed on the
status bar) until:
you press Esc.
you use one of the arrow or other navigation keys.
you activate another window.
you select a menu command.
a message box appears.
the active view is updated with a new or edited item via an
agent, a DDE message, or, for a client database, a
synchronization with the Server database.
 
If you are searching through a connection control (either the list of
connected items or the list of all items in the connected category),
then choosing another connection control also cancels the current
prefix string.
Note: In connection controls only, press Ctrl + Spacebar to
insert a space in the current prefix matching string.
Natural Language Dates and Times Commence
interprets date and time values entered in plain
English words (e.g., tomorrow, noon).
 
Select a topic below to see a description of the valid formats for
each type of field:
Natural Language Dates for the Date Field
Natural Language Times for the Time Field
Add a Command Line Option
See Also
Command line options are useful if you want to control how
Commence starts up. For example, if you always want to open
the same database, you can use the /DATABASE= option, or if
you run multiple instances of Commence and do not want to be
bothered with the message that there is already a copy running
message, you can use the /QUIET option.
 
To add a command line option, follow the steps below:
 
If you start Commence with an icon on the desktop:
1. Single click on the icon to highlight it.
2. Right click the icon to bring up the menu.
3. Select Properties.
4. Select the Shortcut tab.
5. At the end of the text displayed in the Target: box, add a
space, then the command line option.
6. Select OK.
 
If you start Commence with a menu selection from the Start
menu:
1. Open the Start menu.
2. Select Settings.
3. Select Taskbar.
4. Select the Start Menu Programs tab.
5. Select the Advanced button.
6. In Explorer, find the Commence program menu item.
7. With the menu item highlighted, right click the selection to
bring up the menu.
8. Select Properties.
9. Select the Shortcut tab.
10. At the end of the text displayed in the Target: box, add a
space, then the command line option
11. Select OK and close all other dialog boxes.
Backup a Commence Database
See Also
You should regularly backup (make a copy) of your
Commence database. This is incase you encounter
problems with your PC or Commence in which your must
recover your data. You can also schedule regular backups
using an agent.
 
To backup a database:
1. Click the Commence Application button, and select
Open/Manage Database.
2. Highlight the database you wish to backup.
3. Select the Backup button.
4. Select the path and the filename you wish to use for
the backup file. The default extension is .XFR. Note: If
the database is large and you wish to backup to floppy,
Commence will span the backup over multiple
diskettes.
5. Select Save.
Restore a Commence Database
See Also
If you have hard drive problems or have had problems with
your Commence database, it may be necessary to restore
your database from a backup made earlier.
 
NOTE: You should regularly backup (make a copy) of your
Commence database. This is incase you encounter
problems with your PC or Commence in which your must
recover your data. You can also schedule regular backups
using an agent.
 
To restore a database:
1. Open a database other than the one you want to
restore to. You cannot restore to the open database.
2. Select Open/Manage Database.
3. Highlight the database you wish to restore to. If you
want to restore to a new database, you will need to
create it by selecting the Add button.
4. Select the Restore button.
5. If the highlighted database is already populated, a
restore will totally delete all files in that directory. This
cannot be reversed. Select Yes if this is what you want
to do, otherwise, select No and then select a different
database to restore to or add a new one.
6. Select the path and the filename of the file you wish to
restore. The default extension is .XFR.
7. Select Open.
8. If the database is being restored from diskette and it
occupies more than one, you will be prompted when to
insert the next diskette.
Open a Database
 
Follow the steps below to open a Commence database.
 
1. Select the Commence Application button, then choose
Open/Manage Database.
2. Highlight the database you wish to open.
3. Select the Open button.
Shortcut: Double-click the mouse on a database name to
open it without selecting the Open button.
Add a Database
 
Follow the steps below to add a Commence database:
 
1. Select the Commence Application button, then choose
Open/Manage Database.
2. Select the Add button.
3. Enter the Name of the database you wish to add. The
name is limited to 50 characters.
4. Enter the path to the database. The path can be an
existing Commence database that is no longer
referenced, an existing directory, or a directory that
does not exist yet.
5. Select Save. If the directory does not exist,
Commence will ask you if you want to create it.
Edit a Database
 
Follow the steps below to edit a Commence database.
 
1. Select the Commence Application button, then choose
Open/Manage Database.
2. Highlight the database you wish to edit.
3. Select the Edit button.
4. Change the Name of the database. Only the Name
can be changed. If you need to change the path, you
should backup the database, add a new database,
then restore into the new database.
Delete a Database
 
Follow the steps below to delete a Commence database.
 
1. Select the Commence Application button, then choose
Open/Manage Database.
2. Highlight the database you wish to delete.
3. Select the Delete button.
4. When prompted “Are you sure?”, select Yes.
5. When prompted for confirmation of deletion of all files
and folders in the database directory, type in “Yes” and
then the OK button.
 
You cannot delete the active database. Commence will
also not let you delete a database if it is the only database
defined.
 
Caution: When you delete a database, Commence gives
prompts you for confirmation of the deletion. If you select
Yes, Commence will warn you that all files and folders
contained in the selected database will be deleted. If you
wish to proceed, type “yes” then select the OK button. If
you do not wish to continue, select the Cancel button.
After the files are deleted, there is no way of recovering
them.
Adding a Field to a Category
Sometimes you want to track some additional information for a
category. For example, you may want to keep track of who you
sent a card to during the holidays. To do this, you could add a
check box field called Sent Card. When you send a card to the
person, you would then check the Sent Card check box.
To add a field to a category:
1. Select Customize-Database select the Category tab. Highlight
the name of the category you wish to edit and click on the Edit
button. This will display the Category Definition dialog box.
2. Select the Add button.
3. Enter a name for the field you wish to add in the Field Label
text box. This name is limited to 20 characters and it cannot
be the same name as any other existing fields.
4. Select a Field Type for the field. The following field types are
available in Commence. To see a definition of the field, select
the field type name below.
Calculation
Check Box
Data File
Date
E-Mail
Excel Cell
Image
Number
Selection
Sequence Number
Telephone
Text
Time
URL (Internet)
 
5. To specify a value to automatically appear when you add an
item to the category, put a default value in the Default text
box. For example, if you add a Date field, you may want the
default value to be today's date. To do this, enter Today in the
default value. To have a Check Box field value appear as
checked, set the default value to checked.
Note: Not all fields are capable of having default values.
6. If you entered a date field, you have the option of making it a
Recurring Date Field. Checking this box will allow you to add
a group of items that occur at regular intervals. For example, a
staff meeting that occurs every Monday.
7. To ensure that a field always has a value, check the
Mandatory Field check box. Commence will display an error
message if you save an item without a value for this field. You
will not be able to save the item until you enter a value for all
mandatory fields.
8. When all the options are set the way you wish, select OK. For
more information of field specific options, select a field type
then press F1.
9. To add another field, continue from step 3.
10. When you are done adding fields, select the Save button and
all your changes will be saved. The time required to save the
category depends on the number of items you have in the
category.
11. To see the changes you have made, select the
mage/additem_shg.gif
button on the tool bar You will see the new fields at the bottom
of the item detail form. To move the new field to another
position on the form, , use the Customize-Design View
command, while the detail form is open. This will display the
Design Detail Form dialog box.
Editing a Field in a Category
To change a field's name or options::
Note: When editing a field, you can only change the field's name
and options; you cannot change the field's type.
 
1. Select Customize-Database. and click on the Category tab.
Highlight the name of the category you wish to edit and click
on the Edit button. This will display the Category Definition
dialog box.
2. Highlight the field you wish to edit in the Fields list box.
3. Select the Edit button.
4. Make the changes to the field label and field options as
needed. Remember, the field type cannot be changed.
Note: If you change the Total Size of a text field to a smaller
size, any existing items that have text in this field may lose
some data. The field will be reduced to the new size. Also, any
changes made to radio group will cause the existing items
radio buttons to change to the new values. Lastly, if any letter
template references a field whose name has been changed,
Commence will not automatically update it. You must edit the
letter template and update the field code to use the new field
name.
5. Select OK when you are done editing the field.
6. To edit more fields, continue from step 3.
7. When you are done editing fields, select the Save button and
all your changes will be saved. The time required to save the
category depends on the number of items you have in the
category.
Customizing a Detail Form
You can customize the detail form by:
* Specifying individual field attributes such as font, size, label
alignment, and color.
* Arranging the position of the fields on the screen by manually
moving fields.
* Changing the color of the form's background.
* Add graphics and text to the form.
* Remove fields from the form.
* Add form tabs and distribute the fields across multiple pages.
 
To arrange a detail form, do the following:
1. Open a view of the category for which you wish to edit the
detail form. For help, see Opening a View.
2. Select Edit-Add Item from the menu bar.
3. A form is displayed. Select the mage/design_shg.gif
button from the tool bar or the Customize-Design View
command.
4. The Detail Form Design dialog box is displayed. For more
information, see Designing Detail Forms or refer to Chapter 5
of the Commence User’s Guide.
5. Select File-Save to save your changes when you are done
editing the form.
Select the File-Exit to cancel all the changes you have made.
6. You are returned to the open detail form in Commence. Notice
that the new form arrangement is in effect. Select the Cancel
button on the form toolbar to return to the Commence work
area.
Working with Views
See Also
A view is a formatted display of category data. You create a view
by combining a category and a view type. The format of the view
is determined by the view layout.
To see how to work with views, select one of the following topics:
Opening a View
Saving a View
Displaying a field that is not in a View
Changing the fonts used in a Report
Sorting a View
Filtering a View
Link Views
Opening a View
If the view is on the view bar, the vertical bar to the left of
the Commence screen:
1. Locate the view you wish to open. If necessary, use the
up and down arrows at the top of the view bar to scroll
the buttons.
2. Left click on the view you wish to open.
Note: If you do not have a mouse, you cannot access any
of the views on the view bar.
 
If the view is on the View menu:
1. Select the View menu.
2. Select the view you wish to open.
 
If you believe the view to be saved, but it is not on the
view bar or View menu, select the View-Open/Manage
Views command. This will display a list of all saved views.
 
If the view you are looking for doesn't exist:
1. Select the View-Open/Manage Views command from
the menu bar. The Open/Manage View dialog box is
displayed. Select the Create New button.
2. In the Category: list box, highlight the name of the
category you wish to view.
3. In the View As radio group, select the view type to use.
For more information on view types, see Views and
View Types.
4. If you choose to use the Report view type, you have a
choice of displaying all the fields in the category or just
the Name field.
5. Select the View button.
Note: This view is just temporary. If you close the view
without saving it, the view will be lost. To make the view
permanent, save the view with View-Save View or
select Yes when Commence asks to save the view.
Saving Changes to a View
If you have made changes to a view and you want this
view to always open with these changes, you must save
the view.
To save changes to a view, either:
Select View-Save View. The view will be saved under the
original name.
Or select Yes when Commence asks to save changes to
the view.
Displaying a Field that is not in a View
From a Report/Grid View
From a Book View
From a Calendar View
From a Gantt View
Changing the fonts used in a Report/Grid View
To change the font used by a field in a report or grid view:
1. With the view open and active, select Customize-
Design View and select the Fields tab.
2. Highlight the field you wish to change in the Fields in
Report list box. If the field you wish to change is not in
the view, see Displaying a Field that is not in a View.
3. Select the Field Options button. This will display the
Set Report Field Options dialog box
4. Select the font, font size, and any other options you
want.
5. Select OK.
6. Repeat from step 2 for each field you wish to change.
7. Select OK in the Fields tab dialog box when you are
finished with your changes.
Note: This change is just temporary. If you close the view
without saving it, the change will be lost. To make the
changes permanent, save the view with View-Save
View or select Yes when Commence asks to save
changes to the view.
To change the font of every field displayed in a view one
step:
1. With the view open and active, select Customize-
Design View and select the Fonts tab.
2. Select the font and font size you want and select
Change All.
3. Select OK in the Fonts tab dialog box when you are
finished with your changes.
Sorting a View
The views that can be sorted are Report Views, Grid
VIews and Gantt Views.
1. Select the Customize-Sort View command.
2. Select the field to sort on from the Primary Field drop-
down list box
Note: You cannot sort on a field other than the Name field
of a connecting category.
3. Select an Ascending or Descending sort by selecting
the appropriate radio button.
4. If more than 1 item has the same value for the Primary
Field, you may wish to select a secondary sort field. To
do this, select a field from the Secondary Field drop-
down list box and select the Ascending or
Descending radio button.
5. Continue until you have set all the desired sort fields.
6. Select OK and the view will be sorted.
Note: When sorting on selection fields, the order is not
necessarily in alphabetic order. The sort is
accomplished using the order of the selection field
values.
Note: The sort is only temporary. If you close the view
without saving it, the sort will be lost. To make the sort
order permanent, save the view with View-Save View or
select Yes when Commence asks to save changes to
the view.
Filtering a View
A filter will select a subset of items for the active category
and display them in the active view. Note that a filter is
temporary. Once the view is closed, the filter is gone. To
make the filter a permanent part of the view, you must
save the view using View-Save View.
To filter a view, see:
Filtering on a Single Field Value
Filtering on More than One Field
Filtering on a Date Range
Finding Items with Blank Fields
 
 
 
 
Customize-Link Views
View Linking is a way to link two open views by a date, date
range, or by the highlighted item. For example, using View Link,
you can link your To-Dos view with the calendar. If your calendar
is currently displaying the current week, your To-Dos view will
also display the To-Dos for the current week. If you then change
the display mode of your calendar to display the current month,
the To-Dos view will change to show the To-Dos for the current
month. Another example is that you can link your
People/Company view to a view of Notes. When you change the
selected contact, you can see the notes referring to that contact.
 
To use View Link:
 
1. 
Open the two views you want to link.
2. 
Select Customize-Link Views. Select what type of View Link
you want to establish:
Selected Date in current Calendar view:
Will use a calendar view as the primary view to drive the
secondary view.
The secondary view will become filtered on the selected
date in the active calendar view. The secondary view
must have a Date field.
Date Range of current Calendar view:
Will use a calendar view as the primary view to drive the
secondary view.
The secondary view will become filtered on the date
range displayed in the active calendar view. (If only one
day is displayed (in day mode), the range is only one
day. If a month is displayed (in month mode), the range
is the whole month). The secondary view must have a
Date field.
Connection to highlighted item:
Will cause the selected primary view to drive the
secondary view. There must be a connection between
the categories used in the primary and secondary view.
The secondary view will display items connected to the
highlighted item in the primary view.
3. 
Select the Primary view. This is the view that will drive the
other view. For example, if linking views to show People
who are connected to a highlighted Company, this would
be the Company view.
4. 
Select the Secondary view. This is the view that will show
the filtered set of items. For example, if linking views to
show People who are connected to a highlighted
Company, this would be the view of People.
5. 
Select the Date Field or Connection Field to use in the View
Link.
6. 
When completed, select Save. The two views will be linked.
Note: A View Link is actually a filter on the secondary view. To
see how the filter is setup, make the secondary view active, then
select Customize-Filter View. Also, since View Link is a filter, it is
only temporary. You must save the view to save the Link.
 
 
 
 
Searching for Text in Multiple Fields and Across
Multiple Categories
See Also
Commence provides a global search feature to find all items
across all categories that contain or exactly match a particular
text string. Using global search, you can quickly locate all
pertinent information for an important meeting, find all the notes,
phone calls, etc., that refer to a particular contact or topic, display
all items from all categories with a certain date or time, and more.
 
Global search lets you scan all or part of a Commence database
for items containing a specified text string or wildcard pattern. If
you want, you can restrict the search to a particular set of
categories.
 
To perform a global search:
1. Select the Tools-Global Search command from the menu bar.
The Global Search dialog box is displayed.
2. Type the text you want to search for in the Search Text box.
3. Use the Match drop-down list to select whether you want to do
a fuzzy search or an exact one. Fuzzy searches are forgiving
of misspellings.
4. To restrict the search to exactly match the case (upper or
lowercase) of the search text, turn on the Match Case check
box.
5. Commence searches all categories by default. To exclude
categories from the search, use the Categories button.
6 Select the Start Search button to start the search. Items are
displayed in a list box as they are found. The Search Status
will be set to SEARCH COMPLETE when the search has
finished.
Finding Items in a Large List
See Also
You can use prefix matching to find a particular item in a long list
of items in a report view, Gantt chart view, Book view, or
connection control.
 
1. Display the view or connection control in which you want to
find the item. For help, see Opening a View.
2. Start typing the name of the item you want to find. As you type
the prefix string, the highlight in the view moves to the first
item in the list whose name contains the prefix string. The
prefix string also appears in the status bar. If there is more
than one item with the same string use Ctrl + N to move to the
next prefix match. If there is no match for a character that you
type, you will hear a beep
Copying an Item
Once data has been entered, you may need similar
information in a different item. You can copy any item to a
new item. This allows you to duplicate data into a new item
without retyping the information.
You can copy an item by dragging the item and dropping it
on a view. To copy an item from one category to another,
drop the item on a view for the other category. The values
in fields having identical names in the source and
destination fields are copied. For more information, see
Drag and Drop.
 
To copy an item within the same category:
1. Open the view where the item is stored. For more
information, see Opening a View.
2. Highlight the item or display it's form by double clicking
on the item name.
3. Select the Edit-Copy Item command.
4. A form appears for the active category. This form
contains the information from the existing item.
Change any field values you wish for the new item.
The original item will remain unchanged.
5. Select the Save button.
Designing a Custom Paper Layout
The Page Setup command is used to create new paper layouts.
With this command you can also edit, copy, and delete any paper
layout. While Commence comes with a variety of paper layouts
already installed, you may want to define a custom paper layout
to print information from Commence on a custom form (such as
an envelope or label form) and it to the choices presented in the
Paper list box of the Page Setup dialog box.
 
Commence's paper layouts allow you to print information from
your Commence database on virtually any form that can fit in
your printer. And you can do it just by entering the various
dimensions of the paper you are going to use.
 
To design a paper layout and enter it into Commence:
 
1. Measure the characteristics of the paper you want to use, as
shown in the diagram below.
 
image/paperlay_shg.gif
 
The example above shows a paper layout with 2 columns and 6
rows (12 regions). The internal margin defined for all regions
reduces the printed area in each region to the size of the
gray box shown in each region. If no internal margins are
chosen, the entire region area is used for printing.
Internal margins are particularly useful for labels that have
rounded corners, that do not meet at their edges, or that
have shipping holes.
2. Select the Home-Page Setup command.
3. Select the Add button on the Page Setup dialog box.
4. In the Paper list box, select the size of the paper you will use
in the printer when using this paper layout. You can choose
from among Commence's many pre-defined paper sizes
(which set the page height and width for you), or select
Custom to specify your own page height and width.
5. Enter the measurements from Step 1 into the appropriate
boxes on the Add Paper Layout dialog box. Make sure the
Sample shown matches your specifications.
6. Type a name for the layout into the Layout Name text box.
7. Select Save.
 
To use the new layout:
1. Open a view of the information you want to print using the
new page layout.
2. Use the Home-Printer Setup command to verify that the
current printer is the one you want to use.
3. Select Home-Print.
4. On the Print dialog box displayed, select the Layout Name
(see Step 6, above) that you gave the new paper layout from
the Paper list box. Choose other options as appropriate.
5. Make sure that you have the proper form(s) loaded into your
printer and that your printer is turned on. Select OK.
The information in the active view is printed using the new
paper layout.
Using Commence's Phone Features
Commence will dial phone numbers for you if you have Windows
Telephony (TAPI) installed and setup with a supported device
such as a modem.
The following How Do I? topics are available for Commence's
Phone Features:
Dialing a Telephone Number
Logging a Phone Call
The Ribbon
The Ribbon (previously the menu bar) is the row of menu
command buttons above the Commence work area just beneath
the title bar. The ribbon menu is always displayed; it can be
minimized to save space on the desktop. This collapses the
ribbon to show just the top-level menu commands. (See Edit-
Preferences-Tool Bars)
 
 
Toolbar
The Home page of the Ribbon features a Toolbar group of
buttons. These buttons are reserved for commonly used
Commence menu commands. The menu commands are
described below. Click each command for additional information.
 
[Drag and Drop] The first slot of the tool bar is not a button but a
place holder for drag and drop items.
 
Design View
Sort View
Filter View
Global Search
Add ? Item
Add Item
Delete Item
Dial Phone
Send Letter
Message Log
 
Agent Toolbar
Also on the Home page of the Ribbon is an Agent Toolbar group
of buttons. These buttons are reserved for user-created agent
buttons that have a Tool Bar Pick Trigger.
 
When creating the agent, you can choose a numbered position to
arrange the button in that specific position. Agent buttons are
shown in numerical order by position. A group can contain as few
as one agent, or up to 15 agents with consecutive position
numbers.
 
Quick Access Toolbar
The Quick access toolbar is a customizable row of buttons above
the Commence ribbon and just beneath the title bar.
 
The Quick access toolbar can be shown either above or below
the Ribbon. To switch positions, click the drop-down arrow at the
end of the Quick access toolbar, and choose 'Show Below the
Ribbon' or 'Show Above the Ribbon'.
 
Print
The Print button is shown on the Quick access toolbar by default.
This button can be hidden without being removed. Click the drop-
down arrow at the end of the toolbar, then remove the checkmark
next to 'Print'.
 
Add Buttons
To add a new button, right-click on any object in the Ribbon, then
choose 'Add to Quick Access Toolbar'. Buttons will be shown in
the order in which they were added.
 
To add a view to the quick access toolbar, first the view must be
added to the Ribbon. To do this,
1. Open the view.
2. Select Open/Manage Views from the View menu.
3. Click Edit on this screen to change the display settings for
the active view. If the View is not currently on the Ribbon,
then 'Display on View Menu for Desktop:' is set to (none).
Change this to (all).
4. On the Home menu, click the arrow in the Launch group for
Views.
5. Find your view; it will be listed in alphabetical order.
6. Right-click on the view name, then choose 'Add to Quick
Access Toolbar'. The view name will now appear in the Quick
access toolbar for all desktops.
Remove Buttons
To remove a button, right-click on the button in the Quick access
toolbar, then choose 'Remove from Quick Access Toolbar'.
 
 
Open/Manage Database
Selecting the Commence Application button, then choosing
Open/Manage Database displays the Open/Manage Database
dialog box. With this command, you can:
Open a different database
Add a new database
Edit an exiting database to determine its location or change
the database name
Backup a database
Restore from a backup of a Commence database.
 
A database consists of the information you enter into categories,
as well as the categories, views, agents, preferences, and other
features that you use.
 
ACTIVE DATABASE
The database that was open when you last exited Commence is
opened automatically when you start Commence; it is called the
active database. To access the data contained in another defined
database, you must open that database. When you do, the
previously open database will be closed automatically. Its data is
inaccessible until you open it again.
 
PASSWORD PROTECTED DATABASES
If you attempt to open a password-protected database, the
Database Password dialog box is displayed. To access the
database enter the password in the Password text box and
select OK. When you enter the correct password, the database is
opened. For more information on facilities to protect your data
from unauthorized access, see Database Protection.
 
 
ADDING DATABASES
You may want to create your own databases to keep certain data
separate or to create a unique application database. You can
define up to 1000 Commence databases. Views, categories,
agents, preferences, etc. defined in a particular database are
accessible only when that database is open. To open multiple
databases at the same time, you will need to run multiple copies
of Commence.
 
EDITING DATABASES
You may also want to change the name of an existing database,
or delete old databases no longer in use. The only thing you
cannot change once a database is created is the Windows folder
in which it resides.
 
The databases delivered with Commence reside in separate
directories under the Application Data Directory by default.
Their locations are selectable on installation.
 
TUTORIAL DATABASE
Maintaining data in separate databases protects data in one
database from changes you make in another. For example, if you
use the tutorial database included with Commence, you will
notice that none of the changes you make while in the tutorial
affect the data or other features in any other database. That's
because the tutorial data is contained in a separate database.
 
Note, however, that the tutorial database is not listed in the
Open/Manage Database dialog box. Also the Open/Manage
Database command is not available while you are using the
tutorial database. This is because entering tutorial mode entails
setting up an entire environment for your practice work as well as
switching to another database. (See Help-Tutorial Database for
more information about the tutorial database.)
Exit
See Also
Use this command to exit Commence and return to Windows.
Selecting the Commence Application button, then choose Exit.
This is equivalent to using the Control Menu Box to close the
Commence window.
 
If there are any currently open views, Commence checks to see if
any of them are new, unsaved views, or saved views that have
been changed and not saved.
 
If a changed view is a saved view, Commence checks your view
preferences to determine what changes you want saved and
whether to save the specified changes automatically (i.e., silently)
or to display the Save View dialog box. If your preferences are set
to Ask and then save View, Commence will ask if you want to
save the view. If you respond by selecting the Yes button, the
Save View dialog box is displayed with the name of the view filled
into the Name text box. You can save the changed view under its
own name, or type in another name.
 
If a changed view is a new unsaved view, Commence checks
your View Preferences. If the New View check box is checked,
Commence will ask if you want to save the view. If you respond
by selecting the Yes button, Commence will display the Save
View dialog box for you to type a name for the view and select
the Save button to save the view. All properties of the view are
saved.
 
 
 
 
Home-Workgroup Sync
Choosing this menu command causes the client database to
immediately synchronize any changes with the server database,
and updates your client database with any changes from the
server database. This command allows you to override the
automatic synchronization that occurs periodically, in cases
where you need to quickly retrieve any changes to the shared
database.
This command is useful when your database contains changes
that you want to share immediately with another user or if the
server contains changes that you need quickly. For example, if
you are using a client database stored on a notebook computer
and you are leaving the office, you can use the Workgroup Sync
command to retrieve the latest changes to the shared database
before disconnecting from the network.
This command is also useful when a client database is connected
to the network by a remote connection, such as remote access
software connected to the network via a modem. This command
can be used to quickly retrieve changes to the shared database
while the connection is available, reducing the amount of time
that the remote connection must be kept open.
 
 
 
 
Message Log This command displays a log of the
messages that have been posted to the status bar.
You can display this log by selecting the Message
Log command or selecting the equivalent toolbar
button. These messages include informational
messages displayed by commands and agents as
they execute that will help you diagnose and correct
problems encountered in Commence.
 
If the currently open database is a shared database and there is a
synchronization problem, the Message Log icon will change color
or shade. If this happens, you should check the Message Log to
find out what is wrong.
 
When you select Message Log, Commence displays the Message
Log dialog box. The Message Log icon is also included on the
Home ribbon menu and also the Help menu for convenience, and
may be added to the Quick Access Toolbar to ensure the icon is
visible at all times.
Send Letter
See Also
Use this command on the Home tool bar to create a letter for the
currently highlighted item (typically this is a person in one of your
person views) or for the active view. You can use any word
processor installed on your system to create your letter, as long
as you make sure that your letter preferences are set correctly
and the word processor has been configured for use with
Commence (see Word Processors for information).
 
When you select Send Letter, Commence displays the Send
Letter dialog box, from which you select the template for your
letter.
Shortcut: Drag an item in a view to the Letter button on the tool
bar to create a letter for the item.
 
You pick an already defined letter template (some are included
with Commence) as the model for the new letter. Based on the
contents of the template, field values from the highlighted item
are merged into the new letter. (See Customize-Database-Letter
Template for details on letter templates.)
 
After you select a template, you can chose to either print the
letter or preview the letter in your word processor (so you can
modify the letter before you print it or save it to a file).
 
If you get any error messages when attempting to send a
formatted letter from Commence, make sure you have followed all
the instructions for Configuring Your Word Processor for Use with
Commence
Dial Phone
See Also
Use this command on the Home tool bar, to dial a phone number
displayed by the active view or item detail. The Dial Phone
command is unavailable unless there is a telephone field present
in the active view or detail form.
 
If there is more than one phone number in the active view or item
detail, Commence will display the Select Telephone Number
dialog box from which you select the number you want.
 
Shortcut: Double click the left mouse button on a telephone field
in an item detail to dial the number in the field without selecting
Dial Phone. You can also drag an item in a view to the Dial
button on the tool bar to dial the items phone number. You can
also highlight a telephone number in a text field and then select
the Dial button on the tool bar.
 
If you have a dialing device such as a modem attached and have
correctly configured your Dialer Preferences, Commence will
transmit the phone number to your dialing device and display the
Commence Dialing dialog box. This dialog box displays the
number being dialed. It also displays a Hang Up button and a
Change Options button. The Hang Up button allows you to
terminate the dialing by disconnecting the modem. The Change
Options button allows you to change the dialing options via TAPI.
 
Note: You cannot select a phone number in a connected field
from a report view for dialing.
Send
Use this command to export the data in the active view to a
HyperText Markup Language (HTML) file. If there is a filter on the
view, only the items and fields displayed in the view will be
included in the HTML file. This command is available only if there
is an active view in calendar or report format.
 
Perform the following steps to save an HTML file:
1. Open a calendar or report view. If the view is not currently in
calendar or report format, use the View-View As command to
change the view type to either Calendar or Report.
2. Select the Send command from the menu bar to launch your
e-mail program. If prompted, enter your e-mail login or profile
information.
3. The active view will be converted to an HTML file and
attached to the e-mail message.
4. The subject of the e-mail message will automatically be filled
with the View Name from Commence.
5. Type the e-mail address of the recipient or select an e-mail
address from the Address Book of your e-mail program. The
names of all your contacts from Commence can be made
available to MAPI address books. For details on how to set
up a Commence Address Book in your e-mail program, see
the topic titled Configuring MS Outlook Address Book.
6. You may also click anywhere above or below the attached
HTML view and press Enter on your keyboard to insert a
blank line. Type any additional comments or other
information as needed in the body of the e-mail message.
7. Send the e-mail as you normally would when you are finished
previewing the message.
Print
See Also
Use this command to print the active view or the currently
displayed item detail. You can select Print from the Home menu
bar, press CTRL + P, or select the print button from the tool bar to
invoke this command.
 
Selecting Print from the Home menu bar, or selecting the print
button from the command bar, or dragging an item in a view to
the print button, displays a dialog box specific to the view type of
the view to be printed; there is also a specific dialog box for
printing item details.
 
After choosing appropriate options from the dialog box displayed
and selecting the OK button, Commence displays a window that
tells you the currently printing page number and the view or item
detail title. The page number restarts at 1 for each item detail
printed, if you choose to print item details. There is a Cancel
button in this window that you can select to cancel the job in
progress.
 
You can also "drag and drop" an item from the active view to the
Print button on the command bar. To do this from any view, click
and hold down the left mouse button on the item, move the
mouse pointer to the Print button, and release the mouse button.
Printer Setup
Use this command to choose printer options (such as available
fonts and page orientation) and select the default printer for
printed output.
 
Selecting Printer Setup from the Home menu bar displays the
Printer Setup dialog box.
 
 
 
 
 
Print Preview
See Also
The Print Preview option is used to see how a printout will look
prior to actually printing it out. This is an invaluable tool when
designing reports and other printed materials. By looking at the
Print Preview for a view, it can be determined if all information will
fit on a single page, if the information appears crowded, or if there
is room for additional information that may be desired to be
included
 
 
 
 
Page Setup
See Also
Use this command to create, edit, copy, and delete paper layouts
for use with the Print command. You can also associate a specific
printer with each paper layout; this printer will be selected
whenever that paper layout is used.
 
A paper layout in Commence is a flexible template for printing
views and item details. It specifies the dimensions of a paper form
you want to use in your printer and the printable area of the form.
When you print information from Commence, the paper layout lets
you specify exactly where you want view and item detail
information to go on the paper. Commence includes many pre-
defined paper layouts, which are suitable for various purposes.
These pre-defined paper layouts may also be edited and saved
under new names.
 
Selecting the Page Setup command from the Home menu bar
displays the Page Setup dialog box.
Launching Views from the Ribbon menu
A numbered list of saved views appears at the bottom of the
Home menu. Selecting a numbered view from the list opens the
chosen view.
 
You can place a view's name on the Home menu by selecting the
View-Open/Manage Views command. Highlight the name of the
view you want to place on the Home menu and click on Edit.
Mark the Display on Menu Bar check box.
 
The ability to place the names of up to 40 of your most frequently
used views on the Views menu is provided as a convenient
alternative to using the View-Open/Manage Views command.
Menu Pick Agents-1, 2, 3...
See Also
This topic describes the menu pick and tool bar pick agents in
Commence.
Menu Pick Agents
If you define and activate an agent with a menu pick trigger, the
menu pick will be added to the bottom of the Agent tab.
 
All active menu pick triggers are listed on the Tools menu. If a
menu pick trigger is associated with a particular category, then it
will be grayed out unless a view of that category is currently
active.
 
If you move the mouse over an agent menu pick, the status bar
will display the help message (if it exists) that was defined for the
trigger. When you deactivate an agent with a menu pick trigger,
its menu pick is removed from the menu.
 
Tool Bar Pick Agents
If you define and activate an agent with a tool bar pick trigger, a
user-selected button will be added to the Tool bar.
 
Buttons for all active tool bar pick agents are displayed on the
right side of the tool bar. If you move the mouse over an agent
tool bar pick, the status bar will display the help message (if it
exists) that was defined for the trigger. When you deactivate an
agent with a tool bar pick trigger, its button is removed from the
tool bar.
 
You can add your own buttons to the tool bar by defining an agent
with a Tool Bar trigger.
Edit-Cut
See Also
Use the Edit-Cut command (or press shift+delete) to delete
highlighted data and place a copy on the Windows Clipboard.
This command can be used to delete the highlighted item in the
active category from the database. It can also be used to delete
data from an individual field in an item detail.
 
This command is particularly useful for moving field values
between item details of items in the same or different views.
 
Typically, you use the Edit-Cut command to delete data from a
field that you want to move to another field. For items, only a
subset of the item's fields are copied to the Clipboard (see
below); any other data in the item is lost.
 
Display an item detail or view and highlight the field data or item
that you want to delete. Then, select the Edit-Cut command from
the menu bar.
 
For fields, only the visibly highlighted text is deleted and placed
on the Clipboard.
 
For items, the entire item is deleted from the Commence
database, regardless of how much of the item is actually visible in
the active view. The fields copied to the Clipboard depend on the
view type and category, as follows:
 
Book All fields in the view (see Customize-Design View)
Calendar Start time field for view (see Customize-Design
View), item name, and clarify field for the category (if
any)
Gantt chart Item name and clarify field (if any)
Report Item name and clarify field (if any)
 
The Edit-Cut command is available for any item displayed by the
active view. In an item detail, it is available only for image fields
and any edit box.
Edit-Copy
See Also
Use the Edit-Copy command (or press ctrl+insert) to copy
highlighted data to the Clipboard. This command can be used on
items in the active category and on fields in an item detail.
 
It is particularly useful for copying field values between items in
the same or different categories.
 
Typically, you use the Edit-Copy command to copy data from a
field that you want to move to another field. For items, only a
subset of the item's fields are copied to the Clipboard (see
below).
 
Display an item detail or view and highlight the field data or item
that you want to copy to the Clipboard. Then, select the Edit-
Copy command from the menu bar. Typically, you would then
display the item detail to which you want to copy the information
and use the Edit-Paste command to paste the data into a field.
 
For fields, only the visibly highlighted text is placed on the
Clipboard.
 
For items, the fields copied to the Clipboard depend on the view
type and category, as follows:
 
Book All fields in the view (see Customize-Design View)
Calendar Start time field for view (see Customize-Design
View), item name, and clarify field for the category (if
any)
Gantt chart Item name and clarify field (if any)
Report Item name and clarify field (if any)
 
The Edit-Copy command is available for any item displayed by
the active category. In an item detail, it is available only for image
fields and any edit box.
Edit-Clear
See Also
Use this command (or press delete) to delete highlighted data
without placing a copy on the Clipboard. This command can be
used on items in the active category and on image fields and edit
boxes in an item detail. If the cursor is in an edit box and no data
is highlighted, this command deletes the character directly to the
right of the cursor (if any).
 
Display an item detail or view and highlight the field data or item
that you want to delete. Then, select the Edit-Clear command
from the menu bar.
 
For fields, only the visibly highlighted text is deleted. For items,
the entire item is deleted, regardless of how much of the item is
actually visible in the active window.
 
The Edit-Clear command is available for any highlighted item
displayed by the active view. In an item detail, however, it is
available only for image fields and edit boxes.
Edit-Paste
See Also
Use this command (or press shift+insert) to copy data from the
Clipboard into a field. It is available only when an item detail is
displayed.
 
The Edit-Paste command is used to copy data into an item detail
field from the Clipboard.
 
Display an item detail and select the field into which you want to
copy the data that is currently on the Clipboard (optionally,
highlight any data you want to replace). Then, select the Edit-
Paste command. Data from the clipboard is copied into the
selected field, replacing any highlighted data.
 
The Edit-Paste command is available only for image fields and
edit boxes.
Edit-Copy To
See Also
Use this command to copy highlighted data to a file. It is available
only when an item detail is displayed and data in an edit box is
highlighted.
 
Display an item detail and highlight the field data that you want to
copy. Then, select the Edit-Copy To command from the menu
bar. A Copy To File dialog box is displayed that asks you for the
file name to which the data should be copied.
 
Edit-Copy To is available only for edit boxes. Only the visibly
highlighted text is copied to the file.
Edit-Paste From
See Also
Use this command to paste data from a file into a field. It is
available only when an item detail is displayed and the cursor is
located in an edit box. This command cannot be used to paste an
image from a file into an image field.
 
Display an item detail and place the cursor in the edit box into
which you want to read the field data. Highlight any field data to
be replaced. Then, select the Edit-Paste From command from
the menu bar.
 
The Paste From File dialog box is displayed. It asks you for the
file name from which the data should be pasted.
 
Data from the file is copied into the currently selected edit box,
replacing any highlighted data. If there is too much text in the file
to fit in the field, Commence asks you if you want to continue. If
you respond Yes, data is added to the field up to the capacity of
the field.
Edit-Global Search
See Also
Use this command, or the corresponding button on the tool bar, to
locate items from any or all categories containing specific text or
a wildcard pattern.
Using global search, you can quickly locate all pertinent
information for an important meeting, find all the notes, phone
calls, etc., that refer to a particular person or topic, display all
items from all categories with a certain date or time, and more.
Commence searches all name, text, date, time, data file, check
box, and radio group type fields for the given Search Text and
displays a list of items with fields that contain the search text.
Number, calculation, image, Excel cell, and sequence number
fields, as well as connection definitions, are not searched by the
global searching mechanism. To look for items based on
information in field types not scanned by global search, use
Customize-Filter View command
When you select Global Search, Commence displays the Global
Search dialog box. Type the text you want to search for in the
Search Text text box and select the Start Search button (the
label on this button then changes to Stop Search). Items are
displayed in a list box as they are found. A message box informs
you when the search is complete.
In the Search Text text box of the Global Search dialog box you
can use a question mark wildcard character to substitute for any
single character, and an asterisk (*) to substitute for one or more
characters.
Fuzzy searching is selected using the Match dropdown list.
Select "Fuzzy", "Fuzzier" or "Fuzziest" to accommodate possible
misspellings in the search text.
Commence searches all categories by default. Use the
Categories button to exclude categories from the search.
Commence looks for any field that contains the search text. Look
for exact matches of field contents by turning on the Equals radio
button. The case (upper or lowercase) of the search text is
ignored unless you turn on the Case Sensitive check box.
Selecting the Categories button on the Global Search dialog box
displays the Select Search Categories dialog box from which you
can alter the list of categories searched. On the first search after
selecting the Tools-Global Search command, all categories are
selected by default.
Find Find allows you to search for a text string in the
items in the current view or item detail. The Find
command will tell you of the first instance it finds of
the text string and lets you edit the item. You can
then continue your search. Find works on Name,
date, number, phone, text, time, Excel cell, and data
file fields.
 
If using Find in a view, Commence will only search the fields
included in the view. If you want to search fields that are not in
the view, you need to include them using Customize-Design
View-Fields. Also, Find will not search fields from connected
categories, even if they are displayed in the view.
Find & Replace
Find and Replace allows you to search for a text string in the
items in the current view or item detail and, if desired, replace it
with another text string. Find & Replace works on Name, date,
number, phone, text, time, Excel cell, and data file fields.
 
If using Find & Replace in a view, Commence will only search the
fields included in the view. If you want to search fields that are not
in the view, you need to include them using Customize-Design
View-Fields. Also, Find & Replace will not search fields from
connected categories, even if they are displayed in the view.
 
WARNING Be careful when using Find & Replace in a view and
using the Replace All button. All occurrences of the find text will
be replaced without being prompted and the changes CANNOT
be undone.
 
Note If you are using Find & Replace in an item detail, the
changes are not saved until you save the item.
Edit-Add ? Item
See Also
Use this command to add an item to any category. You can also
select the equivalent button from the tool bar or press ctrl+a
(with no active view) to invoke this command.
The Edit-Add ? Item command can be selected at any time to
add an item to any category.
When you select this command, Commence displays the Add
Item dialog box. After selecting the appropriate category and
detail form from the dialog box, the detail form you selected is
displayed.
Once you have completed filling in the fields on the detail form,
select the Save button to add the item; the Cancel button on the
detail form cancels the Add ? Item command.
If time conflict checking is enabled for the active category (see
Customize-Database-Category-Time Conflict), Commence checks
to see if the item being added conflicts with the date and time of
another item already in the category. If so, you are asked for
confirmation before the item is added.
If a detail form allows you to add recurring items to a category
(such as the Initial Form for the Appointment category) and
conflict checking is enabled for the, Commence checks to see if
any of the recurring items being added conflict with the dates and
times of any other items already in the category. If so, a list of all
conflicting item names is displayed and you are given the option
to ignore these conflicts or cancel the addition of all of the items.
Note that once the new item is added, it is not necessarily
displayed by the active view (if any); this depends upon the filter
used by the view and the contents of the item.
The new item is highlighted in any view containing it when it is
added. Views containing the new item may need to be redrawn
on your terminal when the new item is added.
Edit-Add Item
See Also
Use this command to add an item to the active category. You can
also select the equivalent button from the tool bar or press ctrl+a
(with an active view of the category) to invoke this command. If
there is no active view or item detail, selecting the button or
pressing ctrl+a invokes the Edit-Add ? Item command.
If there is an active view or item detail, Commence assumes you
want to add an item to the active category and displays a detail
form for that category.
If no views are currently open, the Add Item dialog box is
displayed. After selecting the appropriate category and detail form
from the dialog box, the detail form you selected is displayed.
Once you have completed filling in the fields on the detail form,
select the Save button to add the item; the Cancel button on the
detail form cancels the Add Item command.
If time conflict checking is enabled for the active category (see
Customize-Database-Category-Time Conflict), Commence checks
to see if the item being added conflicts with the date and time of
another item already in the category. If so, you are asked for
confirmation before the item is added. Time conflict checking also
makes sure that you specify a start time that is later than the end
time of the item.
If a detail form allows you to add recurring items to a category
(such as the Initial Form for the Appointment category) and
conflict checking is enabled for the category, Commence checks
to see if any of the recurring items being added conflict with the
dates and times of any other items already in the category. If so,
a list of all conflicting item names is displayed and you are given
the option to ignore these conflicts or cancel the addition of all of
the items.
Note that once the new item is added, it is not necessarily
displayed by the active view (if any); this depends upon the filter
used by the view and the contents of the item.
The new item is highlighted in any view containing it when it is
added. Views containing the new item may need to be redrawn
on your terminal when the new item is added.
Edit-Edit Item
Use this command to select an item from the active view for
editing. You can edit any of the fields in the item detail displayed.
This command is available only if there is an active view.
 
Shortcut: You can also highlight an item and press enter, or
double-click the mouse on the item name, to edit its item detail.
 
Highlight the item you want to edit from the active view and select
the Edit-Edit Item command. Commence displays an item detail
of the item.
 
A detail form for the active category is displayed, filled in with the
field values for the selected item. Detail forms are specific to each
category.
 
Once you have completed editing the item's fields, select the
Save button to save your changes; the Cancel button cancels the
Edit-Edit Item command.
Edit-Copy Item
See Also
Use this command to add a new item to the active category by
copying the currently highlighted item or the displayed item detail.
This command is available only if an active view or item detail is
displayed.
 
This command is useful if you want to add an item to a category
that is similar to an existing item, without retyping all the identical
information.
 
You can only copy items within the category used by the active
view.
 
Highlight the item you want to copy from the active view, or
display the item's item detail, and select the Edit-Copy Item
command.
 
You can also copy an item from one category to another by
dragging the item and dropping it on another view. The field
values having identical names in the source and destination fields
are copied. If an appropriate connection exists between the two
categories, the destination item is connected to the source item. If
possible, Commence changes the values in the destination item
to match the values in the destination view's filter (if any). For
more information, see Drag and Drop Operations.
Note: If you drag an item from a Calendar view; to copy it you
must hold down the Ctrl key while dragging the item. The default
drag and drop operation for a Calendar view is move.
 
A detail form for the active category is displayed, filled in with the
field values for the selected item, except for the item name which
is left blank. Detail forms are specific to each category.
 
Once you have completed editing the fields on the layout
(including supplying an item name for the new item), select the
Save button to save your changes; the Cancel button cancels the
Edit-Copy Item command.
 
The new item is highlighted in any view containing it when it is
added. Views containing the new item may need to be redrawn
on your screen when the new item is added.
Edit-Delete Item
See Also
Use this command (or press ctrl+d) to delete the highlighted item
from the active category. This command is available only if there
is an active view or item detail. You can also use the Delete key
or the delete item button on the tool bar to invoke this command.
For more information see Drag and Drop Operations.
 
Highlight the item from the active category that you want to
delete, or display the item's item detail. Then, select the Edit-
Delete Item command from the menu bar to delete the item. You
can also drag an item in a view to the delete button on the
command bar and the item will be deleted.
 
Commence, depending on your preference settings, may ask for
confirmation before deleting the item. If it does, select the Yes
button to confirm the deletion; select the No button to cancel the
deletion. (See Edit-Preferences-Other).
 
If the item you are deleting is a recurring item, Commence will
prompt you for the range of items to delete.
 
The item immediately after the deleted item is highlighted in the
view. Views from which the item was deleted may need to be
redrawn on your screen.
Edit-Delete All Items
See Also
Use this command to delete all the items displayed by the active
view from the active category. This command is available only if
there is an active view and is not available for calendar views.
 
Select the Edit-Delete All Items command from the menu bar to
delete all items in the view from the active category.
 
If the active view currently displays all the items from the active
category, then the category is emptied. If the view is filtered, only
the items displayed by the active view are deleted.
 
Commence will ask for confirmation before deleting the items.
Type “yes” and then select the OK button to confirm the deletion;
select the Cancel button to cancel the deletion.
 
After the items are deleted, all open views of the active category
are updated and redrawn.
Edit-Share All Items
Use this command to promote all the items displayed by the
active view from the active category. This command is available
only if there is an active view for a shared category. It is not
available for calendar views.
Select the Edit-Share All Items command from the menu bar to
promote all items in the view to the shared database.
A local item is one that is stored only on your client database, you
are the only person who can add, edit, or delete it. Shared items
are used by all authorized Workgroup users.
If the active view currently displays all the items from the active
category, then all the items in the category are promoted. If the
view is filtered, only the items displayed by the active view are
promoted. If an item is already shared, its status does not
change.
You can tell the difference between a local and a shared item by
opening the item detail and looking at the Shared check box. If it
is turned on and grayed out, the item is shared. The Shared
check box does not appear if you are in a local category.

Use this command to define preferences for views
using the calendar view type.
These preferences control the starting day of the week for the
month displays in the day, month and year modes. It also affects
printing for the Day, Week, and Month calendar styles (see the
Print Calendar dialog box for an explanation of calendar printing
styles).
 
Selecting Preferences-Calendar
displays the Calendar Preferences
dialog box.
 
 
 
 
Edit-Preferences-Database
See Also
Use the Preferences-Database command to set a password for
your database and to set preferences for shared databases.
 
This tab displays this Database Preferences dialog box.
Edit-Preferences-Dialer
See Also
If you want to use Commence's automatic dialing feature to dial
telephone numbers from views and item details, you must select
your dialing device.
 
In Commence there is, by default, only one choice for dialing the
telephone from your PC:
-- Dial Using: Windows Telephony
Selecting the Control Panel button will switch you to the
Windows Control Panel to change the Windows Telephony
settings for your dialing device. Windows Telephony allows you to
autodial from Commence using a calling card. You can also
change the access codes used to dial your numbers based on
your location. If your phone/modem manufacturer also provides
you with a Telephony Service Provider (*.TSP), you may also be
able to use Caller ID on incoming calls.
 
Note: You will have to close Commence and restart it for Control
Panel changes to take effect.
