Other:
Selections in a combo box 4000
Databases 1000
Tabs per detail form 25
Data Manipulation Functions
See Also
 
Commence supports functions that permit data manipulation.
These functions can be used for numeric calculations and
date/time manipulations. These functions can be used in
calculation fields and in agents. The following table lists the
available data manipulation functions (also knows as @
functions).
 
Function Returns value:
@ABS(x) absolute value of x
@INT(x) integer value of x (truncates the decimal places)
@AVG(x,y, . . . ,z) average of values
@MAX(x,y, . . .,z) maximum of the values
@MIN(x,y, . . .,z) minimum of the values
@DATE(date) numeric value of date. Date can be any date or
natural language date such as Today.
@DAY(date) day component of the date (1 . . . 31)
@MONTH(date) month component of the date(1 . . . 12)
@YEAR(date) year component of the date(1600 . . . 2999)
@DOW(date) day of the week for the date (0=Sunday,
1=Monday, etc.)
@QTR(date) quarter of the year in which the date falls (1 . . . 4)
@TIME(time) numeric value of time. Time can be any date or
natural language time such as "Now"
@HOUR(time) hour component of the time (0 . . . 24)
@MINUTE(time) minute component of the time (0 . . . 59)
@AGE(date) age in years. The age calculation is based on
today's date. If the date is in the future,
@AGE returns 0.
@POW(x,y) xy (x raised to the y-th power).
@FV(Payment,Rate,Term) future value of an ordinary annuity of
equal Payments, earning a fixed interest
Rate per term, compounded over several
Terms. It is assumed that the first Payment is
at the end of the first period. The formula
used is:
Payment ((1 + Rate)Term - 1)
Rate
@PV(Payment,Rate,Term) present value of an ordinary annuity
of equal Payments, earning a fixed interest
Rate per term, over several Terms. It is
assumed that the first Payment is at the end
of the first term. The formula used is:
Payment (1 - (1 + Rate)Term)
Rate
@PMT(Principal,Rate,Term) the periodic payment for a Principal
based upon a fixed interest Rate per
compounding period over a given Term. The
formula used is:
(Present Value)(Rate)
(1 - (1 + Rate)Term)
@RATE(FutureValue,PresentValue,Term)
the fixed interest rate per compounding period needed for
PresentValue to grow to FutureValue over
Term. The formula used is:
(FutureValue/PresentValue)1/Term - 1
@TERM(Payment,Rate,FutureValue)
the number of compounding periods necessary for a series of
equal Payments, earning a fixed interest
Rate, to grow to FutureValue.
@LN(x) the natural logarithm of x.
@LOG(x) the base 10 logarithm of x.
@ROUND(x, y) Rounds a number to a specified number of
digits.
 
 
In the above list, x, y, and z must be valid numeric arguments,
and date and time must be valid date and time strings, such as
"January 1," "Today," "Now," and "Noon." The values for
Payment, Principle, PresentValue, FutureValue are all number or
currency values. Rate is a percentage, without the %, i.e. 9.00
for 9.00%. The value for term is a number value, for example, 12
for 12 months or 1 for 1 year. If you use these functions with
mismatched arguments, for example @DAY([Start Time]), the
results will be unpredictable.
 
It is suggested that arguments be enclosed in double quotation
marks ( " " ) since the field substitution may introduce commas or
quotation marks which will alter the result of the function. For
example, if using the function @MAX([Number], 500) and
Number is "1,200", the result would be "500" since Commence
would interpret it as @MAX(1,200,500), which is three numbers.
If the function is changed to be @MAX("[Number]",500), the
correct result "1,200" would be returned.
 
Note: When defining an Add Item or Edit Item agent action,
Commence will automatically add the double quote delimiters.
Book View Type
See Also
The book view type displays items in a window that looks like an
address book. Any category can be displayed in a view using the
book view type.
 
An example is the Address Book view included with Commence; it
is a book view of the Person/Company category.
 
Items are arranged alphabetically in the book. Each page in the
book displays a letter of the alphabet at the top of the page; items
appearing on the page have an item name field that begins with
that letter of the alphabet. Items beginning with numbers or
special characters (including a tab or space) appear under the #
character.
 
The book view provides the following controls:
ALPHABETIC TABS
To see items in the view whose item name fields begin with a
particular letter, use your mouse to select the appropriate tab on
the left or right side of the book. You can also type the letter on
your keyboard to go to the first page of items that begin with the
letter typed. A grayed tab indicates that there are no items in the
view beginning with that letter.
FOLDED PAGE CORNERS
Select a folded corner at the top of the page with your mouse (or
use the left and right arrow keys on your keyboard) to move
through the book one page at a time. Use the up arrow key on
your keyboard to move to the first preceding page that contains
an item. Use the down arrow key to move to the first following
page that contains an item.
 
To display the item detail of an item in a book view, double-click
the mouse on the item or highlight the item and press the ENTER
key.
Calendar View Type
The calendar view type is used to display items that occur on a
particular date and have a time interval on that date. A category
must have at least one time field and one date field to be
displayed by a view using the calendar view type.
 
You can display the item detail of any item displayed by a
calendar view in Day display mode (see below) by selecting the
item name of the item you want to see. From the other display
modes (Two Days Week, Two Weeks, Month, Six Weeks and
Year) you can double-click on a date to display the Calendar
Items dialog and then select any item to display its item detail.
 
The topics listed below explain the various parts and display
modes of the calendar view. Select a topic or use the Browse >>
button to display each topic in order.
 
Active Date
Display Modes
Calendar Tool Bar
Gantt Chart View Type
See Also
A Gantt chart view provides a way to graphically display the
durations of items (such as tasks) that may span more than one
day. A category must have at least one date field in order to be
used in a Gantt chart view.
 
The Project category included with Commence is an example of a
category that has start date and end date fields and is used in a
Gantt chart view. This is displayed in the Project Manager view.
 
The Gantt chart view type displays item names on the left side of
the view window, while the right side of the window displays color
coded triangles and horizontal bars to represent the duration of
each item according to the time scale displayed at the top.
 
The time scale for a Gantt chart view can be changed using the
Customize-Design View command and selecting the Display tab.
 
Horizontal bars represent the duration of an item with different
start and end dates; triangles are used for items whose start and
end dates are the same date (one-day duration items).
 
A legend that explains the color coding scheme can be displayed
by selecting the Customize-Design View command and
selecting the Display tab. You can change the color coding
scheme used by a calendar view by selecting a color coding
template with the Customize-Design View command and
selecting the Colors tab. You can create new color coding
templates using the Customize-Database command and selecting
the Color Code tab.
 
Display the item detail of an item in a Gantt chart view by double-
clicking the mouse on the item name or its associated duration.
Grid View Type
See Also
A grid view is a type of view that displays data in a row/column
format. A grid can be used in two modes: read-only and update
mode. When used in read-only mode grid views are similar to
reports in many ways. Data can be viewed and printed; items
can be rearranged and queried through sorting and filtering;
letters can be created, and agents can be fired.
 
Special field handles are provided for some field types to
perform functions unique to that field. These functions work in
both update mode and read-only mode. Supported functions
include: -- sending an email from an E-Mail Address field
-- opening a Web page from an Internet Address (URL) field
-- auto-dialing a phone number from a Telephone field
-- launching the associated application from a Data File field
 
Grid Toolbar
The toolbar located at the top of the grid view is similar to that
on a detail form. The Switch Modes button on this toolbar
allows you to toggle between read-only and update mode. In
update mode, the data in each row or column can be updated
directly in the grid without opening a detail form.
 
Most of the buttons found on the Grid Toolbar are only used in
update mode. All other buttons are inactive in update mode.
 
Button
Purpose
image/saveplus_shg.
gif
SAVE AND ADD ANOTHER: Press
this button to save pending changes
to the active item and add new row
 
 
image/savechk_shg.gi
f
SAVE: Press this button to save
pending changes to the active item.
Moving the cursor to a different
item/row also saves all field edits for
the previous item.
 
 
image/cancel_shg.gif
CANCEL: Press this button to undo
changes; revert active item to last
saved state.
 
 
image/delrow_shg.gif
DELETE ITEM (Read-only mode):
Press this button to delete the
selected item/row.
 
 
image/share_shg.gif
SHARE ITEM: This button is only
active for shared categories. Press
this button to share the selected item
with other clients in the workgroup.
Once shared, an item cannot be
made local and the button will
become inactive.
 
 
image/refresh_shg.gif
REFRESH: Press this button to
refresh the active view. Toggling
from update mode to read-only also
refreshes the view automatically
 
 
image/readupd_shg.gi
f
SWITCH MODES: Press this button
to toggle between read-only and
update mode.
 
 
image/updatepd_shg.
gif
PENDING REFRESH ALERT
(Update mode): This indicator
reflects whether there are pending
database changes that need to be
integrated with the grid view.
 
 
Adding and Editing Items
Using a grid view in update mode is equivalent to opening a
detail form. Changes can be made to most fields by simply
typing directly into the cell. When working with connections or
fields with unique properties, special field handles are provided
to give you the same flexibility in modifying the field as in Detail
Forms.
 
As records are changed in the database (due to agents, import,
workgroup, etc.) the grid view holds these changes in the
background allowing you to finish your record updates. The
Pending Refresh Alert indicator reflects whether there are
pending database changes that need to be integrated with the
grid view. Pressing the REFRESH button will make the view
include these changes, and will turn off the indicator. Exiting
UPDATE mode will also make the grid view include these
changes, and will turn off the indicator. When active it indicates
that an update has been received or that a change has been
made to the view requiring a refresh.
 
The grid view layout cannot be modified while in update mode.
As with detail forms the Design View, Sort View, and Filter
View commands on the Customize menu are inactive in this
mode. The same buttons on the Toolbar are also disabled for a
grid view in update mode. Synchronization can occur in the
background but does not update the view immediately. This
prevents unsaved items from being lost in the process. View
refreshes are also suspended. Press the REFRESH button on
the grid toolbar to apply and pending view updates to the active
grid view.
 
Read-Only Mode
Using a grid view in read-only mode is very similar to using a
report view. Prefix Matching is supported for quickly locating an
item by name. The following keyboard shortcuts can also be
used to expedite navigating through large views:
 
Page Up/Down: Set the active row one page above or
below the selected row.
Tab/ Enter: Set the active column to the next field.
Shift+Tab: Set the active column to the previous field.
Arrow Keys: Move the cursor one field in the direction of
the arrow.
 
 
Special Considerations
Grid views share many of the same features as report views
and detail forms with a few exceptions. The following features
are not supported in a grid view:
 
Visual Basic Scripting: A script defined on a detail form has
no effect on items in a grid view, even in update
mode. Any item modifications that affect or are
affected by script processing should be
performed in a detail form. A detail from can be
launched for any item in a grid view by double-
clicking on the header for the row, or by
selecting the Edit -> Edit Item command.
 
Other preferences: The “Treat Enter like Tab key” preference
for detail forms does not apply to grid views,
even in update mode. Much like a spreadsheet,
the “Enter” key is used to tab from one column
to the next in a row. This functionality
supercedes any other common uses of the
Enter key, such as opening a detail form, saving
item changes, or inserting a carriage return into
a large text field.
 
Keyboard hotkeys: Most keyboard hotkeys and shortcuts will
perform different functions or may be
suppressed when a grid view is active so that
grid view functions can take precedence.
Minimizing or closing the grid view restores
normal keyboard operation.
Document View Type
 
The Document view provides a way to integrate Internet and
Intranet based applications and pages directly on your
Commence desktop. It creates a seamless link between your
local data and applications and remote applications and
content.
 
The Document view incorporates the Microsoft Internet Explorer
browser, including all of the extensibility, scripting, and
programming support offered on the web, including but not
limited to HTML, DHTML, VBScript, Javascript, Java objects,
and ActiveX controls. This allows you to display HTML content
directly from the Commence database or from a remote URL.
Multi-View Type
See Also
A Multi-View is a set of related views displayed in one window
frame. The window is split into two panes, either horizontally or
vertically. The top or left pane is the primary window and
displays one view. The primary view can be opened as a detail
form or as any of the standard view types: Book, Calendar,
Gantt, Report, and Grid. The various methods of navigation for
these view types is unchanged in a multi-view. For example,
you can click on the alphabetic tabs in a Book view or use
prefix matching to locate a particular item.
 
The secondary pane is located either below or to the right of the
primary pane. One or more views can be saved in the
secondary window, but only one secondary view is displayed at
a time. Each view is opened by clicking on the View Tab for that
view.
 
View Tabs
The secondary pane in a multi-view window has a set of VCR-
style buttons next to the view tabs. These buttons are used
when there are too many secondary views defined to fit all the
view tabs on the visible portion of the screen. The first button
makes the first view tab visible, the last button makes the last
view tab visible, and the middle buttons allow you to scroll right
or left one tab at a time. Once you have located the view you
wish to open, click on that view tab. Note that the active view is
not changed until after you click on the view tab to open a
different secondary view.
 
Using a detail form in the primary pane
When a detail form is saved as the primary window in a multi-
view, only one item at a time will be displayed. The Next and
Back buttons on the detail form toolbar can be used to navigate
to other items in the view. Closing and reopening a multi-view
resets the display to the first item in the view.
 
Splitter Bar
A splitter bar separates the primary window from the secondary
window in a multi-view. By default the primary window is sized
to fit 45% of the window. You can adjust the proportions any
time after the multi-view has been created by simply dragging
the splitter bar on screen.
Report View Type
See Also
The report view type displays each item in a view in a horizontal
row or vertical column format. Any category can be displayed in a
view using the report view type.
 
The Person/Company, Support Call, and Opportunity categories
are examples of categories with views using the report view type.
 
Items are displayed in a horizontal or vertical format, with one
field per column. If a connection definition included in the report
contains more values than can fit in a column or row, (multiple)
appears as the last value for the field, indicating that there are
more values in the connection definition. Edit the Field Options
for the field using the Customize-Design View command and
selecting the Field tab increase the size of the connection
definition's display area and display more of the values in the
report.
 
To see the item detail of an item in a report, double-click the
mouse on the item or highlight the item and press the ENTER
key.
 
If field labels are displayed, the primary and secondary sort fields
are indicated by a solid underline and dotted underline,
respectively. You can change the either sort key by pointing at a
field label with the mouse and pressing the left (for the primary
sort key) or right (for the secondary sort key) mouse buttons. Use
the Customize-Sort View command to change any of the four
allowed sort fields.
 
Report views also allow you to show different groups and sub-
totals based upon the fields used for the sort order. See
Customize-Sort View for more information on Report View Groups
and Sub-Totals.
 
Report views provide a great deal of flexibility in the way your
data is displayed, though they don't have controls like the other
view types. To change the way a report displays your data, you
use the Customize-Design View command or the
image/design_shg.gif
icon on the tool bar.
Report Viewer View Type
The Report Viewer displays data from your Commence database
in read-only mode. Data can be viewed, printed, and exported to
various file formats. Once the report has been generated,
changes can be made only by redesigning the report. Features
that require you to select an item in the view (such as sending
letters, using drag and drop, or agents) are unavailable in the
report viewer.
 
Toolbar
The toolbar located at the top of the report viewer window
contains commands for:
Exporting the report to other file formats
Refreshing the report with the latest database information
Changing the Zoom level of the view
Navigating between pages of the report
 
Creating a new Report Viewer
To create a formatted report using the report viewer:
1. Select Views -> Open/Manage Views from the menu. Click
Create New. The New View dialog box will appear.
2. In the View As radio group, choose “Report Viewer”.
3. From the category list box, highlight the category that
contains the data for the new view. Click View to open the
new report.
4. A New View will be opened.
5. Select Customize -> Design View to begin formatting the
report.
 
Fields: This tab lets you select which fields will be
displayed in the report viewer
Filter/Sort: This tab lets you set filters and sorts for
the report viewer
Design: This tab takes you to the Commence
Report Designer so you can design your report
layout and configure other report attributes
 
6. On the Design tab, click Launch Designer. You will be
prompted to save the new report viewer.
7. Enter a View Name and click Save.
Note: The report viewer may be saved to the View Bar or
Views Menu along with your other Commence views.
8. After saving the report viewer, the Commence Report
Designer will launch. For more information, see the
“Commence Report Designer” in the User's Guide.
 
Report Designer
The Commence Report Designer is used to customize the report
layout for the Report Viewer.
1. The report viewer window should be the active window.
2. After selecting Customize -> Design View, click the Design
tab.
3. Click the Launch Designer... button to open the Report
Designer window.
4. The fields you selected in the report viewer will be listed in
the Fields toolbox.
Note: Each field in this list has been bound to a text box,
also known as a control, within the Report Designer. As you
customize the properties for each field, you are actually
setting the properties for the control that the field has been
bound to.
5. Use drag and drop to move each field into the report layout.
Fields can be positioned anywhere in the Header, Body, or
Footer of the report. When you drop the field into the report,
it will be displayed as a box and the field label will be
changed to ’txtFieldname’ or ’Field1’ indicating that this field
is now a text or field control.
Tip To add multiple copies of the same field to the report,
you can hold the CTRL key on your keyboard while dragging
and dropping the field from the Fields toolbox to the report.
6. Each field can be resized to accomodate the amount of data
expected for each field. To resize a field, click on the edge of
the control for that field until handles appear on each side. A
handle can be dragged in two directions to increase or
decrease the field width and height as needed.
7. Additional controls, such as labels, lines and images can be
added to the report.
8. As you design the report, you may click Preview at the top of
the Commence Report Designer window to see what the
report looks like at each stage in the design process.
9. Each field you add to the report has properties that can be
set, such as font and color. In the Property Toolbox, select
the field you want to customize from the drop-down list. Or
click directly on the field in the report to access the
properties for that field.
10. You can add page numbering to your report using the field's
PageCount summary type. Page numbers are created using
a running summary field.
11. When you are finished designing the report, click on Save to
Commence to save the report viewer layout.
12. Select File -> Exit to close the Commence Report Designer.
 
More Information
See the Commence User's Guide for more details about
using the Report Viewer commands
creating and designing a Report Viewer view type
View Menu Commands
Use commands on the Views menu to:
 
Open/Manage Views Open a saved view.
Save View Save a new view; save changes to a saved view.
Save View As Save a changed view under another name. (i.e.
copy a view)
Save View As HTML Save a report or calendar view as an HTML
file.
View As Click any view type in this group to copy a view to a new
view type.
Cascade Layer all open windows so that title bar of each is
visible.
 
Resize All Resize all open views to fit on the active desktop while
maintaining the relative porportions and
positions of individual views.
 
Tile Horizontally Size and re-display all open windows horizontally
so that no windows overlap.
 
Tile Vertically Size and re-display all open windows vertically so
that no windows overlap.
 
Close All Close all open windows (including any hidden behind
other windows).
 
Switch Windows 1, 2, 3... A numbered list of open views appears
at the bottom of this menu. The active view
has a check mark next to its name in the list.
Selecting a view from the list makes the
chosen view the active view.
More Windows... Activate an open view not appearing on the
Window menu.
 
Customize Menu Commands
Use the commands on the Customize menu to:
Design View Change the display, fields, colors, or fonts for the
active view.
Filter View Add or edit a filter that selects particular items from the
active category for display by the active view. Also
lets you remove the filter from the view so that it
displays all the items in the category.
Sort View Alter the order in which items are sorted for display by
the active view. This command is available only if the
active view is a Gantt chart or report.
Link Views Setup a dynamic view link between two open views,
enabling you to display a filtered set of items in one
view related to the highlighted item in the other view.
With Calendar views, you can display the items in
another view for the selected date or for the date
range displayed on the Calendar.
Desktops Add, edit, or delete desktops; open existing desktops;
show/hide all desktop tabs.
Database Add, edit, or delete categories, connections, agents,
color code templates, import/export templates, letter
templates, and detail forms.

One of the most powerful features of Commence is its agent
facility. Agents let you instruct Commence to automatically

This command displays the Agent tab.

Natural Language Dates
See Also
You can specify dates in a date type field using plain English
words. This is a convenient alternative to specifying exact dates,
as it allows you to enter commonly used phrases, like "a week
from today", "two months ago", and "a week from next Sunday".
The sections below list the valid formats by keyword.
When you enter a date in one of these formats, Commence
figures out the exact date for you. For example, if today's date is
1/3/94, and you enter "one week from today" in a date field,
Commence inserts "1/10/94" into the date field for you when you
switch to another field or save the item.
Always check Commence's interpretation of any natural language
dates you use before you save the item. Do this by moving the
cursor to another field after you type in the natural language date.
If the format you used is valid, the natural language will change to
a real date.
Commence will, given a vague phrase, make a best guess as to
your meaning. That is, a phrase like "a day after next week"
would evaluate the same as "a week from today"; "next january"
would evaluate to January 1st of the following year. Remember
that the phrases you use should indicate an exact date.
Some phrases, like "january after next week", are too ambiguous
and will generate an invalid date error if you try to save the item
with such a date.
Capitalizations, punctuation, and the words "a", "the", and "day"
(as in "22nd day of august") are optional in natural language
dates, but are allowed. For example, "The twenty-second Day of
August, 1998" could be entered as "twenty-second of august
1998" with the same result.
Note: Another method of entering dates is to double-click on any
date field in an item detail or dialog box. This displays the Select
Date dialog box where you can display monthly calendars to
select a date and then select the OK button to return to the
previous window.
Day Names
You can specify any of the following day names in a date field:
Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday, Friday, Saturday,
Sunday
Mon, Tues, Wed, Thu, Fri, Sat, Sun
Day names specified by themselves in a date field return either
the current day or the next day with the name specified. That is, if
today is Monday and you type "Monday" in a date field,
Commence assumes you mean today; if you type "Tuesday",
Commence assumes you mean tomorrow, etc.
 
The date returned by a day name used in conjunction with other
date formats depends on the format used. In other formats, a day
can be preceded by an ordinal, as in First Tuesday, Second
Thursday, etc.
Special Days
Commence recognizes the following special days and substitutes
the indicated date: Lincoln's Birthday February 12
Veterans Day November 11
Veterans' Day
Columbus Day October 12
Month Names
You can specify any of the following month names in a date field:
January, February, March, April, May, June, July, August,
September, October, November, December
Jan, Feb, Mar, Apr, May, Jun, Jul, Aug, Sep, Oct, Nov, Dec
Month names specified by themselves in a date field return the
first day of the named month in the current year. That is, if this
month is April and you type "April" in a date field, Commence
assumes you mean April 1st of the current year; if you type
"March", Commence assumes you mean March 1st of the current
year, etc.
Day Aliases
The following day aliases:
Yesterday, Yest, Today, Tomorrow, Tom
return the date of the indicated day when specified alone in a
date field. The date returned by a day alias used in conjunction
with other date formats depends on the format used.
Month Ordinal
Days of the month can be specified in any of the following
formats:
June First, June Second, June Third, June Fourth,
June Twenty-Second, etc.
June 1st, June 2nd, June 3rd, June 4th, June 22nd, etc.
1st of June, 2nd of June, 3rd of June, 4th of June,
22nd of June, etc.
First of June, Second of June, Twenty-third of June, etc.
June 1, June 2, June 3, June 4, June 22, etc.
1 June, 2 June, 3 June, 4 June, 22 June, etc.
The current year is assumed unless you add a year at the end
(e.g., June 2, 1995).
Last/This/Next
You can use the words Last, This, or Next in the following ways
to specify a date: Last/This/Next <DayName/MonthName>
Last/This/Next week/month/quarter/year (date returned is the
first day in the appropriate week, month, quarter, or year) <any of
above formats> of Last/This/Next week/month/quarter/year
Last in this format means "previous" and not "final"; i.e., "the last
day of 1989" is an invalid date format.
 
Some examples:
Next Monday
Last week
Third Tuesday of next quarter
Quarter
Specify the first day of a quarter of the year using the following
formats:
1q, 2q, 3q, 4q
1st quarter, 2nd quarter, etc.
First quarter, Second quarter, etc.
The current year is assumed unless you add a year at the end
(e.g., 1q 1995).
End of Period
You can specify the last day of the following formats using the
form:
The end of <MonthName>/<Last/This/Next>/<Quarter> You can
add a year at the end of the above format (e.g., "end of first
quarter, 1989"), except when using <Last/This/Next> (e.g., "the
end of last quarter, 1998" is not valid). You can also use a year by
itself (e.g., "end of 1999").
Beginning/End
You can use the words beginning and end to indicate the end of
a period: The beginning/end of the week/month/quarter/year
The beginning/end of <Last/This/Next>
week/month/quarter/year
month and year can also be literal months (i.e., June, July, etc.)
in the first format.
Before/After
You can use the words before or after in the following ways to
specify a date: A day before/after <any of the above formats> A
week/month/quarter/year before/after
<DayAlias>/<MonthOrdinal>/<Last/This/Next> Complex
Formats
The following complex formats are also allowed. The <format>
string refers to any of the above formats, though some specific
forms may not be allowed if they do not specify an exact date.
 
When using complex formats, be sure to check the interpreted
value of the date field before selecting Save on an item detail; do
this by moving the cursor to another field after you type in the
natural language date. If the format you used is valid, the natural
language will change to a real date.
 
The formats below cannot be combined with one another to
construct even more complex formats.
 
The subsections below present examples of complex format
usage. The lists are not exhaustive since the possible
combinations of formats are almost limitless.
The <Ordinal> Day/Month/Week/Quarter Of/In The
Week/Month/Quarter/Year
Second month of 1993
Third day of next week
Second week in June
Fourth quarter of 1998
<format> before <format>
First thursday before may first
The friday before the beginning of next month
One month before Lincoln's Birthday, 1998
<format> after <format>
Next thursday after 1/5/99
The second friday after the beginning of next quarter
<format> from <format>
Two weeks from today
One month from yesterday
Three weeks from the beginning of next quarter
<number of> days/weeks/months/quarters/years ago
Two weeks ago
A month ago
A year ago
Natural Language Times
See Also
You can specify times in a time type field using numbers (e.g.,
9:00 am, 9 am, 9) and also using plain English words. Using
words is a convenient alternative to specifying exact times, as it
allows you to enter commonly used phrases, like "now" and
"noon". Time type fields accept a limited set of keywords in place
of exact times. The valid keywords and their translations are
listed below.
begin
start
The system clock time at the time the item detail was opened
using the Edit-Add Item command. These are typically used as
start times in log items to record the beginning of a logged event
(such as a phone call).
end
stop
The system clock time at the time the item detail (opened using
the Edit-Add Item command) was closed and saved. These are
typically used as end times in log items to record the end of a
logged event (such as a phone call).
now
The current time.
noon
12PM or 1200 hours (midday).
midnight
mid-night
midnite
12AM or 2400 hours.
 
Short Time Formats
Time fields allow you to be flexible when entering times. A space
can be used as the hour/minute separator and you can use A and
P to substitute for AM and PM
 
The following are some examples of how the short time format
works:
Time value entered: Evaluates to:
1 1:00
123 1:23
1234 12:34
1p 1:00 PM
1 23p 1:23 PM
 
Simple Calculations with Time Fields
Time fields allow simple operations using + and - to add or
subtract some number of minutes.
 
The following are some examples of how the simple calculations
work with time fields:
Time value entered: Evaluates to:
10 PM + 5 10:05 PM
10 AM - 5 9:55 AM
Midnight + 60 1:00 AM
Midnight + 720 12:00 PM
Start + 10 System time (when item was added) + 10 minutes
Open/Manage Database Dialog Box From here you
can:
Open a different database
Add a new database
Edit the Name of an exiting database
Delete a database
Backup a database
Restore a backup of a Commence database.
 
This dialog box displays the names of all the Commence
databases in the Commence Databases list box. The name of
the open, or active, database has the word (active) to its right in
the list box.
 
Up to 1000 Commence databases can appear in the Commence
Databases list box. Views, categories, agents, preferences, etc.
defined in a particular database are accessible only when that
database is open. Only one database can be open at a time.
Detail Form Design Dialog Box
You can customize the detail form by:
 
Changing attributes for the entire form using the Properties
window.
Customizing individual field attributes by highlighting one or
more fields and selecting Properties from the right click
menu.
Customizing the arrangement of the fields on the screen.
See Designing Detail Forms for how to arrange detail form
fields.
Keyboard users can change the automatic field Tabbing
Order which is based on the arrangement of the fields on the
screen. Setting the tab order ignores how the fields are
arranged and follows the order you specify.
Customize the tab pages of a form by adding, editing, and
deleting tabs. See Working with Detail Form Tabs for more
information.
 
 
 
SAVE
Choose the File-Save command. If you make any changes to
the form or field attributes, this button changes to a Save
Arrangement button; select it when you are done editing the
form to save your changes.
 
EXIT
Choose the File-Exit command if you have made no changes or
if you want to leave the form as it is and discard your changes.
This will cancel any unsaved changes you have made.
Designing Detail Forms
See Also
Use the lower part of the Design Detail Form dialog box to tune
the arrangement of your fields so they don't overlap and are in an
order you like .
 
To drag a field to a new position with a mouse, simply left click on
it and, holding the left mouse button down, drag it to the location
you want, and release the left mouse button. To drag a field to a
new position using the keyboard, TAB to the field and select
SPACE BAR to "grab" the field. Use the ARROW KEYS to move
the field a small amount or use CTRL+ARROW KEYS to move
the field by larger increments. When you have the field in the
position you want, select SPACE BAR to release the field.
 
If you try to drag the field past the edge of the window, the screen
will automatically scroll.
 
When you select a field, lightly colored "cross-hairs" will appear to
help orient you in aligning the field with adjacent fields.
Commence automatically determines the tab order for the fields
based on the postion of each field on the detail form. The tab
order starts with the field in the upper left corner, then tabs to the
next field(s) in that row (if any), then tabs to the next row.
Commence’s automatic tab order assumes the tops of the boxes
are lined up for each row of fields on the form. You can also set
your own Tab Order if you prefer.
 
For example, the Field Start options for a text field gives you a
vertical cross-hair at the start of a text entry box, while the Label
Start option gives you a vertical cross-hair at the beginning of the
first letter of the field label. If you want to align by the end of the
text entry box, use the Field End alignment option.
New View Dialog Box
The New View dialog box is displayed by the View-Open/Manage
Views command and clicking on the Create New button. Use it to
create a view. After filling out this dialog box and selecting the
View button, the new view is displayed and becomes the active
view.
 
The elements of this dialog box are:
CATEGORY
From the category list box, highlight the category that you want to
use to create a view.
Shortcut: You can open a new view by first choosing appropriate
radio buttons in the View As and/or Report Style radio groups
and then double-clicking the mouse over the name of the
category you want.
VIEW AS
In the View As radio group, choose the view type that you want to
use to create a view. Choose one of the alternatives by clicking
the mouse on the radio button next to the view type you want.
 
See View Types for a review of the view types. See Choosing a
View Type for how to pick an appropriate view type for a category.
REPORT STYLE
For report views, choose All Fields in the Report Style radio
group to include all fields in the report; select Name Only for a
report that includes the name field alone.
 
Once you create the view, you can use the Customize-Design
View command and select the Fields tab to change the fields
included in the report.
VIEW
Once you have selected all the options you want from the New
View dialog box, select the View button.
 
If you have selected a Multi-View, you will be presented with the
Multi-View Properties wizard which allows you to set the various
properties.
 
If you have selected a Document view, you will be presented with
the Document View Options which allows you to specify the
Document URL.
 
All views require a view design. Commence will attempt to pick
appropriate defaults for the view layout when you select View.
This process may fail if you do not have appropriate fields for a
calendar or Gantt chart view (default values can always be
chosen for book, grid and report views).
 
A calendar one time field and one date field (the due date field;
i.e., the date the item takes place). A Gantt chart view requires
two date fields (one for start date and one for end date of each
item). If appropriate time and/or date fields do not exist in the
selected category, Commence informs you of this fact, and the
view is not displayed.
 
You can change any view's layout using the Customize-Design
View command and selecting the Fields tab. You may want to do
this after you create the new view if multiple time and or date
fields exist in the category and you want to ensure that
Commence picked appropriate view layout fields.
 
 
 
 
 
To see a field that isn't shown in the active report/grid
view:
 
1. 
With the view open and active, select Customize-
Design View and select the Fields tab.
2. 
On the left side of the Fields dialog box are the
fields in the view. On the right side are the fields not
in the view. Highlight the field on the right that you
wish to see in the report.
3. 
To put the field at the end of the report, select the
Append Field button. The field will be placed at the
end of the view.
4. 
To place the field somewhere in the middle of the
view, in the Fields In Report list box, highlight the
field you want the new field to be above and then
select the Insert button. The field will be moved
above the highlighted field.
5. 
To add a combined field (multiple fields on one line)
to a view, highlight the first field you want to include
in the combined field and select the Combine
Fields button.
6. 
Select OK.
7. 
The view will now show the field in the report.
Note: This change is just temporary.
If you close the view without saving it, the change will be
lost. To make the changes permanent, save the view with
View-Save View or select Yes when Commence asks to
save changes to the view.
 
 
 
 
To see a field that isn't shown in the active book view:
1. With the book view open and active, select Customize-
Design View and select the Fields tab.
2. On the left side of the Fields dialog box are the fields in
the report. On the right side are the fields not in the
report. Highlight the field on the right that you wish to
see in the report.
Note: The book view can show up to 20 fields, including
combined fields. The book view can also display the
name field of a connected item. You can define the
number of lines provided for each field and, optionally,
a label to be displayed for each field. See Fields Tab
(Book views) for more details.
3. To put the field at the end of the report, select the
Append Field button. The field will be placed at the
end of the report.
4. To place the field somewhere in the middle of the
report, in the Fields In Report list box, highlight the
field you want the new field to be above and then select
the Insert button. The field will be moved above the
highlighted field.
5. To add a combined field (multiple fields on one line) to
a report, highlight the first field you want to include in
the combined field and select the Combine Fields
button.
6. Select OK to make the changes.
Note: This change is just temporary. If you close the view
without saving it, the change will be lost. To make the
changes permanent, save the view with View-Save
View or select Yes when Commence asks to save
changes to the view.
 
 
 
 
 
To see a field that isn't shown in the active calendar
view:
1. With the calendar view open and active, select
Customize-Design View and select the Fields tab.
2. On the left side of the dialog box, there are drop
down list boxes. In each of the drop-down list boxes
select the field you wish to display. Up to 5 fields to be
displayed on the calendar view.
3. Select OK to make the changes.
Note: This change is just temporary.
If you close the view without saving it, the change will be
lost. To make the changes permanent, save the view with
View-Save View or select Yes when Commence asks to
save changes to the view.
 
 
 
 
The Gantt view is a special view. For the Gantt view, you can only
change the fields used to display the time bars.
Set Report Field Options Dialog Box
Type a font name and font size in the Font Name and Font Size
text boxes, or select from the associated drop-down list boxes.
The valid font names and sizes in the list box reflect the fonts you
have installed in Windows. You can also change the font style to
Bold, Italic, and/or Underline by selecting the appropriate check
boxes. The Font Appearance box shows you what the selected
font and style look like. Select OK once you've made your
selections.
Set the size of the area in which a field's contents will appear in
the report, enter the appropriate number of characters into the
Width text box, and the appropriate number of lines into the
Height text box. This box originally contains the options set when
the field was added to the active category.
Note that the height of a connected field is grayed out and cannot
be changed from this dialog box. You can change this optoin by
selecting the Customize-Design View command, then selecting
the Display tab. You may want to do this in particular if one or
more of your connected fields contains more connected item
names than can fit in the default field height.
Set the amount of indentation for a field by entering a
measurement into the Indentation text box.
You can label individual field values with the name of the field by
turning on the Include Field Label check box. The field label will
be shown as if it were part of the field data, just before the field
value on the report. This is unlike the Show Labels setting,
where the labels are included on the left side of a vertical format
report and across the top of a horizontal format report
Remove blank lines from within large text fields in a report by
turning on the Remove Embedded Blank Lines check box.
Removing blank lines is particularly useful when printing labels.
Blank lines may occur in an item if one or more fields do not
contain a value. Turning this check box on eliminates blank lines
within items while maintaining the space between items. Blank
spaces are not removed, however, from the end of a field; i.e., if
the space allocated for a field is too big for the field, blank lines
will appear at the end of the field, regardless of the setting of this
check box.
Reverse names in text fields that are in the "last name, first
name" format by turning on the Reverse Name check box.
Set the alignment of a field in the report column in which it
appears. A field may be left justified, centered, or right justified.
Choose the appropriate radio button from the Alignment radio
group. For example, to line up the decimal point in a currency
value, the field must be right justified.
To calculate field summaries on selected calculation and number
fields in a report, check one or both choices from the Calculation
checkboxes. The Total checkbox sums up the field value for all
the items in the view. The Average checkbox sums up the field
value for all items in the view, then divides by the total number of
items in the view. To view the field summary, open the report view
then select Tools - Field Summary from the menu.
Filtering on One Field
If you wish to find a subset of items based on the value of a
single field in the active category or the name of a connected
item:
1. Select Customize-Filter View This will display the Filter1 tab.
2. Determine whether to filter on a value in the active category,
or on a connected item.
To filter on a value in the active category:
A. Highlight the field you wish to filter on in the Filter for All
Items box. The Filter by box will display (Field value).
B. Enter the value to filter on and how to filter it. Methods of
filtering a field depend on the field type.
For a calculation, number, or sequence number field type,
the comparison operators are equal to, not equal to, less
than, greater than, and is between. Select the comparison
you wish to make from the drop down list and enter a
numeric comparison value in the text box.
For a check box field, select the appropriate radio button to
filter. You can choose if the value is Checked or Not
Checked.
For a date field, the comparison operators are before, on,
after, is between and is blank. Select the comparison you
wish to make from the drop down list and type a
comparison value (in the appropriate date format selected
via the Windows Control Panel) in the text box. You can
also use natural language date as a value, as described in
Natural Language Dates. For example, to find items with
Today's date, select On and enter "today" in the text box.
For name, telephone, and text fields, the comparison
operators are equals, contains, does not contain, and is
blank. Select the comparison you wish to make from the
drop down list and enter a comparison value. For example,
to filter on all items that have "Ask Bill" in the Notes field,
select Contains and enter "Ask Bill" in the text box. Note:
For Name and Text fields, you can select the Options
button. This allows you to filter for all or one of up to 10
different strings.
C. Select the OK button. The view will now be filtered.
To filter on item connected to a particular item (for example, all
appointments attended by Bill Smith):
A. Highlight the connection you wish to filter on in the Filter for
All Items box, and select the field in the connected category
you want to make the comparison to from the Filter By drop
down list box.
B. Select the item you wish to filter on.
C. Select the OK button. The view will now contain all the items
connected to the item you selected
Filtering on More than One Field
If you wish to find a subset of items based on the values two or
more fields in the active category or the names of a connected
items:
1. Select Customize-Filter View .
2. Select the Summary tab, then choose the next available filter.
3. Determine if you want to filter on a value in the active
category, or on a connected item.
To filter on a value in the active category:
1. Highlight the field you wish to filter on in the Filter for All
Items box. The Filter By box will display (Field value).
2. Enter the value to filter on and how to filter it. Methods of
filtering a field depend on the field type.
For a calculation, number, or sequence number field type, the
comparison operators are equal to, not equal to, less than,
greater than, and is between. Select the comparison you wish
to make from the drop down list and enter a numeric
comparison value in the text box.
For a check box field, select the appropriate radio button to
filter. You can choose if the value is Checked or Not
Checked.
For a date field, the comparison operators are before, on,
after, is between and is blank.. Select the comparison you
want to make from the drop down list and type a comparison
value (in the appropriate date format selected via the Windows
Control Panel) into the text box. You can also use natural
language date as a value, as described in Natural Language
Dates. For example, to find items with Today's date, select the
on button and enter "today" in the text box.
For name, telephone, and text fields, the comparison
operators are equals, contains, does not contain, and is blank.
Select the comparison you want to make from the drop down
list and enter a comparison value. For example, to filter on all
items that have "Ask Bill" in the Notes field, select the
Contains button and enter "Ask Bill" in the text box. Note: For
Name and Text fields, you can select the Options button. This
allows you to filter for all or one of up to 10 different strings.
3. Select the OK button.
4. Follow the process for the other fields you wish to filter.
5. Use the And and Or radio group to form a filter.
6. Use the Not check box to reverse the sense of a filter.
7. When completed, select OK to filter the view.
To filter if an item is connected to a particular item in a
connecting category:
1. Highlight the connection you wish to filter on in the Filter for
All Items box.
2. Select the item you wish to filter on.
3. Select the OK button.
4. Follow the process to achieve the filter you want.
5. When completed, select OK to filter the view.
Filtering on a Date Range
Filtering on a date range involves specifying two values to
be compared to the same field. For example, you may
need to see a list of your To-Dos due during a particular
week. You would need to filter the calendar on the Date
field using the Customize-Filter View command. One value
specifies the items with Dates After the last day of the last
week and the second with Dates Before the first day of
the next week. Combining these two values with the “Is
Between” comparison operator gives you a view of only
To-Dos due during the week.
To set a filter for a date range (for example, September
19th through September 25th):
1. Display the view for the category for which you are
setting a date range.
Make sure the category contains date fields to filter on
and contains data that will pass through the filter.
2. Select Customize-Filter View from the menu or select
the mage/filter_shg.gif button on the tool bar
3. To set the filter, select the Filter1 tab.
4. In the Filter for All Items list box, select the date field
you wish to base the filter on.
5. Select Is Between as the comparison value from the
drop down list of comparison values.
6. Enter the values you desire for the Minimum Value
(beginning of the date range) and Maximum Value
(end of the date range).
7. Select the Save button.
Your view is re-displayed containing only items whose
dates fall in the range you specified in the filter.
Note: This change is just temporary. If you close the view
without saving it, the change will be lost. To make the
changes permanent, save the view with View-Save
View or select Yes when Commence asks to save
changes to the view.
Finding Items with Blank Fields
To locate items that have no value in a field, use the Is Blank
comparison operator. This operator can be used for a number of
different field types, including Text, Time, Date, Calculation, and
Telephone type fields.
1. Display the view you wish to filter. For help, see Opening a
View.
2. Select the Customize-Filter View command from the menu
bar. To set the filter, select the Filter1 tab.
If you are creating more than one filter select the Summary tab.
3. In the Filter for All Items list box, select the date field to filter
on.
4. Select the Is Blank comparison operator from the drop down
list.
5. Select the Save button.
This returns you to the active view and the filter will be in effect.
Some field types are defined with specific allowed values and
therefore can never have blank values. This applies to Check
Box, Selection, Sequence Number, Number, Name, and
Connected item Name fields. Select a field type below to see a
description of filtering for blank values for some of the special
field types listed above:
Number
Connections
 
The remaining field types cannot be filtered; this includes Data
File, Excel cell, and Image fields.
Global Search Dialog Box
This dialog box is displayed when you select the Tools-Global
Search command.
 
To use the dialog box, type the text you want to search for in the
Search Text text box and select the Start Search button The
label on this button then changes to Stop Search.
 
The Global Search dialog box contains these elements:
SEARCH TEXT
Type the text for which you want to search into this text box. You
can use a question mark wildcard to substitute for any single
character in the search text, and an asterisk (*) to substitute for
one or more characters. For example, typing "12/*/06" tells
Commence to look for any number of characters between the
slashes. Typing "12/?/06" tells Commence to look for exactly one
character between the slashes. Typing "12/??/06" tells
Commence to look for exactly two characters between the
slashes.
 
To search the database for multiple values, such as John or Joe
or Sue or Mary, you could create a multi–layered text string (for
example, John.OR.Joe OR.Sue.OR.Mary). By specifying text
strings in combination using the Boolean .AND. and .OR.
operators, Commence will now search the active database for
these text strings and return those items that meet the specified
criteria. Notice that there are periods around the Boolean
Operators. This is to distinguish the Boolean Operators AND and
OR from the text strings AND and OR. Additionally, Boolean
Operators are case sensitive, and must be entered as Upper
Case in order for Commence to identify them as Boolean
Operators.
MATCH
Select the degree of fuzziness to use for search. An "Exact"
match will find text that is just as you have entered it in the
Search Text box. "Fuzzy", "Fuzzier" and "Fuzziest" make ever
greater allowances for possible misspellings. Fuzzy searches are
always case insensitive.
MATCH CASE
Turn on this check box to specify an exact match of the case
used in the search text. For example, with Match Case turned on,
the search text "paul" may select a different set of items than the
search text "Paul". This check box is turned off by default.
FIND WHOLE WORDS ONLY
By default, Commence searches for any item that has a field
containing the search text; other text can also appear in the field.
Marking the Find whole words only checkbox means that you
only want to see items having a field whose entire contents
exactly matches the search text. No other text can appear in the
field.
# ITEMS FOUND
After you select the Start Search button, this list box displays the
name fields of all items matching the text string. The label on the
list box changes to reflect the number of matching items found.
Up to 500 item names can be displayed. If a global search
produces more than 500 matches, only the first 500 are
displayed.
 
Information about the currently highlighted item in this list is
displayed in the Information on Highlighted Item group box
(see below). You can display an item detail of the highlighted item
by double-clicking the left mouse button over the name of the
item, or by selecting the Display Item button.
 
If the name of an item appearing in the list changes (because of
edits performed while the Global Search dialog box is open), the
changed name is reflected in the list.
SEARCH STATUS
Indicates the status of the current, or last, search. If no search
has yet been performed, it is blank.
INFORMATION...
Displays a summary of the item currently highlighted in the #
Items Found list box, including the Category of the item, the
Matching Field in which the search text was found, and the
contents (Text) of that field.
START SEARCH
This button starts a search using the current Search Text. Once a
search is begun, the label on the button changes to Stop Search,
which you can use to stop the current search. If you cancel a
search that is in progress, the button label changes to Resume
Search; using this button will restart the search where you left off.
The button label returns to Start Search once you change the
Search Text, Categories, Case Sensitive, or Contains/Equals
settings.
CATEGORIES
Select this button to alter the list of categories searched. It
displays the Select Search Categories dialog box . By default, all
categories are searched.
DISPLAY ITEM
Highlight an item in the # Items Found list box and select this
button to display an item detail of the item. (Alternatively, you can
double-click the left mouse button over the name of the item.)
CLOSE
Select this button to close the Global Search dialog box. The
current Search Text is lost once you do this.
 
Note: The # Items Found list box displayed by the Global
Search dialog box is not updated if you do a global search and
then edit or add an item or items that will now match the
previously performed global search. You will have to search again
to add the item(s) to the list. Item deletions, however, are
immediately reflected in the # Items Found list box.
 
Shortcut: To display the item detail of an item in the list box,
double-click the left mouse button over the item name.
Page Setup Dialog Box
This dialog box is displayed by the Page Setup command.
 
Select the Add button to create a new paper layout. To edit, copy,
or delete a paper layout, highlight a layout name in the Paper
Layout list box and then select the appropriate button.
 
Shortcut: Double-click the mouse pointer on the name of a layout
in the Paper Layout list box to edit it without selecting the Edit
button.
ADD
Displays the Add Paper Layout dialog box.
EDIT
Displays the Edit Paper Layout dialog box, filled in with all the
details of the currently highlighted layout in the Paper Layouts
list box.
COPY
Displays the Copy Paper Layout dialog box, filled in with all the
details (except the name) of the currently highlighted layout in
the Paper Layouts list box.
DELETE
Deletes the currently highlighted layout in the Paper Layouts list
box.
SET DEFAULT
Selects the currently highlighted layout in the Paper Layouts list
box as the default to be used when printing.
DEFAULT PAPER LAYOUT
Selects Displays the current default paper layout. Whenever a
Print Options dialog box is displayed, the default layout appears
in the Paper Layout drop down list. Use the Set Default button to
change the default paper layout.
OK
Closes the Page Setup dialog box.
Add/Edit/Copy Paper Layout Dialog Box
See Also
This dialog box is displayed when you select the Add, Edit, or
Copy button on the Page Setup dialog box. To use the dialog
box, fill in any missing information, accept or change any default
values, and select the Save button.
 
To include the print title (if one is defined) for the active view on
each printed page of view output, turn on the Include Title check
box in the Header group box. To include today's date on each
printed page of output, turn on the And Date check box.
LAYOUT NAME
Type or edit a name for the paper layout. The name can be up to
35 alphanumeric characters.
ORIENTATION
Select the Portrait radio button to use the longer edge of the
paper as the vertical side, or select the Landscape radio button
to use the shorter edge of the paper as the vertical side. The
default for adding a layout is Portrait.
PAGE
Select a paper size for the layout from the Paper drop-down list
box. You can choose from the many standard paper sizes
included with Commence, or select Custom to specify a non-
standard Width and Height (these text boxes are otherwise
uneditable). The radio buttons at the bottom of the Page group
box specify the measuring system (Inches or Centimeters) used
for the currently selected paper size; this setting affects all the
measurements on the dialog box.
REGIONS
Enter the number of Columns and Rows for the layout.
Commence will pick a Width and/or Height that spreads the
columns out evenly over the page, but you can pick other values.
If the number of Columns or Rows is 1, the column width or row
height defaults to the page width or page height (minus margins),
and cannot be changed. Note that the settings in the Regions
group box change as you change the settings in the Page group
box.
INTERNAL MARGINS
These text boxes specify measurements from the Left, Right,
Top, and Bottom edges of a single Region, defining the printed
area of the region. The measuring system depends on the radio
button selected in the Page group box. Edit the internal margins
by selecting the appropriate box(es) and editing the text. The
default margin settings for all internal margins when adding a
layout is 0 (no internal margins).
HEADER
Check the Include Title check box to add a header containing
the view title (specified via the Customize-Design View-Display
command) to the printout. If Include Title is checked, you can
also check And Date to add today's date to the header. The
default when adding a layout is to include both the title and the
date.
MARGINS
These text boxes specify measurements from the Left, Right,
Top, and Bottom edges of the page, defining the printed area of
the page. The measuring system used depends on the radio
button selected in the Page group box. Edit the layout margins
by selecting the appropriate box(es) and changing the numbers.
The default margin settings for all four margins when adding a
layout is 1 inch or 2.54 centimeters.
SAMPLE
This non-selectable area allows you to preview the paper layout
design as you enter information into the dialog box. A
specification typed into a text box is reflected in the Sample as
soon as you move the insertion point from the text box (e.g.,
when you press the Tab key or move the insertion point with the
mouse).
PRINTER
Select the Printer... button to associate a printer with this paper
layout. A Select Printer dialog box will be displayed, the selected
printer will be used whenever this paper layout is used for
printing. If you select no printer for a paper layout (or if you
select "Commence Printer" from the list) the default Windows
printer will be used. To determine the default Windows printer,
see the Printers section of the Windows Control Panel.
SAVE
Select the Save button to save your selections as a paper layout
under the Layout Name specified. If your specifications are
outside the capacity of the currently selected page size, a
message box is displayed that points out the error(s) and asks
you to try again.
CANCEL
Select this button to cancel the Paper Layout dialog box and
discard the current specifications.
 
If your margin specifications are outside the margins of the
currently selected page size, a message box is displayed that
points out the incorrectly specified margin(s) and asks you to try
again.
Print Dialog Box
The Print dialog box is displayed while Commence is printing a
view or item detail. Select Cancel to cancel the print operation.
Once the print job is completed, this dialog box disappears.
Dialing a Telephone Number
If you have a modem, and Windows Telephony (TAPI)
installed, you can have Commence dial the telephone for
you.
To dial a telephone number:
1. If the number shows in the view, highlight the item.
If the telephone number is not visible, double click on the
item to open it's form.
2. Select the Dial Phone command on the Home tab.
Note: You can dial any number from a detail form by
double clicking on a phone number field or by
highlighting a phone number in a text field and
selecting the Dial button.
3. If there is more than one telephone number entered for
the item you are trying to place a call to, the Select
Telephone Number dialog box appears. Select the
telephone number you would like to call and select the
Dial button.
4. The Commence Dialing dialog box shows the number
being dialed. It also displays a Hang Up button and a
Change Options button. The Hang Up button allows
you to terminate the dialing by disconnecting the
modem. The Change Options button allows you to
change the dialing options via TAPI.
You can pick the phone up at any point after it has dialed.
Logging a Phone Call
Logging a phone call is similar to logging a letter. It tracks
your phone calls, who they were to, their duration (in
minutes), what they where about, date, time, etc. It
automatically connects the log item to the item you are
dialing. The Notes/History category is used when you log a
call.
You can log a call even if Commence did not dial it. If you
are using a modem, read this entire topic. If not, you can
manually log a call.
To log a call not dialed through Commence, select the Dial
button on the tool bar. Skip to step 4 below and continue
reading for a description of the process.
 
To log a phone call dialed using Commence:
1. Select the Edit-Preferences... command. Select the
Event Logs tab. Set your preferences for when you
would like to log phone calls. There are two options, if
Phone Call is answered and if Phone Call is not
answered. You may select both options.
Note: If both checkbox , Phone Call is answered and
Phone Call is not answered, are not marked, all
phone calls must be logged manually.
2. Dial the number as described in Dialing a Telephone
Number.
3. Select the Talk button in the ATSP Call Status dialog
box. A phone log form appears, if the option Phone
Call is answered was chosen.
Select the Hang Up button in the ATSP Call Status dialog
box. A phone log form appears, if the option Phone
Call is not answered was chosen.
4. In the Date field, the date will automatically be
specified as Today. This changes to the current date
when you move to the next field.
5. When you have completed your selections in the form,
select the Save button.
Tool Bar Pick Trigger
Tool Bar Pick triggers provide a way to manually initiate an
agent. It fires when you select the button on the tool bar
associated with the trigger.
Tool Bar Pick triggers are added as new buttons to the agent tool
bar. Use the Button drop-down list box to select the icon to use
as an identifier on the button. Select the position of the button in
the Position drop-down list box. Only the unused positions for
that desktop will appear in the Position drop-down list box.
Commence checks for any active agents that use Tool Bar Pick
triggers, then makes sure the positions they use will not appear in
the list. You can also define a specific desktop on which the agent
will appear and a keyboard hotkey.
You can enter Help Text that will be associated with the button
(when the mouse is over the tool bar button, the help text will
appear on the status bar). This is a useful way to remind yourself
of what the agent does.
A Tool Bar Pick trigger can be associated with a specific
category (e.g., Person/Company) using the Active Category
drop-down list box. If you make this association, then the button
is only enabled when either a view of the category or an item
detail from the category has the focus. If you pick the button when
a different a view or detail from a different category is active, you
will get a message box reminding you that the button is not
enabled for that category.
A Tool Bar Pick trigger that is associated with a specific category
passes data from a single item in that category as the trigger
item. This trigger item is either the selected data item in the active
view, or the item from an active item detail.
If you check All Categories, then you can always select the
menu pick, regardless of the active category, however, you will
not easily be able to pass data to be used in the conditions or the
actions.
Database Password Dialog Box
See Also
The database you are opening with the Open/Manage Database
command is password-protected.
 
Enter the password for the database in the Password text box
and select the OK button. When you enter the correct password,
the database is opened. If you do not know the password select
the Cancel button.
Caution: If you use the Open/Manage Database command to
back up a database and then later restore from the backup file
created, you can access the restored database only if you use
the password that was in effect at the time the backup was
made.
Database Protection
Commence provides several ways of protecting your data from
being accessed by unauthorized users:
 
Database Scrambling
All of the information in a Commence database is scrambled
automatically by Commence. As a result, no one can easily scan
the database files on disk and read your data. This database
scrambling is done behind the scenes and requires no action on
your part.
Database Passwords
You can assign a password to a database to prevent someone
from turning on your PC and opening the database from within
Commence without your knowledge. After you assign a
password to a database, you will need to enter the password
each time you attempt to open the database.
Password-Protecting a Database
Any database can be password protected. To password protect a
database, follow the steps below:
 
1. Open the database you want to password protect using the
Open/Manage Database command.
2. Select the Edit-Preferences-Database command.
3. In the Database Password group box turn on the
Password Protect Database check box.
4. To set (or change) your password, select the Set Password
button. The Change Database Password dialog box
appears.
5. If this is the first time this database has been password
protected, the Old Password field is grayed out. Otherwise,
type your current password in the Old Password field.
6. Type the password you want into the New Password text
box. The characters you type are echoed with asterisks in
the text box.
7. Type the password again into the Verify Password text box.
8. If you correctly entered the Old Password (if any), and
entered the same password into the New and Verify
Password text boxes, Commence returns you to the
Database tab. If you made a mistake, Commence displays a
message box asking you to try again. Select OK and go
back to Step 4.
9. Select Done to close and save the Database tab.
Opening a Password-Protected Database
The open database is now password-protected. The next time
you attempt to open this (or any) password-protected database,
the Database Password dialog box is displayed.
 
Enter the password you chose in Step 6 above and select OK.
When you enter the correct password, the database is opened.
Caution: If you use the Open/Manage Database command to
backup a database and then later restore from the backup file
created, you can access the restored database only if you use
the password that was in effect at the time the backup was
made.
Save/Save As/Edit View Dialog Box
These dialog boxes are displayed when you select the Views-
Save View, or Views-Save View As command. The Edit View
dialog box is displayed when you select View-Open/Manage
Views, then select the Edit button.
 
This dialog box contains these elements:
VIEW NAME
The View Name (if any) of the active view is shown in the View
Name text box; view names are limited to 30 characters.
VIEW ICON LABEL
The View Icon Label text box displays the label that the view's
icon will have if it is displayed on the view bar (see the Display
on View Bar check box below). You can change the contents of
the text box as you see fit. Icon labels are limited to 10
characters. As delivered, default labels are defined only for the
pre-defined views that appear on the view bar.
ICON
The Icon drop-down list box shows the current icon for the view.
The default icon for a view reflects the view type of the view. You
can change the current icon by selecting one from the drop-
down list box.
 
Note: The icons displayed in the Icon drop-down list box include
both internal Commence icons and external icons that you can
supply. Commence searches the active database directory and
the central icon directory (if one is defined) for all files with a .ico
extension and displays the first 500 icons it finds in the list box.
If you specify an icon in a .ico file for a view icon and this file
cannot be found when the icon is needed, Commence uses the
default icon for the type of the view. See the Edit-Preferences-
Tool Bars command for more information on using external
icons.
DISPLAY ON VIEW BAR
The Display on View Bar check box, if turned on, indicates that
the active view is currently marked to appear on the view bar. If
it is turned off, the active view is currently not marked to appear
on the view bar.
 
Use this option for frequently used views as an alternative to
using the View-Open/Manage Views command or placing views
on the Views menu (see below). Up to 40 view icons can appear
on the view bar. To add the active view to the view bar, turn on
the Display on View Bar check box; to remove the active view
from the view bar, turn the check box off.
 
When you click the left mouse button on an icon on the view bar,
the associated view is opened. If that view is already opened,
clicking the left mouse button makes the view active. Clicking
the right mouse button on the view icon opens another instance
of a view. Multiple instances of a view have a colon and a
number appended to their view titles. The number displayed
increases from 1 as new instances are opened.
 
If a view does not have a label, no default label is assigned to it.
If you don't choose a label for a view and you place that view on
the view bar, no label will appear for the view when the view bar
is displayed with labels.
 
Note that you can choose whether or not to display the view bar
itself by selecting the Edit-Preferences-Tool Bars command.
DISPLAY ON VIEW MENU
The Display on Menu Bar check box, if turned on, indicates that
the name of the active view currently appears on the Views
menu. If it is turned off, the active view's name does not
currently appear on the Views menu.
 
Selecting a view name from the Views menu opens the view.
Use this option for frequently used views as an alternative to
using the View-Open/Manage View command. Up to 16 view
names can appear on the Views menu. To add the active view to
the Views menu, turn on the Display on Menu Bar check box;
to remove the active view from the Views menu, turn the check
box off.
OK
Select the OK button to save the view and return to the work
area.
CANCEL
Select the Cancel button to return to the work area without
saving the view.
Message Log Dialog Box
When you select the Help-Message Log command, Commence
displays the Message Log dialog box.
 
This dialog box displays a log of the messages that have been
posted to the status bar. These messages include informational
messages displayed by commands and agents as they execute
that will help you diagnose and correct problems encountered in
Commence. The entries for agent actions correspond to the firing
of an agent's trigger. The date and time is shown, along with the
name of the agent. If the agent operated normally, the word
"Fired" is shown after the name. If an error occurred, the type of
error is noted after the agent name.
 
Use the scroll bar on the right side of the Messages list box to
move through the list.
 
Turn on one or more of the check boxes in the Show Message
Level and Show Message Type group boxes to select the
message level and type of messages you want to see. This does
not affect what is stored in the log, but only what you see in the
Messages list box. Not all messages are written to the Message
Log. The Log only includes messages from agents, Sync Link
In/Out, and Transfer.
 
Select the OK button to return to the work area.
Configuring Your Word Processor for Use with
Commence
See Also
Summary
Commence lets you use your favorite text editor and word
processor for writing letters. These letters can contain
information from a Commence database.
 
You can write a letter that contains information from the currently
highlighted item using the Send Letter command on the Home
menu; or, you can write multiple letters (one for each item in the
active view) using the same Send Letter command.
 
After selecting one of these commands, you then choose a
template for the letter. Letter templates specify common text for
letters as well as the field codes used to pull information from the
item(s) selected (see above). Commence supplies several
sample letter templates; you can also add your own templates to
Commence.
 
Commence launches the text editor or word processor
application with which the template was created. You can make
changes to the letter from within the selected application.
Commence pulls the information specified in the template from
the item or view, as appropriate, and uses this information in the
letter(s). You can then print or fax the letter(s) for delivery to the
recipient(s).
 
Sending a Fax from Commence
If you have WinFax installed on your system, you can fax a letter
from Commence instead of printing it. The recipient's fax number
is retrieved from Commence and sent to the fax software and the
selected letter(s) are faxed automatically.
 
Configuring a Text Editor
Commence comes configured to use Windows Write for
unformatted (text) letters. If you want to use a Windows-based
text editor other than Write, simply specify another one via the
Letters tab under preferences. This is the only configuration step
necessary in order to use a text editor to write letters from
Commence.
 
Configuring a Word Processor
To use a formatted word processor and take advantage of all the
special features that word processors provide, you must
configure both Commence and the word processor.
 
Basically, you need to tell Commence about the word processor
you want to use and install macros for your word processor that
will allow it (through Windows' Dynamic Data Exchange) to pull
information into your letters from Commence.
 
The help topic for the Letters tab tells you how to specify the
word processor you want to use with Commence.
 
When installing Commence you will be asked which word
processor(s) you use. If you select your word processor at this
time, Commence will automatically set your preferences and
install the macros for you. If you skipped this step during
installation, or if you’ve installed a new word processor after
Commence was installed, then you should reinstall Commence.
Send Letter Dialog Box
This dialog box is displayed when you select the Send Letter
