To display a view as the active view, highlight a view from the list
and select the OK button.
 
Shortcut: You can also double-click the left mouse button while
pointing at a view name in the list to activate it without selecting
the OK button.
CANCEL BUTTON
Select this button to return to the desktop without selecting a new
active view.
Customize-Design View-Display Tab
See Also
Use this command to specify a default title and paper layout for
printing a view, so that you don't need to choose one each time
you print the view. The following options can be set for all view
types:
 
PRINT TITLE
Allows you to select a default print title for the active view that will
be used unless you specify another title (or no title) at the time
you select the Print command to print the view.
 
Type (or edit if there already is a default title) a title for the active
view. The title is limited to 80 characters. Select the OK button
when you are satisfied with the title. Note that the view title will be
printed only if printing titles is specified via the Page Setup
command.
 
PAPER LAYOUT
Allows you to select a default paper layout to be used when
printing the view. If none is selected, Commence will use the
default paper layout defined with the Page Setup command.
 
The Book View is limited to the above display options. For all the
other view types, there are additional options on the display tab.
Customize-Design View-Colors Tab
See Also
Change the color code template used in the active view. Initially, a
new view's items are displayed without color coding. To use color
coding, you must first select a color code template.
 
Color coding the time bars for a calendar or Gantt chart view
allows you to add visual appeal and differentiation between
different kinds of items. For example, you may want to use red to
display meetings in a calendar view, green to display trips, and
other colors for other appointment types. Optionally, the text
entered in every field of an item can also be color coded for all
view types.
 
SELECT COLOR FOR
Select the default colors for displaying the view. You can select
colors for the item text, label text (used for field labels and the
title of the view), window background, and the color of the
highlight. Additionally, for book views, you can set the color of the
book cover.
COLOR CODING
Select a color code template from the list. If no templates are
listed, create a new one using the Customize-Database-Color
Code command.
COLOR CODE ITEM TEXT
Use this checkbox to change the color of all text for an item
based on the color code template. For example, incomplete To-
Do’s can be displayed in red text, while completed To-Do’s are
displayed in black text, making it easier to see important items at
a glance. This setting affects all fields in the item.
SHOW LEGEND
Use this checkbox to toggle the display of a legend for a calendar
or Gantt chart view. A legend explains the color coding scheme (if
any) for the view. The selected color code template is displayed in
a Legend window in the active view's window.
Customize-Design View-Fonts Tab
Use this command to select the default font used to display items
for the active view.
 
SELECT FONT FOR
Choose which element of the active view to select the font for.
You can select a font used to display the Item Text for all view
types. For calendar views, you can set the font for:
Item Text (1 column) The text used for items in Day display
mode.
Item Text (2 column) The text used for items in Week, and 2
Days display modes.
Item Text (7 column) The text used for items in Month, and 6
Weeks display modes.
Label Text The title bar text showing the date.
Mini-month Text The numbers and text on the small month
calendars in Day display mode.
 
Type a font name and font size in the Font Name and Font Size
text boxes, or select from the associated drop-down list boxes.
The valid font names and sizes in the list box reflect the fonts you
have installed in Windows. You can also change the font style to
Bold, Italic, and/or Underline by selecting the appropriate check
boxes. The Font Appearance box shows you what the selected
font and style look like.
 
CHANGE ALL
This button will change the font for every field and element in the
view.
 
Shortcut: If you would like to select the same font for every field
(or most of the fields) in the active view, use this button to quickly
change the font for all fields in one step. Then, if desired, use
Customize-Design View-Fields to selectively change the font for
some of the fields.
 
OK
Select OK once you've made your selections.
Filter/Sort Dialog Box
This dialog box is displayed when you select the Customize-Sort
View command or the Customize-Design View command while a
report viewer is the active view.
 
The items in the report viewer can be filtered as with any other
Commence view. Setting a filter will determine which items are
displayed in the finished report. Likewise, setting the sort order
will affect the order in which those items appear in the finished
report.
 
To filter and/or sort the items in the Report Viewer:
1. After selecting Customize -> Design View, click the
Filter/Sort tab.
2. Press the Filter button on this screen if you wish to define a
filter. The standard Filter dialog will appear.
3. Press the Sort button on the Filter/Sort screen to apply a
sort to the items in the Report Viewer.
4. A modified version of the Sort Fields dialog for the Report
Viewer will appear.
5. From here, select what fields are to be used for the sort and
whether the sort on the field is to be in Ascending (A - Z) or
Descending (Z - A) order.
6. When you have finished your changes, you may click OK to
save your changes and close the window.
 
Note: Group Headers and Group Totals are managed from the
Report Designer. These options cannot be set using the Sort
dialog within Commence.
Design Dialog Box(Report Viewer)
This dialog box is displayed when you select the Customize-
Design View command while a report viewer is the active view.
 
Press the Launch Designer button on the Design Tab to begin
customizing the report layout.
 
The Commence Report Designer is used to customize the report
layout for the Report Viewer.
1. 
The report viewer window should be the active window.
2. 
After selecting Customize -> Design View, click the Design
tab.
3. 
Click the Launch Designer... button to open the Report
Designer window.
4. 
The fields you selected in the report viewer will be listed in
the Fields toolbox.
Note: Each field in this list has been bound to a text box,
also known as a control, within the Report Designer. As
you customize the properties for each field, you are
actually setting the properties for the control that the field
has been bound to.
5. 
Use drag and drop to move each field into the report
layout. Fields can be positioned anywhere in the Header,
Body, or Footer of the report. When you drop the field into
the report, it will be displayed as a box and the field label
will be changed to ’txtFieldname’ or ’Field1’ indicating that
this field is now a text or field control.
Tip To add multiple copies of the same field to the report,
you can hold the CTRL key on your keyboard while
dragging and dropping the field from the Fields toolbox to
the report.
6. 
Each field can be resized to accommodate the amount of
data expected for each field. To resize a field, click on the
edge of the control for that field until handles appear on
each side. A handle can be dragged in two directions to
increase or decrease the field width and height as needed.
7. 
Additional controls, such as labels, lines and images can
be added to the report.
Filter Tab
See Also
The Customize-Filter View command displays a Summary tab of
all filters selected for the active view. If a simple filter is already
defined for this view, its definition is displayed as Filter #1. You
can reverse the sense of your filter (i.e., select all items that do
not match the filter) by turning on the Except check box.
 
To select or change one of these filters, select the tab (Filter #)
for the filter you want. As you add more filters, you can use logical
operations to relate the filters to each other using the And and Or
radio buttons.
 
The And radio button lets you chain up to eight filters together.
For example, you can say you want only items that have both
Filter #1 and Filter #2 to be true.
 
The Or radio button lets you filter on mutually exclusive
conditions. For example, you can say you want only the items
that have either Filter #1 or Filter #2 to be true.
 
You can also use a mix of And's and Or's. When you do this, you
should pay attention to the grouping boxes on the Summary tab.
For example, there is one group box around filters #1 and #2 and
another group box around filters #3 and #4. The And/Or is applied
within the group boxes first and then the And/Or is applied
between the group boxes.
Sort Fields Dialog Box
See Also
This dialog box is displayed when you select the Customize-Sort
View command.
 
For each sort field, there is a drop-down list box for the field name
and a radio group for the sort type. The drop-down list boxes
contain a list of all the fields and connections in the view.
 
To choose a field for a sort field, select the down arrow on the
right side of the appropriate list box (Primary Field, Secondary
Field, Third Field, Fourth Field) and select one of the fields
displayed. The default primary sort field is set to the Name field
for that category.
 
To clear a sort, select the down arrow on the right side of the
appropriate list box (Primary Field, Secondary Field, Third Field,
Fourth Field) and select (none) from the top of the list. To clear all
sorts at once, press the Clear Sort button. This resets the
primary sort back to the Name field.
 
To change the sort type, select either the ascending or
descending radio button that corresponds to the sort type you
want for that field. Ascending sorts the field in a forward direction
from the lowest field value to the highest, while descending sorts
backwards. For more details, see the topic titled Sort Order.
 
Shortcut: In report and grid views, the primary sort field is
indicated by a solid underline under the field label of the sort field
(if displayed). The secondary sort field is indicated by a dotted
underline under the field label of the sort field (if that field is
displayed in the view). You can change the primary and
secondary sort fields by following the steps below, instantly
affecting the sort order of the view.
Left click on field label
sets primary sort,
ascending
shift + left click on field
label
sets primary sort,
descending
ctrl + left click on field
label
sets secondary sort,
ascending
shift + ctrl + left click on
field label
sets secondary sort,
descending
 
If you choose a connection field as a sort field in a report or grid
view, the view sorts (alphanumerically) on the name field of the
first connected item, regardless of the actual connected field
selected. If a connection field is chosen as the primary or
secondary sort field, the solid or dotted underline indicator is
placed under the field label for the connection.
 
If you choose a connection definition name as a sort field in a
Gantt chart view, the Gantt chart sorts (alphanumerically) on the
name field of the first item linked on that connection definition.
 
 
See Customize-Design View-Fields Tab for how to include
connection fields in a view.
Customize-Database-Category
See Also
Summary of the commands on the Category tab:
 
Add: Add a new category.
Edit: Edit the definition of the selected category. The category
definition includes:
-- category name
-- field definitions; each field has:
-- field type
-- field name
-- field size
-- allow items with duplicate name fields (toggle)
-- clarify field for duplicate item Names
Copy: Add a new category by copying the definition of the
selected category.
Delete: Delete the selected category.
Time Conflict: Enable/disable event time conflict checking for
the selected category and select fields to be used
for time conflict checking.
Customize-Database-Color Code
See Also
Selecting Customize-Database-Color Code from the menu bar
displays the Color Code tab.
 
Use this command to add, edit, and delete color code templates.
Color code templates specify the colors used to accent entries in
views.
 
In a calendar, the color code is used in the background area
behind the item description. In standard report views and grid
views, the color code is used as the text foreground color. In
general, items can be color coded according to the value of a
check box field, selection field, or a connection in each item.
 
For example, in the default Calendar view, the color code
template is based on the Type of Calendar item. If you turn on the
Show Legend checkbox, a legend is displayed that explains the
color code template in use. The color code template used by the
Calendar view in the Tutorial Database is the Appointment Type
template.
 
Color code templates are associated with a particular category
and can be used by any view in that category.
 
You can define as many color coding templates for a category as
you like, up to the maximum allowed per database.
Customize-Database-Connection
A connection is a bi-directional link between two categories (or a
category and itself). Use the Customize-Database-Connection
command to add, edit, and delete connections between any two
categories, or between a category and itself.
 
Once you establish a connection, you can link items from the
categories participating in the connection together to create
relationships among your data, and reduce the amount of
redundant data in your database.
 
Selecting the Customize-Database-Connection command
displays the Connection dialog box, which lists the names of the
connections from the active category to other categories. The
Add, Edit, and Delete buttons in this dialog box allow you to
manage the connections between the categories in the active
database.
SEE ALSO:
Connections Overview A discussion of the basics of connections
and connecting items together.
Connecting an Item to Another Item Detailed instructions on using
connection controls on item details.
Customize-Database-Detail Form Describes detail forms and how
to change their layouts (content,
appearance, and arrangement of fields).
Customize-Database-Detail Form A detail form is
used to enter data into a category. Detail forms are
also used to view particular items in a category.
Many different detail forms may be defined for each
category, making it possible for very specific forms
to be developed to meet various requirements.
 
The Customize-Database-Detail Form command displays the
Detail Form dialog box. Use this command to customize how an
item detail is displayed using a particular detail form. You can:
select which fields are displayed on a form
change the arrangement of fields
select different text and background colors
select different font styles
add new detail forms
create VBScripts for forms
 
If you want to arrange the fields on an open item detail, however,
we recommend you use the Customize-Design View command
(or the design button 
image/design_shg.gif
on the tool bar) instead of this command. This will avoid having to
save all the open item details before rearranging the fields. For
example, if you are editing or adding an item for the Calendar
category, and you don't like the arrangement of fields on the item
detail form, select Customize-Design View instead of this
command; the Customize-Database-Detail Form command
requires that you close any open item details that use a detail
form before editing the detail form.
Import/Export Dialog Box
Use the Import/Export dialog box to manage your import/export
templates. An import/export template stores the name of the
Commence category to use, how to read the external file, how the
fields in the external file correspond with the fields in your
category, and how Commence should update your category.
 
Commence is delivered with an import/export template for each
category. Use the Edit button as described below to examine the
template definitions.
 
Unless your external file is self-describing (that is, one with a
sample file), you will need to know how the external file is
formatted, including the order of the fields.
 
Note: There are some special measures that you must take when
you import connections from an external file. See Importing
Connections.
ADD BUTTON
When you select the Add button, Commence displays the
Template Definition dialog box.
EDIT BUTTON
Select the Edit command to view or edit the import/export
template highlighted in the Import/Export Templates list box.
Commence displays the Template Definition dialog box filled in
with the details of the template.
 
You cannot change the category name or the file format for a
template.
COPY BUTTON
Select the Copy command to make a copy of the highlighted
template in the Import/Export Templates list box. The category
selection, field mapping, import rules, and export rules (if
applicable) will all be identical to the original template. You must,
however, enter a unique Template Name to identify the newly
copied template.
DELETE BUTTON
To delete an import/export template that you do not expect to use
in the future, highlight the template name in the Import/Export
Templates list box and select the Delete command. Commence
displays a confirmation message before deleting the template.
 
Select Yes to delete the template, No to cancel the deletion.
CLOSE BUTTON
Select CLOSE to return to the work area.
Database Options Dialog Box
This dialog box is displayed when you choose the Customize-
Workgroup command, then press the Next > button once to get to
this screen.
DEFAULT PERMISSIONS...
Use this command to set the default user level and category
permissions for the active shared database. Pressing this button
displays the Authorization Defaults dialog box.
ASSIGN USERS...
Use this command to match up the Login ID’s of Commence
users in your workgroup with items in the Person category (or
similar category). The names of all users should be entered into
the Commence Person category before you can perform this
step. This information will be used for selective permissions to
determine which users should receive which items. Pressing this
button displays the Assign Users to Commence Items dialog box.
SELECT CONNECTIONS...
Use this command to configure which connections (between each
shared category and the Person category) to use for selective
permissions. Pressing this button displays the Select Connections
for Selective Permissions dialog box.
REQUIRE USERS TO LOGIN TO THE SHARED DATABASE
Turn on the Require Users to Login... check box if you wish to
restrict who can access the currently open database. If selected,
Commence will prompt for a login name and password whenever
you open this database.
Initially, you can set the passwords for all your users. Start
ComAdmin, then select the Users icon (or select File-Users from
the menu). Select the user’s name and press Edit to change or
remove the password. Each user can then personalize that
password from Commence, after they have logged in for the first
time, by selecting Edit-Preferences-Database.
BACK
Use the Back button to go back to the previous dialog box.
NEXT
Use the Next button to advance to the next dialog box.
FINISH
Select the Finish button to close the Customize Workgroup
dialog box. This will save whatever changes you may have
made, or cancel if no changes were made.
Permissions Dialog Box
This dialog box is displayed when you choose the Customize-
Workgroup command, then press the Next > button twice to get
to this screen.
SET PERMISSIONS FOR...
You may set permissions for clients to this shared database by
selecting one or more user name(s) from the list box.
SELECT ALL/DE-SELECT ALL
Pressing the Select All button selects all the user names from
the list, allowing you to set permissions globally for all users at
once. To undo your selection, press the same button, which will
now be labeled Deselect All.
EDIT PERMISSIONS...
After selecting the user names, categories, or desktops, press
this button to change the permission from the current values
shown. Pressing this button displays the Edit Authorizations
dialog box.
BACK
Use the Back button to go back to the previous dialog box.
NEXT
Use the Next button to advance to the next dialog box.
FINISH
Select the Finish button to close the Customize Workgroup
dialog box. This will save whatever changes you may have
made, or cancel if no changes were made.
Communications Dialog Box
This dialog box is displayed when you choose the Customize-
Workgroup command, then press the Next > button until you get
to this screen. For more detailed instructions, see:
 Configuring Server and Client Communications
CONFIGURE...
This button in the Server Communications group box is used to
configure the communication parameters for the Server. If any
clients will be syncing via E-Mail, the parameters entered would
include the Server’s E-Mail Login Name and password. If any
clients will be syncing via a shared LAN directory, the only
parameter you need to specify is which directory the server will
use for storing updates for the active shared database. Pressing
this button displays the Workgroup Communications dialog box.
CONFIGURE...
This button in the Client Communications group box is used to
select the preferred method of syncing for each of your clients.
Select one client at a time from the list and press the Configure
button to display the Workgroup Communications dialog box.
BACK
Use the Back button to go back to the previous dialog box.
NEXT
Use the Next button to advance to the next dialog box.
FINISH
Select the Finish button to close the Customize Workgroup
dialog box. This will save whatever changes you may have
made, or cancel if no changes were made.
Register Dialog Box
This dialog box is displayed when you choose the Customize-
Workgroup command, then press the Next > button until you get
to the Register screen. For more detailed step-by-step
instructions see:
How to register Clients
ALL USERS
In the list of all users, select a user who is not yet registered to
the shared database. Pressing the Register button adds the
selected user’s name to the list of registered users who have
access to the shared database. Note: Access rights (Read-Write-
Delete) should be configured using the Permissions screen
before the user is registered.
REGISTER
Use this command to add the selected user’s name to the
list of registered users.
Each user can only be registered once; if the user you
select is already registered, the Register button will be
grayed out.
REGISTERED USERS
Select the name of a registered user from this list, then press
either the Details button or the Remove button. See the
description of each button below for more information.
DETAILS
Use this command to see the user’s authorization key, or to
create an instruction sheet for that user. Pressing this button
displays the Registered User dialog box.
REMOVE
Use this command to remove (i.e. un-register) a user from
the workgroup. A confirmation message will be displayed
before the user is permanently removed. This frees up that
user’s license to be used by another user. This also notifies
the server to perform the necessary cleanup.
BACK
Use the Back button to go back to the previous dialog box.
NEXT
Use the Next button to advance to the next dialog box.
FINISH
Select the Finish button to close the Customize Workgroup
dialog box. This will save whatever changes you may have
made, or cancel if no changes were made.
Enroll Client Dialog Box
This dialog box is displayed when you select the Customize-
Workgroup command at a user’s workstation from a database
that has not yet enrolled to the shared database.
CANCEL
Select Cancel to return to the work area without enrolling in a
shared database.
BACK
The Back button will be grayed out on this screen. Use the
Cancel button to go back to the work area.
NEXT
Select the Next button to continue with the enroll process.
FINISH
The Finish button will be grayed out until all the enroll dialog
boxes are filled in properly.
Spell Checker Dialog Box
This dialog box is displayed by the Tools-Spell Check command
when it finds a misspelled word in a text field on an item detail.
 
As the spell checker finds a spelling it does not recognize, it
displays a portion of the text containing the unrecognized spelling
and highlights it.
 
The Spell Checker dialog box gives you three buttons for
accepting the spelling of the currently highlighted word as correct,
one that allows you to manually edit the word or ask the spell
checker for suggested spellings, and a Done button to leave the
spell checker. Once all misspelled words have been accepted or
replaced, only the Done button remains selectable.
HERE
To accept only the currently highlighted occurrence of a word's
spelling within the field being checked, select the Here button
from the Accept group box. Commence then highlights the next
misspelled word in the field (if any).
EVERYWHERE
To accept all occurrences of a word's spelling within the field
being checked, select the Everywhere button from the Accept
group box. Commence then highlights the next misspelled word in
the field (if any).
PERMANENTLY
To place the current spelling of a word into your personal
dictionary and accept all occurrences of a word's spelling within
the field being checked, select the Permanently button from the
Accept group box. This lets you add words, such as jargon or
acronyms, common to your business or profession, so that the
spell checker won't flag them as misspellings. Commence then
highlights the next misspelled word in the field (if any).
SUGGESTIONS
Select the Suggestions button to manually edit the misspelled
word or get spelling suggestions from the spell checker. Selecting
this button displays the Type a Correction or Select a Suggestion
dialog box.
DONE
Select the Done button when you are finished checking the
spelling of words.
Schedule Meeting Dialog Box
See Also
This dialog box is displayed when you select the Tools-Schedule
Meeting command.
 
The description of this dialog box is based on the Meeting
Preferences set in Commence's pre-defined categories. If (Use
Outlook) has been selected using the Edit-Preferences-Meeting
menu command, the Schedule Meeting command will launch the
Microsoft Outlook Meeting Scheduler. Refer to the Microsoft
Outlook online help for additional information about using the
Microsoft Outlook Meeting Scheduler.
 
The Schedule Meeting dialog box contains these elements:
NAME
Type in a descriptive name for the appointment or calendar to-do.
The name can be up to 35 characters. This field is required.
LOCATED IN
This list box contains the names of items from a location
category. Select one item as the location for the meeting. This
field is optional.
PARTICIPANTS AND NON-PARTICIPANTS
These list boxes contain item names from the Person/Company
category. The boxes are used to build a list of participants for the
meeting as described below. If you select Find Time, the
appointment category of the selected Participants is checked for
availability. See Find Time dialog box. If you select Save, the
saved appointment is connected to these selected Participants.
 
To select Participants, highlight one or more people from the non-
participants list box and select either the Required or Optional
button to move them to the Participants list box. Required
participants have check marks in front of their names in the list
box. If you have a User Item selected, your name will already be
selected as a "Required" participant.
 
To remove participants from the meeting, highlight a name from
the Participants list box and select the Remove button.
 
To highlight multiple individual items in either of the Participants
list boxes, press and hold down the Ctrl key as you click the
mouse button over them. To highlight a range of items in either of
the Participants list boxes, highlight the first item in the range,
move the list box using the scroll bars (if necessary) to display
the item at the other end of the range, then press and hold down
the Shift key as you click the mouse button over the last item in
the range.
Shortcut: To add a single optional participant, double click the
mouse button over the name of a participant in the list box at
right. To remove a single optional or required participant, double
click the mouse button over the name of a participant in the list
box at left.
FIND TIME
Select this button to find an available appointment date and time
for the selected Participants. For more information, see Find
Time dialog box.
DATE
Type in a date for the meeting. If present, it is used as the starting
date for Find Time. This field is optional and defaults to today's
date.
START TIME
Type in a start time for the meeting. This field is optional if you
are using the Find Time feature or saving a Day Title.
END TIME
Type in an end time for the meeting. This field is optional if you
are using the Find Time feature or saving a Day Title. If you save
this information with a Start Time but a blank End Time,
Commence automatically sets your End Time to equal your Start
Time.
COMMENTS
Type in an agenda, notes, or comments for the meeting.
COORDINATED BY
This box lets you select the name of the person responsible for
the meeting. When you select Save, this person will be
connected to your appointment item. This person's appointments
are not checked for availability.
RESOURCES
Select this button to choose resources such as a viewgraph
projector or speaker phone needed for your meeting. See the
Resources dialog box for more information. When you select
Save from the Schedule Meeting dialog box, any resources you
selected are automatically connected to the appointment.
SAVE
Select this button to add your new item and its connections to
your calendar category.
CANCEL
Select this button to close the Schedule Meeting dialog box
without adding an item to your calendar category.
Select File Dialog Box
This dialog box appears when a command or operation requires a
file name. The Select File dialog box lets you search through the
available disks on your system for an appropriate file.
FILE NAME
Type a file name in the File Name text box or select a name from
the File Name list box. This box lists all the files with the
extension selected in the List Files Of Type box. If the file you
want has an extension other than the one selected by
Commence, or you want to search for other files, type an asterisk
(*), a period, and an extension (example: .txt), then choose OK to
see a list of files (from the current directory) with that extension in
the File Name list box. To see files with more than one extension,
type a semicolon (;), a second asterik (*), a period, and another
extension. For example if you wnat to see all the files with the
.XFR and .TFR extensions, type *.XFR;*.TFR.
 
FILES OF TYPE
Select the type of file you want to see in the File Name list box.
Backup
Commence puts an initial file name into the Filename text box
that is the name of the database directory whose contents you
are trying to backup plus the .xfr extension. You can change the
file name to anything you want.
Restore
Commence puts the string *.xfr into the Filename text box so
that, by default, only filenames with the .xfr extension are listed in
the Files list box. If your backup file has a different extension,
replace xfr in the Filename list box so your file will be listed. (See
Open/Manage Database for an explanation of backup and
restore.)
Import/Export
Commence will select a default file extension for the Filename
edit box based on the file format specified in your selected
import/export template. The default file extensions are shown
below. (See File-Import for an explanation of import/export
templates.)
 
ASCII *.txt
dBASE *.dbf
DIF *.dif
 
The DOS Text checkbox allows you to translate between the
Windows standard character set and the DOS character set. If
you are sharing information between Commence and other
Windows applications, leave this box unchecked. If you are
sharing infomation between Commence and other applications
that run in DOS but are not designed specifically for Windows,
you should check this box.
 
For an Export operation, an Archive check box also is displayed.
 
Caution: If you turn on the Archive check box, all items in the
exported category will be deleted from your database. Use this
option only to archive and delete items you no longer need in
your database.
Transfer
Commence puts the default extension for transfer files, the string
*.tfr, into the Filename text box so that, by default, only filenames
with the .tfr extension are listed in the Files list box.
Sync Link
Commence puts the default extension for update files, the string
*.snc, into the Filename text box so that, by default, only
filenames with the .snc extension are listed in the Files list box.
Directories
Select the directory from the Directories list box.
Drives
Select the drive that contains the file you want to select.
 
 
Once the file name you want appears in the File Name text box,
select OK to confirm your choice.
 
Shortcut: You can double-click the mouse on a file name in the
File Name list box, or on a drive in the Drives list box or directory
name in the Directories list box to select them.
 
Choose Cancel to cancel the select file operation.
Sync Out Dialog Box
See Also
This dialog box is displayed by the Tools-Sync Link Out
command.
 
In this dialog box, you can define and maintain a set of two or
more identical databases (called a sync link group). You can also
manage this sync link group by adding, editing, and deleting
remote databases. Once you establish a sync link group you can
compress a Commence database into a single file (called an
update file) to sync link the database changes from one PC to the
other as you move between them. When you choose the
database to synchronize and select OK, the sync out begins.
SYNCHRONIZE WITH DATABASE
Use the Synchronize with Database drop-down list box to
select the name of the remote database for which you are
creating an update file. Database names are added to this list
when you select the Add button. See the heading Adding A
Remote Database below.
SYNC FILENAME
Use the Sync filename text box to enter (or use the Select
button to choose) a filename to hold the updates for the selected
remote database. The update file is the input file for a Tools-Sync
Link In command.
DEFINE REMOTE DATABASE
The buttons in the Define Remote Database group box allow
you to Add, Edit, and Delete remote database names from the
base database of a sync link group only. These buttons are
unavailable on a remote database in a sync link group. See the
headings Adding/Editing/Deleting A Remote Database below.
SYNC TYPE
This radio group is used to specify the amount of information in
the update file. Select Incremental for an "incremental update"
which consists of only the information which has been updated
since the previous update file was created. This is the default
setting. Select Complete for a "complete update" which consists
of all definitions and data in the open database.
OK
Select OK to begin creation of the update file for the selected
database.
CANCEL
Select Cancel to return to the work area.
Creating An Update File
To create a update file, first select the database you want to
synchronize with from the Synchronize with Database drop-
down list box and then select Incremental or Complete from the
Sync Type radio group. The name of the update file will appear
in the Sync Filename text box. If you want to change the
filename, type it in or use the Select button to choose an update
file name. Once you select OK the sync out begins.
Adding A Remote Database
To add a new database to a sync link group, select the Add
button. This displays the Add Remote Database dialog box.
Make sure that the base database is the last database in which
you did a Tools-Sync Link In command.
Caution: If your base database does not have the most
recent updates to the sync link group's data and you
initialize your new remote database, your sync link
group will now have inconsistent copies of the
database. This means that your sync link group will
no longer be synchronized and you may lose data.
Note: After adding a new remote database you will need to create
an initial update file for the database. See Creating the Initial
Update File for a description of this process.
Editing A Remote Database
Selecting the Edit button from the Sync Out dialog box lets you
change the name of the currently selected remote database. This
displays the Edit Remote Database dialog box.
Deleting A Remote Database
Selecting this button displays a message box that asks for
confirmation before deleting the database name currently
displayed. Select Yes to delete the indicated database name.
Select No to cancel.
Caution: You can always re-add a deleted database name, but
you will have to create an initial update file for the
database (see the instructions given above under
Adding a Remote Database). This means that you
must start over with an empty remote database. The
database formerly used as the remote under the
deleted name cannot be used again as a remote
database.
Transfer Rules Dialog Box
This dialog box may be displayed after you select OK on the
Select Transfer File dialog box. It appears automatically when
Commence detects data (i.e., items) in a transfer file. To use the
box, choose appropriate options and select OK to begin the
transfer.
TRANSFER IN ITEMS
This check box is on by default and if it is left on the items
contained in the transfer file will also be transferred. If this check
box is turned off, the other choices in this dialog box are
disabled, and no items are transferred into the database.
NEW ITEMS
This radio group tells Commence how to insert items with new
names into your category. Select the Add radio button to
automatically add each item with a new name into the category.
Select the Ask before adding radio button to have Commence
prompt you for confirmation before adding each new item.
EXISTING ITEMS
This radio group tells Commence how to handle incoming items
that have the same name as one or more existing items in the
category. How you want to handle these items will depend in part
on whether the category allows duplicates. The radio button
choices in this group are described in the Existing Item Rules
section, below.
OK
Select OK to confirm the selected transfer rules and begin
importing items from the transfer file.
CANCEL
Select Cancel to terminate the transfer of items. Any definitions
already transferred from the transfer file remain in the open
database.
Existing Item Rules
The selected rule in the Existing Items radio group determines
what happens when there is a conflict between the name of an
existing item and an item (or items) being transferred into the
open database.
 
In the explanations below, the "name" of an item includes the
contents of the name field and the clarify field for the category (if
one is defined). Items with the identical name values but with
different clarify field values are not considered duplicates.
 
Replace duplicates, all fields
Delete each item in the target category with the same name as
that of an incoming item, and replace it with the data item from
the transfer file.
Replace duplicates, incoming fields only
For duplicate items, replace only the field values being
transferred in. Other field values in the existing item remain
unchanged.
Ask on duplicates, then replace all fields
Operates the same way as Replace duplicates, all fields
(above), except that for each duplicate item found, displays a
message box showing the item name and asks if the item should
be replaced (OK) or skipped (Cancel).
Ask on duplicates, then replace incoming fields only
Operates the same way as Replace duplicates, incoming
fields only (above), except that for each duplicate item found,
displays a message box showing the item name and asks if the
fields in the item should be replaced (OK) or skipped (Cancel).
Add duplicates as new item
If the category allows duplicates, items with the same names as
existing items will simply be added to the category.
Skip duplicates
Ignore an incoming item that has the same name as an existing
item.
Transferring In Objects with Duplicate Names
During the transfer in, whenever Commence detects an object
(category, view, etc.) in the transfer file that has the same name
as an existing object of the same type in the target database,
Commence uses the existing definition rather than the one in the
transfer file.
 
For example, let's say that you have a Project category in your
database that has four fields defined. You transfer in from a file
that has a Project category with the same four fields as the
existing category, plus another field. Commence will skip over
the extra field in the transfer file and add only the items from the
file. As it adds items, any fields in the incoming data that are not
defined in the existing category are not transferred. In our
example, the fifth field from the transfer file's Project category is
not transferred into the target database.
 
If you want to replace the Project category with the category from
the transfer file, then you will have to first change the name of
the existing Project category (using Customize-Database-
Category) and then start the transfer.
 
Note: Transfer cannot be used to update field definitions or to
merge categories with the same name but different field
definitions. If the category in the target database is modified to
match the category being transferred in, only then will all items
and fields in the transfer file successfully merge with the target
database. Use the Sync Link Out command to make changes to
categories and fields in a target database.
Transfer Out Options Dialog Box
See Also
This dialog box is displayed by the Tools-Transfer Out command.
 
To create a transfer file, first select Category or View from the
Transfer by group box. Then select the categories or views you
want to transfer from the multi-select list box on the left, and any
agents you want to transfer from the Transfer Agents multi-
select list box on the right. If you want to transfer items and
related data files (DOS files pointed to by data file fields), turn on
the appropriate check box(es). Select OK and then select a
transfer file name. Once you choose a file name, the transfer out
begins.
TRANSFER BY
Select the Category or View button to display a list of categories
or views in the list box on the left of the dialog box. You may
select multiple categories or views from the list by clicking the
left mouse button over them (see Keyboard Commands for how
to do this using the keyboard).
 
Dependent definitions and data are included in the transfer file
automatically.
TRANSFER AGENTS
Select the agents you want to transfer from this list box. You may
select multiple agents from the list. Dependent definitions and
data are included in the transfer file automatically.
SELECT ALL
These buttons allow you to highlight all of the entries in the list
directly above the button selected.
ALSO TRANSFER
Turn on the Items check box to transfer out all items in the
selected categories or views. If you turn on the Items check box,
you may also turn on the Related Data Files check box to
transfer out all files referenced by data file fields in the
transferred items. See External Data Files group box in the
Edit-Preferences-Other dialog box for more information on where
data files are stored when transferred into a database.
OK
Select this button to confirm your transfer option choices and
select a transfer file name. This displays the Select Transfer File
dialog box
CANCEL
Select this button to cancel the Transfer Out command.
 
Note: If you want to transfer only items, use the Export-By
Category command or the Export-By View command to create a
DOS file and use the Import command to read the file into a
database.
.
Export Dialog Box
This dialog box is displayed by the File-Export-By Category and
File-Export-By View commands.
 
Use the Export dialog box to tell Commence which template to
use when you want to export data. An import/export template
stores the name of the Commence category to be exported, what
data format to use when writing the external file, and which fields
in your category you want to export.
 
The same templates and dialog boxes are used for importing and
exporting; the only major difference between import and export is
the direction of the transfer.
 
Note that there are special considerations concerning importing
connection definition data from an export file. See Importing
Connections.
EXPORT BUTTON
Before selecting the Export button on the Export dialog box,
highlight the name of the import/export template you want to use.
When you select the Export button, Commence displays the
Select Export File dialog box.
 
Commence displays a message box as the export progresses.
You can select Cancel to stop the export operation, in which
case nothing is written to disk.
CANCEL BUTTON
Select CANCEL to discontinue the export process and return to
the work area.
Basic Concepts
If you are learning Commence concepts for the first time, you
should read this section.
 
This Basic Concepts section is divided into the following topics:
Categories
Views and View Types
Connections
Agents
Databases
Drag and Drop Operations
Finding Items in a List with Prefix Matching
Natural Language Dates and Times
Installing the Server Software
The first step to be done when establishing a Commence
Workgroup is to install the Server Software. Any PC connected to
a Local Area Network (LAN) or the Internet can be used as the
Commence Server. Since the Server is the hub for all data
changes within the Workgroup, it is recommended that a
dedicated PC be used for the Commence Server, especially if
there will be many Clients. For a small workgroup, the Server can
run on the same PC as a Commence Client. The Commence
Server must be installed from the PC that will run the Server
software.
Perform the following procedure to install Commence Workgroup.
The same procedure is used whether installing Commence for the
first time or re–installing Commence.
NOTE: If working with a previous installation of Commence, a
new copy of Commence can be installed over the previous
installation, or it can be installed in a new location, resulting in
two different versions of Commence residing on the same
machine.
 
1. Insert the Commence CD into the computer’s CD ROM drive.
2. If autorun is enabled the CD will run automatically.
Otherwise, select the Run command from the Start menu on
the Windows task bar.
3. Type d:\setup.exe then click OK. This example assumes d:\
is the drive letter that refers to the CD ROM drive. Click the
Browse button if you are unsure of which driver letter to use.
4. An introductory screen will appear. You may click Continue
to proceed or wait for the menu to appear.
5. Click Install to select which software to install. Select
Commence RM Workgroup to begin the server installation.
6. The Software License agreement will be displayed. If you
agree to abide by the terms of the license agreement click
Yes to continue. Clicking No will cancel the setup operation.
7. You will be prompted to enter your user information. You
must enter a valid Commence RM license number in the box
labeled Serial number. Click Next after all the information is
entered correctly. Then click Yes to confirm your registration
information.
8. Select Next. Follow the instructions on the screen and enter
the requested information. If additional assistance is required
select the Help button provided on the installation screens.
 
When the installation is complete, you will be prompted to run
the license administration program (license.exe). You should
select Yes to do so. This will allow you to set the user limit
for the number of clients that will be accessing the shared
database. You will also need to activate the software before
it can be used.
License Administration
Once the Commence Server has been installed, you will need to
run the license administration software. Sales will provide you
with a license upgrade key that indicates the number of clients
you can enroll to a shared database in Commence.
To administer licenses:
1. Select the Run command from the Start menu on the
Windows task bar.
2. Click Browse to locate the LICENSE.EXE program. This file
can be found in the \ADMIN subdirectory under the
Commence program directory. This is the same directory as
ComAdmin.
3. The License Administration Program will automatically fill in
your user information (Name, Company Name, Serial No.)
from the Commence installation.
4. In the Authorization Key: field, enter the key you received
from Commence Sales.
5. Select OK.
This will set your user limit in ComAdmin allowing you to add
the correct number of users.
Adding Users to the Commence Workgroup
After adding the client licenses, you will need to add users for
your workgroup.
Note The “admin” Login ID identifies the special user that has
permission to make all changes to the Commence
workgroup in order to set it up, maintain users, and maintain
shared databases. The “admin” Login ID is automatically
added to your Commence workgroup during installation and
does not have a password by default.
To add users:
1. Start ComAdmin from the Windows Program Manager or
Start Menu. Note: The only Login ID that can add users is
the “admin” ID.
2. Select the Users button.
3. For each user you want to add, select the Add button.
4. Enter the settings for the user, including their full name, their
Commence login ID, and a password. Note that a password
is not required and may be left blank.
Note: The login ID is the name that is entered when logging into a
Commence shared database. This is typically the user’s
initials, first name, last name, or a combination of the above.
It is recommended that each user’s login ID be set to the
same ID they use for logging into the network in order to
make it easier to remember.
5. When complete, select Save.
Note You must have a license for each user. Additional licenses
can be purchased from your dealer or Commence
Corporation. If a user needs to have more than one client
database, for example one at work and one on their laptop,
they can use Sync Link. For more information see, Using
Sync Link in your Workgroup in the Server Guide
(svrguide.pdf).
Note Each Commence Server has a limit on the number of clients
that it can service. This limit can be displayed by opening
the About dialog box in ComAdmin with the Help-About
menu command. You can only add as many client licenses
and users as this limit allows and can upgrade the
Commence Server Edition to handle more clients as
necessary. Contact your distributor for information on
increasing the number of clients allowed by the Commence
Server.
Setting Up a Shared Database
The basis for any Commence Workgroup is a Shared Database,
which is defined using ComAdmin. Whether creating a new
database from scratch, or converting an existing stand–alone
database to a Shared Database, the database will need to be
added via ComAdmin.
Perform the following procedure to add a Shared Database:
To add a shared database:
1. Start ComAdmin from the Windows Program Manager or
Start Menu. Note: The only Login ID that can add shared
databases is the “admin” ID. By default, this user has no
Password. You can change the password after you login for
the first time.
2. In ComAdmin, select the Databases button. You will be
presented with a dialog box that shows the defined shared
databases.
3. Select the Add button.
4. Enter a Name and Description for the new shared
database.
5. In the Database Location box, enter the path of the
database that will become the shared database. This can
either be the path of an existing database that you want to
be shared or an empty, new database.
Note: This is the database that will be run by the Server. This
database path can be located on a local drive of the server
PC. Do not use a directory that is used for other Commence
files such as the Workgroup Administration Files or Program
Directory.
6. When finished filling out the Add Shared Database dialog,
select the OK button to save the changes and create the
shared database. For more information on shared
databases, see the Server Guide (svrguide.pdf), or run
ComAdmin and search for ‘Adding a Shared Database’ in
the on-line help.
Starting the Server
See Also
The Commence server must be running in order to:
set user access
configure client communications
register clients
synchronize data
Before any configuring of the database can begin, the Commence
Server must be running and the Shared Database must be open.
Note: The following procedures assume that the Server was
installed using the default path values. If the Server was
installed in a location other than the default location, some
the menu selections indicated will need to be modified.
Starting the Server:
1. From the Start Menu, select All Programs -> Commence
RM Workgroup -> Commence RM Workgroup.
2. If Commence does not open in the shared database, select
Open/Manage Database and open the desired database.
 
Note: The server MUST be running the shared database in order
for any client to enroll or to have any synchronization occur.
After the Server has been successfully started and the Shared
Database opened, the next step is to establish what the Default
Permissions are to be for a newly added User. Proceed to Set
Default Permissions to continue configuring the Shared
Database.
Note You should always log into the server using the “admin” ID.
If you have more than one shared database, each database
must be opened using a separate copy of Commence. For
more information, see Running Multiple Commence Servers
on One PC.
Configuring Permissions
Commence can control who can see and change information in
the shared database. This section describes how you can
configure the default permissions which are assigned to all new
users added to a Commence workgroup. These default
permissions can be overridden for each user in order to fine-tune
what data they can see or change. Permissions in a Commence
shared database are described in more detail the Server Guide
(svrguide.pdf).
Setting Default Permissions
The default permissions for a shared database are User for the
User Level and All for the Category Permissions. If you do not
want to change these defaults, you can skip this step.
To configure the default permissions for the database:
1. 
Verify that the proper Server database is open.
2. 
Select Customize-Workgroup and then select the Next>
button until you reach the Database Options screen.
3. 
Select the Default Permissions button.
4. 
You can now set the default permissions for the database
and the shared categories in the database. All users are
assigned these default permissions unless you
specifically override them on a per-user basis.
5. 
When completed, select Save.
Setting Client and Server Communications
The Commence server and clients communicate with each other
by sending data files back-and-forth to each other. This
communication can be accomplished by using an FTP directory, a
shared LAN directory or an external e-mail program.
Before configuring the communications information first make a
list of all users and how they should communicate with the server.
If all the users will use the shared LAN directory then there is no
need to set e-mail parameters. If e-mail will be used make sure
that the users have been added to the e-mail program and that
you know their user IDs.
Note The Commence server can synchronize using all available
methods (FTP, shared LAN directory and e-mail) but each
client must choose any one method.
 
image/chiclet.gif Setting Server Communications
image/chiclet.gif Setting Client Communications
Registering Clients
Each client must be registered with the Shared Database.
Registering will create a link between the Clients and the Server.
Registering from the Server will generate an Instruction Sheet,
which is to be given to the appropriate user. This sheet includes
information necessary in order to enroll as a user from the Client
Database. Also, initialization information will be sent to each client
using the communication settings as configured above.
This process gives the Commence administrator full control over
which users can enroll to a shared database. Users cannot enroll
to a shared database without the administrator's knowledge,
because this process gives the Commence administrator full
control over which users can enroll to a shared database. Users
cannot enroll to a shared database without the administrator's
knowledge, because the administrator must register the user for
that specific database ahead of time.
 
Perform the following procedure to register a client with the
shared database:
1. From the Customize Workgroup - Communications Dialog
Box, click Next >. The Customize Workgroup - Register
Dialog Box is displayed.
2. From the list of All Users, highlight the user that is to be
registered with the Shared Database.
3. Click Register. The Register User Dialog Box is displayed.
4. Click OK. An informational Dialog Box is displayed, providing
the authorization key for the user just registered. Additionally,
it will ask if an Instruction Sheet should be generated for this
user.
The instruction sheet provides step–by–step instructions for
enrolling in the shared database. Since the instruction sheet is
a text file generated by Commence, it can be printed out, e–
mailed, or faxed to the user.
The instruction sheet is generated from a template file that
should be customized to include information about the site. At
a minimum, it should include the Commence Administrator’s
name and telephone number so that users can contact the
Administrator if they experience any difficulties. The template
file is a text file that can be edited using any text editor (such
as Notepad or WordPad).
There are three template files included with Commence:
.steps1.txt - is the e–mail template file
.steps2.txt - is the shared LAN template file
.steps8.txt - is the Internet template file
The templates are stored in the server's Admin Path (e.g.,
wgfiles7) which is located in the Application Data directory.
Each template file has special markers that Commence
replaces with the appropriate data to create the instruction
sheet. Some examples of the special markers include (-
Directory-) and (-Serial Number-). Do not remove these
markers or the instruction sheets will be missing vital
information!
5. Click Yes. The Save Dialog Box is displayed.
6. Indicate in which directory the instruction sheet is to be
stored, and what the name of the user–specific sheet is to
be.
7. Click Save. An informational dialog box will be displayed
indicating that the instruction sheet has been successfully
saved.
8. Click OK.
9. Repeat steps 2 through 8 for each user to be Registered with
the Shared Database.
Installing the Client Software
After the Server and Shared Database have each been
configured, Client software must be installed for each user. The
user can install the Client Software themselves, but it must be
verified that the software has been properly installed.
Clients can install the software themselves. However, the
Commence Administrator may be called upon for assistance.
WARNING: If running a Client on the same PC as the Server, DO
NOT install Client Software.
Note: The same installation procedure is followed whether
installing the Client software for the first time or re–
installing software for an existing Client.
 
1. Insert the Commence CD into the computer’s CD ROM drive.
2. If autorun is enabled the CD will run automatically.
Otherwise, select the Run command from the Start menu on
the Windows task bar.
3. Type d:\setup.exe then click OK. This example assumes d:\
is the drive letter that refers to the CD ROM drive. Click the
Browse button if you are unsure of which driver letter to use.
4. An introductory screen will appear. You may click Continue
to proceed or wait for the menu to appear.
5. Click Install to select which software to install. Select
Commece RM Workgroup to begin the server installation.
6. The Software License agreement will be displayed. If you
agree to abide by the terms of the license agreement click
Yes to continue. Clicking No will cancel the setup operation.
7. You will be prompted to enter your user information. You
must enter a valid Commece RM license number in the box
labeled Serial:. This number can be found with the license
agreement in the front of the Getting Started Guide. Click
Next after all the information is entered correctly. Then click
Yes to confirm your registration information.
8. Select Next. Follow the instructions on the screen and enter
the requested information. If additional assistance is required
select the Help button provided on the installation screens.
Enrolling Clients
To become a client in a workgroup, you must register the user to
the shared database at the server. This will generate a instruction
sheet with instructions on how to enroll from the client and send
initialization information to the client. After that, the client can
enroll to the shared database. During this process, the client will
receive a copy of the shared database. The client can complete
this step on their own, but you as the Commence Administrator
may be called upon for assistance. Remember that for a user to
enroll or to transmit or receive updates, the server copy of the
database must be open. The following is how you would enroll a
client.
Perform the following procedure to enroll a client to the shared
database:
1. Verify that the Commence Administrator has registered the
user to be enrolled with the server.
2. Launch Commence at the Client PC.
3. Open a standalone database. This is a database that is neither
a Client Database nor a Server Database. If necessary, create
a new database by using the File -> Open/Manage Database
command.
4. Select Customize -> Workgroup. The Customize Workgroup
– Enroll Client Dialog Box is displayed.
5. Select the Next > button.
6. Enter the authorization key in the space provided. This
number is found on the Instruction Sheet generated when the
Client was registered with the Server, and is available for each
user from the Commence Administrator.
7. Select the Next > button. When each user was registered with
the Commence Server, it was established how the Client
Database would synchronize with the server (i.e. via a shared
LAN directory, by e–mail, or the Internet.) The specific
communications parameters must now be defined in order to
enable the synchronization of data between the Server and
Client Databases.
8. Select Configure. The Workgroup Communications Dialog Box
is displayed.
9. Indicate by which method data synchronization will take place
for the Client that is being enrolled.
10. Click OK and proceed as indicated below. Any information
required may be found by referring to the information sheet
provided by the Commence Administrator.
When synchronizing by Shared LAN, indicate the path to
the Shared LAN Directory, as well as the frequency with
which Commence is to check this shared LAN directory for
synchronization requests. When the information is
complete, select OK. Proceed to step 11.
When synchronizing by E–Mail, select the Mail Program
that will be used, as well as all related information that is
indicated based on the mail program selected. When all
information is complete, select OK. See the following figure.
Indicate the Server E–Mail Address, as well as the
frequency with which Commence is to check the e–mail
program for synchronization requests. When the information
is complete, select OK. Proceed to step 11.
When synchronizing by Internet FTP Directory, enter the full
path to the FTP directory, the user name, and password.
When the information is complete, select OK. Proceed to
step 11.
11. When all Workgroup Communications parameters have been
defined, the Customize Workgroup – Enroll Client Dialog Box
is again displayed. Select the Next > button.
12. Next, information about the Local Database needs to be
completed. Complete the following information for the Local
Database:
Local Database Name - The Local Database Name
appears across the top of the Commence desktop, or when
File -> Open/Manage Database is selected from the Menu
Bar.
Local Database Path - This is the directory in which the
Client Copy of the shared database is stored For example,
c:\commence\clientdb. Do not choose the path of an
existing database, or a directory that contains any files
used by Commence or any other program. If the directory
indicated does not exist, Commence will automatically
create it.
13. Select the Next > button. When all information has been
correctly entered, a message indicating this is displayed.
14. Select Finish. Commence opens the new client database and
proceeds to process the initial sync from the Commence
Server. This downloads all information from the Commence
Server to the Client Database. This may take several minutes.
While Commence is busy, the Sync in Progress Message Box
is displayed.
 
CAUTION The procedure just described must be completed by
each client in order to enroll and obtain a personal copy
of the shared database. It is not possible to simply copy
the shared database for use by a Client.
Note Some information in Commence such as Preferences do not
synchronize regularly, but may sync once during the
initial enrollment to facilitate the client setup.
Note If an error occurs during the enroll process, contact the
Commence Administrator. It will be necessary to Re–
Register the Client with the Commence Server.

104 Invalid entry; please try again.
110 You cannot delete the Item Name field.
 
Each category has one mandatory field that identifies each item.
The field's type is name and it is labeled Name, by default.
Commence uses this field to sort all book and calendar views.
Default reports are sorted by the name field and default Gantt
charts use the name field as the third sort field (though the sort
fields for these two view types are user-selectable).
 
Although it cannot be deleted, you can change a name type
field's label from Name to whatever you want, though this may
adversely affect any agents that require a name field labeled
Name for this category.
111 Replacing the text will exceed the field's maximum
size. Text cannot be replaced
112 The default string's length is greater than the
field's size. You must reduce the size of the default
string.
 
You must limit the default string to the number of allowable
characters for the field. For example, even though the size of a
default string is 255 characters maximum.
 
Either reduce the length of the default string or increase the size
of the field. Press F1 while a field type is highlighted on the
Add/Edit Field dialog boxes for an explanation of limitations on
the default value for that field type.
113 The default selection must have a label. Change
the default or add a label.
 
The default button must have a label. Add a label for the chosen

label or re-label any selection, or you can change the default
selection.
 
If you labeled a selectionby accident and saved the field, but did
not save the change to the new or edited category, select Cancel
until you are back in the work area and begin again. See Editing a
Selection Field for additional considerations.

130 The formula entered is too long. It cannot be
saved.
 
The number of fields in the calculation equation (including
duplicates) plus the number of mathematical symbols used
(including parentheses) cannot exceed 20. For example, the
formula ([Miles]*[Rate]) has a length of five (two field names,
one asterisk, and two parentheses).

has this check box turned on.

195 Cannot create a Sync Link Out Agent. No remote

one.
 
A Sync Link Out agent will create an update file for a remote
database. You must first use the File-Sync Link Out command
and select the Add button to create a remote database, then you
can define a Sync Link Out agent.
Using Sync Link Commands
If you are using several PCs and are not on a LAN, you can still
maintain identical databases by using the Sync Link feature.
With Sync Link, you can create a compressed update file from
the designated base PC and read it into the remote PCs. See
Sync Link Concepts for an more complete explanation of the
Sync Link facility.
This section describes procedures for using the sync link group
and for adding, editing, and deleting remote databases. To see
more about a procedure, select a topic from the list below or use
the Browse >> button to see each topic in order:
1. Defining the Sync link Group
2. Creating the Initial Update Files
3. Initializing Remote Databases
4. Using the Sync link Group
For more details, see the Sync Link Out and Sync Link In
commands and their options.

Import agent.
 
An Export agent will create an external file from data in the open
database.. You must first use the File-Export by View or File-

an export template, then you can define an Export agent.

310 The telephone number format is unrecognized and
cannot be dialed.
 
TAPI could not interpret the telephone number, it may contain
invalid characters or be improperly formatted. To help TAPI
recognize the telephone number please re-format the number
using the 'canonical format' which is of the form +cc (ac) number
where cc is the country code, ac is the area code and number is
the local telephone number. For example, +1 (908) 555-1234 is in
canonical format.

MODERN Examples: Pica, Elite, Courier ROMAN Examples:
Times Roman, Century Schoolbook SWISS Examples:
Helvetica, Swiss
Commence searches for an 8 point font first.

646 Insufficient disk space on drive %s to complete

You chose an operation where Commence will need to create

to safely work with the active database.
 
Commence creates temporary files while processing certain
definition modification requests. When the process is complete,
Commence will remove the original files and make the temporary
files permanent. This is the safest method of expanding your
database, but it will temporarily cause a need for additional disk
space. To correct this problem, free up a sufficient amount of disk
space before you continue to use the database.

continue to use the database. Either use the File-Export-By
Category command to archive categories, or delete categories
(using the Customize-Database-Category command) that you no
longer use.

921 Transfer error: <error condition>
The indicated problem was encountered which prevented the
transfer command from completing successfully. Transfer
problems are usually due to insufficient memory or disk space on
the current drive or in the temporary directory indicated by the
TEMP= environment variable. Verify that you have enough
available memory and disk space and try this command again.
In order to check the integrity of a database, do the following:
Turn on trace by appending a command line option of /Trace to
the Commence icon.
1. Perform a File-Transfer Out. Press both 'Select All' buttons,
and check the box to also transfer items. Send this to a file called
"Test".
2. Go to File-Open/Manage Database-Add a new database,
typing in "Test" into the Name and Path boxes.
3. Open this database, then perform a Transfer in of the "Test"
file. (Warning: Do NOT transfer into the original database.) If no
errors are detected during either the transfer Out or In process,
then the database is probably OK.
Otherwise, check the active.log file in the data directory to see
what Commence was attempting to process before the error
occurred.
1016 This database is not empty and may not be used
as a remote database.
 
You have selected the File-Sync Link In command, and specified
an update file (*.SNC) which contains an initial synchronization
information. An initial update file contains special commands that
will change the open database to a remote database when it is
processed, and it requires that the open database be empty.
 
Commence will not perform a Sync Link In because the open
database is not empty. To correct this, select the File-
Open/Manage Database command to open another database or
create a new database with the File-Open/Manage Database
command and then retry the Sync Link In command.

remote database or select the Cancel button to return to the work
area.
1036 SyncLink error: <error>.
 
Accessing admin files failed
Make sure the base database contains all the appropriate
administration files. These filenames will all have .DAT
extensions. The date and size of these files should match
those found in the Workgroup Administration Files directory
(e.g. \wgfiles5) on the Server.
To ensure that none of your changes are lost, you must
perform a complete sync link out from the base database,
then retry the sync link in at the remote database.
Canceled by user
This message is to inform you that the sync link was
canceled at your request.
Insufficient permissions
Contact your Commence Administrator. The permissions you
have been granted do not allow you to perform this
operation. Only the administrator can modify your
permissions to give you the required access.
Importing item data failed
The data you are attempting to import may be corrupt. At the
source database where you produced the sync file, test the
data by selecting File-Transfer Out. Transfer all categories,
agents, and item out to a file and take note of any error
messages. Contact Technical Support if the Transfer fails.
Invalid sync id found in the database
Please contact Jensen-Jones Sales to obtain the latest
version of Commence.
File could not be accessed
If your sync file is located on a shared drive, check to ensure
that the sync file is not being accessed by another user or
another copy of Commence. Make sure you have the correct
read/write permissions to the drive where the sync file is
located.
Disk read failed
The sync link file could not be read because of a disk error. If
you suspect disk failure, run diagnostic utilities. Some
common diagnostic utilities include SCANDISK (DOS 6.0
and later), and CHKDSK. To ensure that none of your
changes are lost, you must perform a complete sync link out
from the base database, then retry the sync link in at the
remote database.
Disk write failed
The sync link file could not be created either because of a
disk error or a lack of disk space. Make sure you have at
least 2-3 times the size of the uncompressed database in
free disk space. If you suspect disk failure, run diagnostic
utilities.
Disk access failed
If you suspect disk failure, run diagnostic utilities.
Out of memory
Select Help-System Information in Commence. Take note of
the amount of total free memory available. If your system
resources are low (below 50%) close down any other
applications and try again. Also try increasing the size of
your swap file using Control Panel-386 Enhanced-Virtual
Memory. Restart Windows and try again.
A maximum was exceeded
Check Appendix A in the Commence User’s Guide for a list of
the maximum capacities allowed for items, fields, views, etc.
The information you are attempting to sync link in would
cause Commence to exceed one of those maximum
capacities.
Incorrect internal state. Please call Tech Support
Contact Technical Support (at the phone number listed in
Help-About). Take note of what you were doing at the time
the error occurred.
Database access failed
Test the data by selecting File-Transfer Out. Transfer all
categories, agents, and item out to a file and take note of any
error messages. Then try transferring in from the .TFR file.
Contact Technical Support if either the Transfer In or Out
fails.
An invalid parameter was found
Contact Technical Support (at the phone number listed in
Help-About) for more information.
A required record was not found
The sync link in could not be completed because a required
item is missing. One or more of your database definitions
(connections, views, detail forms, agents etc.) views, is
filtered by a connection to a particular item in the database.
That connected item either cannot be created or does not
exist.
If you are performing an initial sync link in, return to the base
database and remove the dependency on this item. For
example, if you have filtered a connection, select Customize-
Database-Connection and edit that connection. Press the
Advanced button. Press the Select Filter button, then press
Clear Filter, and Save. Then perform an incremental sync
link out using a new sync link group.
A duplicate record was found
The information you are attempting to sync link in would
cause a duplicate item to be added to a category. If duplicate
items are not allowed in any of your categories, you should
change this setting by selecting Customize-Database-
Category. Edit the category and turn on the Allow Duplicate
Names checkbox.
Internal error. Please call Tech Support

the error occurred.

Contact Technical Support (at the phone number listed in
Help-About). Take note of what you were doing at the time

1037 SyncLink error: disk write to output file or
temporary directory failed.
 
Commence was unable to save the sync link file. This error is
usually due to insufficient memory or disk space on the current
drive or in the temporary directory indicated by the TEMP=
environment variable. To determine where your temporary
directory is located, type SET at the DOS prompt and hit ENTER.
Take note of the path specified after TEMP= or TMP=. Then verify
that you have enough available disk space on that drive and try
this command again. To perform a sync link in, make sure you
have at least 10-15 times the size of the compressed .SNC file in
free space in your temporary directory.
 
To perform a sync link out, make sure you have at least 2-3 times
the size of the uncompressed database in free disk space.
1038 SyncLink error: reading or writing the database
data files failed.
 
An error occurred and Commence could not process the data.
Check the amount of free space on the disk. If you suspect
disk failure, run diagnostic utilities and/or have your disk
drive serviced before you continue. Some common
diagnostic utilities include SCANDISK (DOS 6.0 and later),
and CHKDSK. If the problem persists, contact Commence
Technical Support (select Help-About).

steps on how to establish a new sync link group for the first time,
see the File-Sync Link Out command.
 
Once you have added a new sync link group, then perform an
incremental sync link out with that remote database.
