1000 Couldn't make items shared.
 
You selected the Edit-Share All Items command and Commence
can not create shared items in a category which is not shared.
1001 Shared categories with local fields are not
allowed in this version of Commence.
Commence does not allow you to add local fields to a shared
category.
If you are trying to display a private field, you may add a local
category to the database then add a connection from the shared
category to the local category. Note that a local form will also
need to be created for the shared category; connections to local
categories cannot be displayed on shared forms.
To add a category, use the Customize-Database-Category
command. To add a connection, use the Customize-Database-
Connection command. To copy a detail form, use the Customize-
Database-Detail Form command and click the Copy button.
To add a category, use the Tools-Customize-Database-Category
command. To add a connection, use the Tools-Customize-
Database-Connection command. To copy a detail form, use the
Tools-Customize-Database-Detail Form command and click the
Copy button.
1002 You have marked the field <fieldname> as shared
but the category is not shared. No fields in a non-
shared category may be shared.
 
Commence cannot save the shared field definition because the
category is a local category. Only local fields can be added to
local categories.
1003 This database cannot create remote databases
because it is not a base database.
 
A database which is itself a remote database cannot create other
remote databases for SyncLinking. Only the base database may
add new remote databases.
1004 This stand-alone database cannot be used with
SyncLink; create a remote database in order to
use SyncLink.
 
A SyncLink is not allowed into or out of a stand-alone database.
The only exception to this rule is an initial SyncLink into an new,
empty, stand-alone database.

permissions do not allow you to save this item or definition as
shared. You may be able to save the item or definition as a local
item or definition. Check with the Commence Administrator if you
think your permissions should be changed.

Your permissions do not allow you to save this item or definition
as shared. Check with the Commence Administrator if you think
your permissions should be changed.

allow you to delete this shared <data or
definition>.
 
Your permissions do not allow you to delete this shared item or
definition. Check with the Commence Administrator if you think
your category and/or database permissions should be changed.
1015 The permissions you have been granted do not
allow you to overwrite this shared <view name>.
 
You have chosen to save a view with the Views-Save View As
command, and the name you have selected matches an existing,
shared view's name. You do not have permission to overwrite the
existing shared view. You can save the view with another
name.Otherwise contact the Commence Administrator and
request that your permissions be changed to Author level.

1024 This version of Commence requires a different
version of the Administration library.
 
The version of the Commence program file (ADMINENG.DLL) for
the Commence Administration library is not the proper version. .
If multiple copies of Commence are installed, make sure you
are running the correct shortcut. Check the shortcut
properties to make sure program directory and path to
commence.exe are correct.
Restart windows to get fresh copy of ADMINENG.DLL into
memory.
Select Customize-Workgroup, then press Next to view the
Communication settings. Make sure shared LAN directory is
available and that the path is set correctly.
Check network permissions to the shared LAN directory.
Clients must have FULL (read/write/create/delete/modify)
permissions. Copy a small text file from the client to the
shared LAN directory. Be sure you can edit, save, and delete
the file.
Run ComAdmin and check list of users, databases, etc. to
make sure they appear intact.
Right click on commence.exe and admineng.dll. Make sure
the versions are correct.
Reinstall Commence
1025 An error occurred while using this database with
the Administration library.
 
A system error occurred when trying to access the Administration
Library. This error is caused by:
problems initializing the admineng.dll
problems initializing the communication settings for a
particular database
 
Close Windows and restart Commence with the /PROMPT
command line option. When you are prompted for which
database to open, try opening a personal database first, then
retry opening the workgroup database.
Restart windows to get fresh copy of ADMINENG.DLL into
memory
Select Customize-Workgroup, then press Next to view the
Communication settings. Make sure shared LAN directory is
available and that the path is set correctly.
Check network permissions to the shared LAN directory.
Clients must have FULL (read/write/create/delete/modify)
permissions. Copy a small text file from the client to the
shared LAN directory. Be sure you can edit, save, and delete
the file.
Check the database.id file. Either delete that file at the server
and reconfigure the Communication settings, or try pointing
to new shared LAN directory. In both cases, the database.id
file will be recreated.
Check the *.DAT files in the \wgfiles5 directory. One of these
files may be corrupt, or abnormally large (e.g., over 100Mb),
or unusually small (under 1kb). Run ComAdmin and check
list of users, databases, etc. to make sure they are intact.
Replace any suspicious DAT files with the equivalent file from
any client data directory.
Check the DATA.ini file on Commence server. Take note of
the SharedDbID=? Note that this number will be in decimal
format, not hexadecimal. Compare with databases defined in
ComAdmin, especially if a new shared database was recently
added. Note that the *.DAT filenames will be in hexadecimal.
Don't forget to convert. (e.g. sharedDbID=20 matches up with
auth0014.dat)
Database was not properly administered. Run ComAdmin.
Remove the database from the shared database list, then re-
add it. Re-enroll all users.
 
If the problem persists, reinstall Commence.

1032 The Login ID was not found. It may have been
deleted, or it does not exist.
 
Upon opening the shared database, you entered a Login ID which
was not valid. The ComAdmin program did not find this login ID in
its records. It is possible that the user record never existed, or it
has since been deleted. Once a user is deleted, only the
Administrator can login to the shared database belonging to that
user.
1033 The automatic login failed; please enter login
name and password.
 
You have selected auto-login in the Other Preferences dialog box.
Commence attempted to log in, but the login has failed. Your
Login ID may be invalid or may have been deleted.
 
Restart Windows. Make sure the CAPS LOCK key is not on and
try again. Check with the Commence Administrator if you can not
determine the cause of problem.
1034 The Commence server license is not valid for the
number of users defined in ComAdmin. Please use
the License utility to update Commence with a
valid license that authorizes <number> users.
 
When Commence launches, several checks are performed to
ensure that the number of clients using the software does not
exceed the total user limit. First confirm that you have purchased
a sufficient number of Commence clients (one per user). Then run
License and check the User Limit. Re-enter your key if necessary
to increase the total user limit. If the key does not produce the
desired User Limit, contact Commence Sales to obtain a new key
or to purchase additional client software.
 
This error may also be caused during an upgrade from a prior
version of Commence. If, for example, you owned 10 client
licenses in a previous version but only upgraded 7 client licenses,
then you must remove the additional 3 users before running
Commence. Select the Users icon in ComAdmin to delete the
extra users.
1042 Commence has detected an invalid file type
during syncing; use Transfer Out and In to a new
database to correct this problem.
This database contains information which Commence cannot
recognize. It is possible that a file has been damaged.
If the database is a Commence Server database, then restore
from a backup of the database. If a recent backup is not
available, you can salvage your data by transferring out all
categories and agents, including items. Then add a new, empty
database using the File-Open/Manage Database command. Open
the new database and use File-Transfer In to rebuild your
database. See Getting Started for more information about how to
set up your new database as a shared database.
If you are a client, you should re-enroll. See the section titled
‘How to Enroll into a Commence Workgroup’ in the Commence
User’s Guide for detailed instructions.
1050 A workgroup error has occurred. ADMINENG.DLL
returned error code <error code>.
 
Check your communication settings using the Customize-
Workgroup command.
 
If the problems persist, take note of the exact error message,
including the error code number, and contact Commence
Technical Support. Select Help-About for the Technical Support
phone number.
1051 <Key> is not a valid authorization key. Enter the
key exactly as it appears on your instruction
sheet. There should be no spaces or punctuations.
 
The authorization key you have entered is not correct. Press the
CAPS LOCK key on your keyboard (to toggle CAPS LOCK on or
off) and try entering the key again.
 
If you do not have an authorization key, contact the Commence
Administrator. At the server, make sure that the client is
registered. You can verify the authorization key by selecting the
Customize-Workgroup command at the server and going to the
Register screen. Highlight the registered user and then select the
Details button.
1052 Template file for the client instruction sheet was
not found <s?>. Instruction sheet cannot be
generated.
As part of the registration process Commence can generate an
instruction sheet that walks a user through the client enroll
process. The instruction sheet is saved as a text file you can print
out, e-mail or fax to the user.
This instruction sheet is generated from a template file that you
should customize to include information about your site. At a
minimum, you should add your name and phone number so that
users can contact you if they experience any problems. The
template file is a text file that you can edit using any text editor
(such as Notepad or WordPad).
There are two template files, one for e-mail and one for a shared
LAN directory:
x:\commence\wgfiles5\steps1.txt is the e-mail template
file, and x:\commence\wgfiles5\steps2.txt is the shared
LAN template file, (where x:\commence is the Commence
program directory).
1053 Server communication parameters not found.
Please configure the server using the Customize-
Workgroup command.
 
At the Commence Server PC, select the Customize-Workgroup
command. Select the Next > button until you get to the
Communications screen. To modify the communication settings
for the Commence Server, press the Configure button on the top
half of the screen.
1054 Commence cannot run while a copy of the
Commence Administration program is active. Shut
down ComAdmin before starting Commence.
 
You must close ComAdmin to save any changes to the
Workgroup Administration files. Commence cannot access that
information while ComAdmin is running. To shut down
ComAdmin, switch over to that application and select File-Exit
from the menu.
1055 The workgroup admin files are currently locked
either by ComAdmin or another copy of
Commence. Try again later.
 
The same Workgroup Administration files (*.DAT files in the
\wgfiles5 directory) are shared by Commence and ComAdmin.
Commence cannot access that information while ComAdmin is
running, and vice versa. Make sure all Server databases in
Commence are closed before attempting to change settings in
ComAdmin. Likewise, make sure ComAdmin is closed before
attempting to change workgroup settings in Commence. Select
File-Exit from the menu to close either application.

administrator to:

and,
2) arrange for the server to re-send the registration
information.

Workgroup command. Use the instruction sheet provided by your
Commence Administrator to make sure any paths and other

To re-send the registration information for this client, the
Commence Administrator must select the Customize-Workgroup
command from the Commence Server PC. Select the Next >
button until you get to the Register screen. Press the Details
button, then press the Re-Register button. This will resend the
registration information to the user.

1059 Shared LAN directory is unavailable and
synchronization
is disabled.
Verify the user's permissions to the shared directory. This

server.
 
 
 
 
 
 
ACTION
An action specifies the work to be done by an agent.
In most cases, actions use values or operate on data passed from
the trigger
and/or conditions
defined for the agent. An action (and a trigger) are required to
define an agent.
 
An agent can have up to four specified actions, chosen from
among the following: modify a displayed icon; display a message
box with a specified message; create and display a new view;
open a saved view; add an item; edit an item; print an item or view;
activate an agent; launch an external Windows application; send a
DDE (Dynamic Data Exchange) message to another Windows
application.
 
 
 
 
ACTIVATE
To activate an agent, select the Customize-Database-Agent
command, highlight the agent that you wish to activate then select
the Edit button. You can then select the Active check box.
ACTIVE
An agent is active when the Active check box is selected for that
agent on the Customize-Database Agent dialog box; a check mark
also appears next to the agent name in the Agents list box. An
active agent is triggered by the occurrence of the trigger condition
specified in the rule.
ACTIVE CATEGORY
The active category is the category used by the active view.
ACTIVE VIEW
The active view is the currently displayed view; if more than one
view is displayed, the active view is the view whose title bar is a
different color or shade than all the other windows. On the Window
menu, the active view is indicated by a check mark next to its
name.
ACTIVE WINDOW
The active window is the selected window with which you are
currently working. The active window appears in front (or on top) of
all the other windows on your desktop or application work area; its
title bar is a different color from the other windows. All keystrokes
and commands that you type affect the active window.
AGENT
Agents allow you to specify criteria under which Commence will
perform various tasks for you. An agent consists of a trigger, one or
two optional conditions, and one to four actions. Agents are added,
copied, edited, deleted, activated, and deactivated by selecting the
Customize-Database Agent command from the menu bar.
 
 
 
 
AGENT TOOL BAR
The agent tool bar displays icons for agents with a tool bar pick
trigger, providing the ability to quickly execute these agents by
selecting an icon with the mouse. You must have a mouse to use
the agent tool bar.
You can choose whether or not to display the agent tool bar using
the Edit-Preferences-Tool Bars command. You can also
 
 
 
 
 
ARRANGE
To position fields when you customize an item detail form.
 
 
 
 
ASSOCIATED CATEGORY
When printing a calendar view, items from the associated category
(chosen with the Edit-Preferences-Calendar
command) that occur on the date(s) being printed are printed as
well.
In addition to the Name field, you can select up to two checkbox or
radio group fields from the associated category to appear on the
calendar printout.
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
BACKUP
To make a copy of a Commence database for use if the original is
lost or damaged. Use the Open/Manage Database command, then
highlight the database name and select the Backup button.
Commence will create a compressed copy of all the data in the
database into a DOS file with a ".xfr" extension. Use the Restore
button to recreate a usable Commence database.
 
 
 
 
BASE DATABASE
When using the Sync Link facility to synchronize databases, the
active database where you define your sync link group is called the
base database. You populate the remote database with the
information from the base database. For detailed information on
creating a a sync link group, see Sync Link.
BUSY BAR
A vertical bar in a calendar day view that indicated when
appointments are scheduled. This feature is no longer available.
CALCULATION FIELD
Calculation fields lets you define formulas based on data in other
fields. Currency, number, date, and time fields can appear in
formulas using addition, subtraction, multiplication, and division
operations.
 
Calculation fields can contain a formula using up to 256
characters, including the +, -, *, and / operators, parentheses ( ) for
grouping, and the currency symbol defined via the Windows
Control Panel (in the United States and some other countries, this
is the dollar sign $).
 
You create formulas by selecting other fields from the category and
separating them with arithmetic operators. An example of a valid
formula is: ([End Time]—[Start Time])*[Rate]/100
Note that date and time fields are useful in calculations only when
the difference between two dates or times are taken. For example,
[Start Date]—[End Date] makes sense; [Start Time]*60 does not.
 
You can use the Tools Field Summary command to display the
sum or average of the calculation, currency, and number fields
appearing in a report view.
CATEGORY
A category is a storage container for your data.
 
There is a limit to the number of categories that can be saved in a
Commence database. Refer to the Database Capacities topic for
more information.
CHECK BOX
A check box allows you to select an option. When an option is
selected that has a check box, the check box is filled with an 'X'.
Otherwise the box is empty. When check boxes appear in groups,
you can select as many or as few of the boxes as you want (i.e.,
check box options are not mutually-exclusive).
CHECK BOX FIELD
A check box field is used to represent a yes/no or on/off condition
or option. When an X appears in a check box, the condition or
option is turned on or enabled; when empty, the condition is turned
off or disabled.
CLARIFY FIELD
A clarify field is a field used by categories to distinguish between
items with duplicate names. Commence uses the clarify field when
making connections, importing data, transferrin in data, and
creating letters and faxes.
 
To set the clarify field for a category select the Customize-
Database-Category command, highlight the category that you wish
to change, select the Edit button then the Clarify Field button.
Choose the field that will make each item in the category unique.
CLIPBOARD
A temporary storage area provided by Windows for text or
graphics. An application can cut or copy data to the clipboard for
later pasting into the same or another application's workspace.
COLOR TEMPLATE
A color coding scheme to accent entries in views. You can create
multiple color templates to choose from, up to the limit of 600 per
database.
 
A color template can be based on the value of a check box, a radio
group, or a connection to a particular item. To define a color
template select the Customize-Database-Color Code
COMBO BOX
Used to display a list of choices from which to select when entering
data. Select one of the choices as the default display..
CONDITION
A condition is an additional, optional constraint used to further
define the scope or operation of an agent. An agent's trigger plus
any defined conditions must all be satisfied before the execution of
the agent's action(s).
 
Conditions can be based on time intervals (such as "every
Monday") or on the state of some discrete piece of data in
Commence (such as the number of items scheduled for a
particular week).
 
An agent can have one, two, or no conditions; if present,
conditions are evaluated only after the trigger for the agent occurs.
Some conditions can pass data to the agent's actions.
CONNECT
To establish a connection between two pieces of information in
Commence.
 
 
 
 
CONNECTED FIELD
An connected field is a field from a connected category that you
choose to display in a report view, to use in an agent action, or to
appear in a letter. You select an connected field by choosing the
name of a connection and then selecting a field from the
connected category.
 
For example, if you choose the connection Relates to... to appear
in a report view of the Contact category, you can choose any field
from the Company category (the category on the other side of the
connection), such as the company Name. In the report, all contacts
connected to one or more Company items along the Relates to...
connection display the company Name field from the connected
item(s).
 
 
 
 
CONNECTION
A connection is a link between items in the same category or in
different categories. It shows relationships among items in linked
categories. An example is "Joe Jones has a lunch appointment on
5/10/97". This connects a Person item to an Appointment item.
 
You can establish a link between any two items by creating a
connection definition in the category (if it does not already exist)
and using the connection control to specify the connected item.
CONNECTION CONTROL
A connection control appears in an item detail as a box with three
control buttons. It displays the item names of items that are
connected to the currently displayed item. The connection control
is labeled with the connection definition name.
 
A connection control is added to a category by adding a connection
definition between that category and another category (or a
category and itself). Selecting the name in the connection control
displays the item detail of the connected item.
 
If more than one connected item exists, one or both of the arrows
image/connarrs_shg.gif
at the right of the connection definition will be darkened depending
on whether the item displayed in the connection control is at the
top, bottom, or middle of the list of connected items. Use these
arrows to scroll through the list of connected items.
 
Selecting the assign connection button 
image/conndiag_shg.gif
at the right of the connection control displays a drop-down list of
the item names of all the items from the connected category.
Connected items have a check mark in the left margin next to their
names. Connect and disconnect items by selecting their names
(click the left mouse button two times on a name, or use the arrow
keys to highlight a name and then press the SPACEBAR).
 
Selecting the plus sign 
image/connadd_shg.gif
at the far right of the connection control allows you to add an item
to the connected category; the new item is automatically
connected to the displayed item detail.
CONNECTION DEFINITION
A connection definition is a link between two categories. A
connection definition allows you to create relationships among
items in linked categories. You can establish a link between any
two categories, or between a category and itself. An example of a
connection definition is Person-Has-Appointment.
 
A connection definition is added to a category by adding a
connection between that category and another category (or a
category and itself). A connection control is added to the item detail
and is labeled with the name given the connection when it was
created. Selecting the name in the connection control displays the
item detail of the connected item.
 
You can add a connection to your database by selecting the
Customize-Database-Connection command.
CONTROL MENU BOX
A window's control menu box is the shaded box in the far upper left
corner of the window, on the same level as the window's title bar.
Commands in this standard Windows menu allow you to restore,
move, size, maximize, minimize, close, and switch windows.
 
For an application window such as the main Commence window,
press ALT+SPACEBAR to access this menu. For a document
window, such as a view window within Commence, press ALT+-
(hyphen). Then, use the mouse (or the UP/DOWN ARROW keys
and ENTER) to select a command.
DATA FILE FIELD
The data file field is used to reference a file on your file system
from an item detail.
 
The data file field appears in its entirety (as described below) only
on item details; the file name associated with a data file field can
be displayed in a book, report or calendar view.
 
A data file field on an item detail contains the following elements:
FILE TEXT BOX
Contains the name of the file you want to reference. When adding
an item that has an data file field, type a file name in this box, or
use the Select button (see below) to search your available disks
for an appropriate file.
VIEW BUTTON
Allows you to display the file from the item detail. The extension of
the file must be associated with an editor in [Extensions] of the
file win.ini. See your Windows documentation for how to do this.
 
For example, if the extension .doc is associated with Word for
Windows in your win.ini file, you can select a .doc file in a data file
field and select the View button to edit the file with Word for
Windows.
SELECT BUTTON
Displays the Select File dialog box so that you can search your
available disks for a file.
 
You can include spreadsheets, word processor files, drawings,
images, and other types of information in an item.
 
 
 
 
DATABASE
A set of categories, data items, views, agents that define what data
you can store in Commence and how Commence acts in this data.
Commence provides you with a pre-defined databases that has a
set of categories, desktops, views and agents that support
calendaring, task management and contact management. You can
use this database as-is or you can customize it to meet your
specific needs. You can also create a custom database from
scratch or you can transfer elements from a different database into
your own database using the Transfer command. You can create
additional databases and open them with the Open/Manage-
Database
command.
 
On disk, each database is stored in its own Windows folder and,
because Commence is based on a proprietary database, you will
see many separate files on disk within that directory. Each file, no
matter how small, serves a unique and important purpose. Deleting
or renaming Commence data files (using DOS commands or some
other utility outside of Commence) can result in an unrecoverable
loss of data.
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
DATABASE DIRECTORY
A database directory is a directory in which Commence keeps its
data files. By default databases are created in a folder within the
Application Data Directory. To determine the database folder
containing the files for a particular database, select Open/Manage-
Database, highlight the desired database and select the Edit
button. The path will be the database directory.
 
 
 
 
DATABASE PASSWORD
A password selected by a Commence user to protect a database
from being opened by an inappropriate person.
DATE FIELD
The Date field type lets you enter a date using either the format
you selected from the Windows control panel (such as 1/16/96) or
using English date phrases such as next Tuesday.
 
If a date field is enabled for recurring dates (done at the time the
date field is added to a category), a Recurring Item button is
displayed when an item is added to the category. To enter a
recurring date, you can either select the Recurring Item button on
the Add Item dialog or you can enter an asterisk (*) in the recurring
date field. The asterisk tells Commence to invoke a dialog box that
asks for the recurring date sequence (such as Every Third
Tuesday) when you save the item.
 
Using filters, you can display all the items in a view that match a
particular date. If you use an English phrase in your filter, such as
today, the date won't be evaluated until you invoke the view. Using
Customize-Sort View, you can a view by ascending or descending
date: this will be evaluated first by year, then month, then day.
 
A single date field indicates a specific date, while two fields (e.g.,
start date and end date fields) can be used in a category to specify
a date range. Any category that has two date fields can use the
Gantt view type for displaying items graphically by date interval.
DAY TITLE
Day titles are displayed at the top of the daily calendar just above
the graphical display of time slots. Day titles are appointments or
events that are scheduled for a day but do not have a start or end
time. An example of a day title is "Anniversary".
DEACTIVATE
Prevents an agent from firing under all circumstances. To
deactivate an agent, select the Customize-Database Agent
command, highlight the agent name from the Agents list box,
select Edit then select the Inactive check box.
DEFINITION
The category definitions, view layouts, agent definitions, detail form
definitions, and color code templates that define a Commence
database.
DESIGN
The arrangement and definition of fields in a view or detail form.
DESKTOP
A set of views and item details that can be started all at once.
Multiple dektops can be defined for quick and easy access to data
used for common operations (e.g. contact management,
appointments, scheduling, etc.).
DETAIL FORM
A detail form is a formatted display of a single item from a
particular category. The detail form is displayed whenever you add
an item to or display an item from a category. The detail form
specifies the arrangement, sizes, fonts, and colors of all fields
included in the layout. Not all fields need be included in a particular
detail form. You can create multiple detail forms for each category.
Default forms can be chosen for item detail display and for the
detail view displayed when adding an item to a category. You can
also place a filter on a detail form so that it will be used for all items
that meet the filter.
DIALING DEVICE
A hardware device that allows your PC to automatically dial the
phone. This could be a modem controlled through Windows
Telphony (TAPI), or it can be a direct connection to a supported
TAPI-compliant telephone switch such as the AT&T PassageWay
system.
DIALOG BOX
A dialog box displays a command's options and parameters,
allowing you to review and change them.
DIRECT FIELD
A direct field is a field from the current category. For example, the
Address field is a direct field in the Person category.
DROP-DOWN LIST BOX
A drop-down list box is a special list box that displays only one
entry from a list (the currently selected entry) in a box with a down
arrow button at right. Selecting the arrow button displays the entire
list, with scroll bars (if necessary). You can then select a new item
or select the arrow button again to hide the list.
EDIT BOX
An edit box on a detail form or dialog box is any box that can hold
character data either typed in by you, pasted from the Windows
Clipboard, or pasted from a DOS file.
E-MAIL ADDRESS FIELD
This field type is used specifically for electronic mail (E–Mail)
addresses. Double clicking on an address within this field will open
up a blank mail note (using the default e–mail software) with the
To: field automatically filled in with the selected address.
Commence allows up to eighty (80) characters for each e–mail
address
EXCEL CELL FIELD
An Excel Cell field allows you to access a value from a Microsoft
ExcelŽ spreadsheet and display it in an item detail. Excel cell
values cannot be exported, printed or displayed in a view.
 
Excel cells appear on item details only. The field contains the
following elements:
VALUE
Displays the current value from the Excel cell.
FILE TEXT BOX
Contains the name of the file containing the Excel spreadsheet.
When adding an item that has an Excel cell field, type a file name
in this box, or use the Select button (see below) to search your
available disks for an appropriate file.
CELL TEXT BOX
Contains the name of the cell in the file named in the File text box
to be used as the source for the field value.
SELECT BUTTON
Displays the Select File dialog box so that you can search your
available disks for an Excel file.
EXPORT
To extract data from a Commence database into an external file in
a pre-selected format that other applications can use. Select the
Export-By View or Export-By Category command to create an
export file from items in your open database.
FIELD
A field is a place within a category used to store a particular type of
information. For example, some of the Person category fields are
Name, Address, Business Phone, and Notes. A category definition
can contain 1 to 100 fields, including connections. When you
create a category or add fields to an existing category, you must
specify the name of each field and its type.
 
The available field types in Commence are: calculation, check box,
data file, date, Excel cell, image, name, number, radio group,
sequence number, telephone, text, and, time. A category can have
only one Name field by default. Labels for fields are limited to 20
characters.
FIELD CODE
A variable that can be used in an agent or letter to represent the
data value of a field. See Using Field Codes in Templates for the
basics of using field codes in letters. See Using Field Codes in
Actions for the basics of using field codes in agent actions. A field
code has the form or either (%Field Name%) or (%Connection
Name%%Connected Categroy Name%%Field Name%).
FIELD VALUE
The value stored in a field.
FIELD SUMMARY
Summary statistics that are displayed or printed for a report. You
can include totals or averages for number and calculation fields
provided the Calculation Type option has been set for the fields
using the report layout's Field Options button. To request a field
summary select Tools-Field Summary when a report view is active.
FILTER
A filter creates a subset of the items based on a field value in a
category and displays the items in the view. For example, you can
filter a view to show only your appointments for today or your To-
Dos that are not completed. You can also filter on a connection to
another category. For example, you can filter on all People
employed by Widgits, Inc. Lastly, you can combine up to four filter
cirteria to get a set of items. For example, you can filter your To-
Dos to show the tiems that are due today, are Priority 1, and are
not completed. You can change the filter on a view by selecting
Customize-Filter View from the menu bar.
 
 
 
 
 
FORMATTED TEMPLATE
Formatted templates are word processor documents and are used
for writing letters using data stored in Commence. The word
processor is used to view, print, or fax letters based on the
formatted template. You can create a formatted letter template by
selecting Customize-Database-Letter Template.
 
Like text templates, formatted templates can include Inserted
Fields that are used to indicate merged values for the Commence
data item. However, unlike text templates, formatted templates
(and the letters you write with them) can include any special
formatting supported by the word processor: e.g., multiple fonts,
indents, bookmarks, fields, bitmaps, tables, etc.
 
 
 
 
GLOBAL SEARCH
A feature that lets you find all items in the database that contain or
exactly match a particular text string.
GROUP BOX
The group box element of a dialog box is used to organize several
options under one main heading.
GROUP CALENDARING
The capability to add an appointment to your client database and
share it with all authorized workgroups users. Used in conjunction
with the Tools-Schedule Meeting command and the Personal
Information Preference.
HYPERTEXT TRANSFER PROTOCOL
The system for requesting HyperText Markup Language (HTML)
documents from the World Wide Web (WWW).
HYPERTEXT MARKUP LANGUAGE
HyperText Markup Language is a collection of platform-
independent styles (indicated by markup tags) that are used to
define the various components of a World Wide Web document.
HTML
Refer to HyperText Markup Language
HTTP
Refer to HyperText Transport Protocol
IMAGE
 
Generally, an image is a piece of data produced outside of
Commence. It can be an image produced by a scanner, video
camera, illustration or presentation software, or any other software
capable of copying images to and from the Windows Clipboard.
IMAGE FIELD
Image fields hold Windows device-independent bit maps that are
produced by a scanner, camera, graphics software, and most
software that is capable of copying images to and from the
Windows Clipboard. To put a bitmap into an image field, it first must
be placed onto the Clipboard, then you can use Edit-Paste to put it
into the field. Alternatively, you can select the field and then choose
Edit-Paste From. This will allow you to copy an image directly from
a file.
 
If you are using Microsoft's Windows for Pen Computing®, you can
also draw into an image field. You can display an image field only
in an item detail. It can be printed along with the rest of the item
data.
IMPORT
To bring data from other applications into items in your open
Commence database. Select the Import command to import a file.
INACTIVE
An agent is inactive when the Active checkbox is unchecked for
that agent. An inactive agent cannot be triggered.
ITEM
An item is a record in the database. Each item can store values for
one or more of the fields defined for the category. For example, a
Person category would contain items that each describe individual
contacts, and might have the following fields defined: address,
phone number, comments, company employed by, etc.
There is an upper limit to the number of items that can be saved in
a category. Refer to the Database Capacities topic for more
information.
 
ITEM DETAIL
An item detail is a formatted display of a single category item; a
category item is displayed in an item detail whenever you add
items to or display items from a category.
 
The item detail's format is determined by the detail form chosen;
the detail form specifies the arrangement, sizes, fonts, and colors
of all fields included in the layout. Not all fields need be included in
a particular detail form.
 
You can create multiple detail forms for each category. Default
forms can be chosen for item detail display and for the item detail
displayed when adding an item to a category.
LAN
Local Area Network.
LAYOUT
A pre-defined or custom template that defines the field
arrangement in a view or an item detail. (See also: Paper Layout)
LEGEND
A small window that explains the color-coding scheme in a
calendar or Gantt chart view. To display a legend, select
Customize-Design View-Color Code, then check the Show
Legend check box.
LETTER TEMPLATE
A form letter that tells Commence what literal text to display and
where to insert data field values from the selected item(s).
 
It is stored as an external file in the tmplts subdirectory of your
active database directory. Letter templates can use fields from a
category and automatically fill in values when you write a letter.
Commence supports two types of letter templates, a Text
Template and Formatted Template. Both types can be used with
an external word processor.
LIST BOX
A list box shows a column of available choices, with scroll bars
provided if there are more than can fit in the box displayed.
Usually, you highlight one of the entries in the list, but some list
boxes allow you to highlight multiple entries.
MAPPING
The process of determining what fields and what order they should
be in when doing an import or export of ASCII, dBASE, or DIF files.
Select the Map button of the Template Definition dialog box to
define your field mapping.
 
 
 
 
 
MULTI-SELECT LIST BOX
A multiple selection list box is a special list box
where you can select more than one item in the list.
 
An item in the list box is selected (highlighted) by clicking the left
mouse button on the item. Clicking on another item selects that
item without removing the highlight from the already selected item.
To deselect a highlighted item, click the left mouse button on it. If
the list has more items in it than can fit in the list box, you can
scroll through the list by clicking the mouse on the scroll bar on the
right side of the box, or by clicking and dragging the box within the
scroll bar.
 
SEE ALSO:
Keyboard How to use a multiple selection list box with a keyboard.
 
 
 
 
NAME FIELD
Exactly one item name field is required for every category item; it is
used to store the item's name, which can be up to 50 characters.
The name field is labeled "Name" by default, but you can change
the label by editing the category's name field definition. This field
type cannot be removed from the category.
NUMBER FIELD
A number field can hold whole numbers (123), floating-point
numbers (123.45) or currency numbers ($123.45). You can also
use the Tools-Field Summary command to display the sum or
average of the number fields in a report view.
 
 
 
 
PAPER LAYOUT
A pre-defined or custom template that defines how you want a view
or item detail printed. You can define paper layouts for printing to a
day planner, or printing envelopes and labels. to define a paper
layout, select Home-Page Setup. You can also define a default
paper layout to be used when printing each view.
 
 
 
 
PASSWORD
You can set a password for your Commence database by selecting
Edit-Preferences-Database.
PREFIX STRING
Prefix string characters help you search for one item in a long list
of items in a report view, Gantt chart view, grid view, Book view, or
connection definition. Begin typing the name of the item you are
searching for and the highlight in the view moves to the first item in
the list whose name contains the prefix string you've entered.
PROPERTY
A property is a characteristic of a view or category that you can
change. Properties include: data definition; size; layout; fonts;
sorting order; filters; preferences; and other attributes.
 
All properties of the active view are accessible through Customize
on the menu bar.
RADIO BUTTON
A radio button allows you to choose an option to a command by
selecting the button. When selected, the center of the button goes
dark, indicating that the option is currently selected. If the center of
the button is not darkened, the option is not selected.
RECURRING ITEM
An item is recurring if the category is defined with a Recurring
Date Field and the recurring button is selected.
 
If you add an item and select the Recurring Item button or enter
an asterisk (*) in the recurring date field, and then select the Save
button, the Specify Date Range dialog box is displayed. You can
specify a frequency and date range for Commence to create
multiple items (such as every Monday in June). These items are
associated by a Related Date connection which allow these items
to be changed or deleted as a group.
Note: The date range specified for Recurring Items are referred to
as Recurring Dates. All recurring items are connected through the
Related Date connection.
RELATED DATE
A category is that defined with a Recurring Date Field will include
a Related Date connection. This connection is used to associate
items which are added as Recurring Items and allows these items
to be changed or deleted as a group.
 
 
 
 
 
REMOTE DATABASE
The databases in a Sync Link sync link group
that are kept synchronized via the base database.
Changes made on the remote database are re-synchronized to the
base database.
 
To define one or more remote databases for the sync link group;
open the base database and select the Sync Link Out command.
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
RESTORE
To select a compressed back up file and restore it to a usable
Commence database. Click the Commence Application Button in
the upper left corner, choose the Open/Manage Database
command, then highlight the database to overwrite and select the
Restore button. You can not restore into the active database.
 
 
 
 
RIBBON
An application Ribbon shows the available commands and is
located at the top of the window, just below the title bar. Similar
commands are grouped onto Tab pages. The available Tabs on the
Commence Ribbon are: Home; Edit; View; Customize; Tools; and
Help.
 
 
 
 
SAVED VIEW
A saved view is a view that has been stored on disk using the
View-Save View or View-Save View As
commands from the menu bar. When you create a new category, a
default report view is automatically saved. When you alter the
properties of an existing saved view, the resulting new view is not
automatically saved; you must use either View-Save View or View-
Save View As to retain the new view.
 
 
 
 
SCROLL BARS
A window's scroll bars allow you to view information that is longer
or wider than the current window size. If present, they are located
at the right side and bottom of the window.
SEARCH TEXT
The text for which you want to search all name, text, date, time,
data file, check box, and radio group type fields in one or multiple
categories. Specify the search text with the Global Search
command.
SELECTION FIELD
A selection field can have one of two attributes:
radio group: presents two to eight mutually exclusive values for a
field, depending on how many radio button fields you
name when you add or edit the field. You select one of
the radio buttons in the group as the default value.
 
combo box presents up to 4,000 mutually exclusive values for a
field, depending on how many selections you define
when you add/edit the field.
SEQUENCE NUMBER FIELD
A sequence number field is used to automatically assign a unique
number to every item added to a category. You can assign your
own starting value to this field type, which must be in the range
+/-2,000,000,000.
SNOOZE
A feature of agent-generated message boxes that allows you to put
a message box to sleep for a set period of time. When the time
elapses, the message box reappears.
STATIC AREA
This is a part of the screen that cannot be changed by the user.
The area can be changed by the program. The area is used to
diplay information to the user.
STATUS BAR
The status bar is located at the very bottom of the Commence
main window. It displays: a brief description of the currently
highlighted menu command (if any); a description of the view icon
at which the mouse is currently pointing (if any); operational
messages; today's date; the current time; and, a count of the
number of items from the active category displayed by the active
view.
SYNC LINK
A feature that lets you maintain identical databases on PCs that
are not connected via a LAN. See Sync Link Concepts for more
information.
SYNCHRONIZE
The process of communicating changes from one database to
another. Changes to definitions as well as to items are
communicated.
 
 
 
 
TELEPHONE FIELD
A telephone field lets you store telephone numbers in any format
you wish. If you have a modem connected to your computer and
Telephony installed, you can dial a telephone field displayed in a
view or a detail form.
Note: To dial international numbers, you must enter your telephone
numbers using the canonical format, +cc (ac) number, where cc is
the country code and ac is the area code.
 
 
 
 
TAB
When you select many commands in Commence a dialog box will
appear with many choices. Each of these choices will appear on a
Tab towards the top of the dialog box. Selecting a tab will bring all
of the options available for that tab to the front of the screen.
TEXT BOX
A text box is a rectangle into which you type information. An
insertion point (a vertical bar) indicates the editing point when you
move to the box. If there is already text in the box, all the text is
selected and anything you type replaces the entire selection. You
can also edit any text in the box using the delete, backspace,
arrow, and keyboard keys.
TEXT FIELD
A text field holds unstructured ASCII data. Text fields can be
specified in three sizes: up to 80 characters; up to 30,000
characters; or a user-specified number of characters up to 30,000
characters. Blank spaces, blank lines, and new line control
characters contribute to the character count.
TEXT TEMPLATES
A template for writing letters using data stored in Commence. A
text template does not supply sophisticated formatting capabilities
(i.e., no fonts, bold or italics, indents, etc.).
 
In the Send Letter dialog box, the type of a text template is TXT,
the standard Windows file extension for text only files. Virtually all
word processors can import, edit, and create text files. When you
highlight a text template in the list box and select View or Print,
Commence creates a text only letter by substituting the Field
Codes it finds in the template with values from the highlighted item
in the active view or from the active item detail. Field Codes are
special text strings that are used to represent data values from
Commence items. The use of Field Codes is described under
Customize-Database-Letter Templates.
TIME FIELD
A time field lets you enter a time using the format you selected
from the Window control panel (such as 4pm or 18:00). A single
time field can indicate a specific time, while two fields (e.g., Start
Time and End Time fields) can be used to specify a range.
 
A default value of Start in a time field definition in a category tells
Commence to start a timer when an Add item is opened. A default
value of Stop or Now in a time field definition of the same category
tells Commence to stop the timer when the item is first saved.
 
Any category that has at least one time field and one date field in it
can use the calendar view type for displaying items graphically by
time slot. A category that has a date field, time field and an alarm
checkbox can also be alarmed using the Item Alarm trigger type.
TITLE BAR
A window's title bar shows the title or name of the window. The
window title reflects the object(s) displayed in the window. The title
bar is located at the top of the window, between the control menu
box and the maximize and minimize icons. The title bar of the
active window is a different color or shade than the rest of the
windows on the desktop.
TOGGLE
A toggle is an option that can be either on or off. The toggle may
be a check-mark toggle on a menu (which is turned on if a check
mark appears next to the entry), a check box (which is turned on if
the box has an 'X' in it), or a radio button (which is turned on if the
button is darkened).
TOOL BAR
A toolbar is the area at the top of a view or form that displays icons
for commands that affect the active view or form. Toolbars provide
the ability to quickly execute these commands by selecting an icon
with the mouse. In many cases, keyboard shortcuts are also
available for toolbar commands.
The Agent Toolbar is an area reserved on the Ribbon to display
user-defined buttons. The Agent toolbar is not displayed when
there are no Agents defined with a Tool Bar Pick trigger.
The Calendar Toolbar appears at the top of calendar views.
TOOL BAR AGENT
A type of agent that appears as a button on the Home page of the
Ribbon, in the Agents group of buttons. Selecting the button
causes the agent to execute. Select the Customize-Database-
Agent command  to create or modify an agent.
TRANSFER
To copy a definition (such as a custom category, its views, and the
data in that category) to another dissimilar Commence database.
Optionally, you can transfer items and referenced data files along
with their definitions.
TRANSFER FILE
An external file created by the Transfer Out command.
SYNC LINK GROUP
A group of Standalone PC’s that are kept synchronized using Sync
Link.
TRIGGER
A trigger is an event or time that causes an activated agent to
evaluate the other agent conditions (if any) and, pending the
successful evaluation of the conditions, execute the action(s)
specified for the agent. A trigger (and an action) must be specified
in order to define an agent.
 
Triggers can be based on: the occurrence of a particular time; a
menu bar or command bar selection; adding, editing, or deleting an
item; the occurrence of date or time conflicts when adding an item;
the occurrence of an item alarm; the receipt of a specified DDE
(Dynamic Data Exchange) message from another Windows
application.
UNIFORM RESOURCE LOCATOR (URL)
A standardized format providing a pointer to information available
on the World Wide Web (WWW).
UPDATE FILE
The DOS file created by the Sync Link Out command.
URL
Refer to Uniform Resource Locator
URL ADDRESS FIELD
This field is used specifically for Uniform Resource Locator (URL)
addresses. Double clicking on a URL address will launch the Web
Browser (if not already open) and go directly to the indicated web
site. Commence allows up to 1024 characters for each URL
address.
VIEW
A view is a formatted display of category data. You create a view
by combining a category and a view type. The format of the view is
determined by the view layout.
 
 
 
 
VIEW BAR
The Commence view bar is a column of view icons on the left of
the main Commence window. By clicking the mouse on an icon,
you open the view associated with the icon.
 
Select the View-Save View As
command from the menu bar to add or remove a view's icon on the
view bar.
The view bar holds a maximum of 40 icons. You can display or
hide the view bar, choose whether or not to display icon labels
and/or the plus buttons for easy adds, by selecting the Edit-
Preferences-Tool Bars command from the menu bar, and checking
the appropriate check boxes.
 
 
 
 
VIEW TYPE
A view type is the basic display style for a view. There following
view types are supported: Book, Calendar, Gantt chart, Grid,
Document, Multi-View, Report (the default view type), and Report
Viewer. When speaking of views, a shorthand phrase like "a book
view" means "a view that uses the Book view type".
WILDCARD PATTERN
A wildcard pattern is used in commands which search for text or
filenames. Search text may contain wildcard characters, the
asterisk (*) and the question mark(?). A question mark means that
any character can occupy that position. An asterisk means that any
character can occupy that position or any of the remaining
positions.
 
The combination of normal text and wildcard characters is called a
wildcard pattern.
WORK AREA
Synonym for desktop.
Menu Command Keys
These key sequences allow you to choose menu commands
without a mouse. Mouse users may also find it easier to input
some commands from the keyboard rather than using the mouse.
ALT+[Key Tip Letter]
Select one of the tabs from the Ribbon.
Once you select a tab, the Key Tips will appear for each
command that is available. Press the letter shown in the
Key Tip for the command you want to use.
CTRL+P Print
SHIFT+DELETE
CTRL+X Edit-Cut
CTRL+INSERT
CTRL+C Edit-Copy
SHIFT+INSERT
CTRL+V Edit-Paste
DELETE Edit-Clear (if the cursor is in a field on a detail form and
some text is highlighted)
Deletes character to right of cursor if the cursor is in a field on a
detail form and no text is highlighted.
Edit-Delete Item (if an item in a view is highlighted)
CTRL+A Edit-Add Item
ENTER Edit-Edit Item (i.e., highlight an item in a view using the
arrow keys and press ENTER to edit the item. If you
highlight an empty time slot in a calendar view and
press ENTER, you execute the Edit-Add Item
command)

F1 Help-Index or dialog and message box help
