Dialog Box and Detail Form Keys
Use the following keys to fill out dialog boxes and detail forms
without the aid of a mouse:
 
TAB Select the next field in a dialog box or item detail.
SHIFT+TAB Select the previous field in a dialog box or item
detail.
CTRL+TAB Move to the next tab in an item detail.
CTRL+ALT+TAB Insert a TAB character into a text field.
SPACEBAR Activate the currently selected radio button or check
box field; place or remove a check mark next to an
item name in a connection field list box.
ARROW KEYS Move the insertion point in any field containing
text; scroll through connected item names in a
connection field (this is the equivalent of selecting
the narrs_shg.gif buttons with the mouse); highlight
items in a list box; select entries in a selection field.
RIGHT ARROW For connection fields, opens a list box showing
the names of connected items (this is equivalent to
selecting the ndiag_shg.gif button with the mouse);
once you do so, SPACEBAR connects and
disconnects the highlighted item in the list box;
ENTER closes the list box.
LEFT ARROW For connection fields, open the item detail of the
connected item whose name currently appears in the
connection field (this is equivalent to selecting the
item name with the mouse).
PLUS SIGN on
numeric keypad For connection fields, open an add item detail
form for the connected category (this is equivalent to
selecting the nadd_shg.gif button with the mouse).
ENTER or CTRL + S Save the form or dialog box; close a
connection field's list box.
SHIFT+ARROW KEYS Select text in a field.
DELETE Delete the selected field text.
ENTER Insert a carriage return into a text field.
INSERT Change the insertion mode in a field containing text.
ALT+BACKSPACE Undo an edit in a field containing text.
(Effective only before save item.)
ESC Cancels changes for item or connection pop-up.
CTRL+F For connection pop-up, toggles the filter
CTRL+1 For connection pop-up, displays pop-up in one list.
CTRL+2 For connection pop-up, displays pop-up in two lists.
CTRL+A For connection pop-up, saves changes and adds item to
connecting category.
CTRL+E For connection pop-up, saves changes and edits
highlighted item in connecting category.
CTRL+B Move to the previous item shown by the detail form.
CTRL+N Move to the next item shown by the detail form.
CTRL+K Move to the first item shown by the detail form,
CTRL+L Move to the last item shown by the detail form.
Multiple Selection List Box Keys
Use the following keyboard keys to select from and scroll through
a multiple selection list box:
 
SPACEBAR For the list box item with the list box cursor (a dotted
outline) on it, selects (or deselects) the item
without affecting already selected items in the list.
RIGHT or DOWN ARROW Moves the list box cursor to the next
item in the list.
LEFT or UP ARROW Moves the list box cursor to the preceding
item in the list.
PAGE UP Scrolls the currently highlighted item to the bottom of
the list box and moves the cursor to the first visible
item in the list.
PAGE DOWN Scrolls the currently highlighted item to the top of
the list box and moves the cursor to the last visible
item in the list.
HOME Scrolls the first item in the list to the top of the list box and
moves the cursor to it.
END Scrolls the last item in the list to the bottom of the list box
and moves the cursor to it.
Keys for Arranging Detail Forms
The following keys allow you to arrange fields on the Design
Detail Form dialog box without the aid of a mouse.
 
TAB select the field you want to move
SPACEBAR "grab" the highlighted field -- alignment guides
appear when the field is "grabbed"; press
SPACEBAR again to release the field
ARROW KEYS move a "grabbed" field in small increments
CTRL+ARROW KEYS move a "grabbed" field in large increments
View Control Keys
You can use the keys in the table below to navigate around any
book, calendar, Gantt chart, grid, or report view).
 
UP ARROW highlight the item above the currently highlighted
item
DOWN ARROW highlight the item directly below the currently
highlighted item
HOME highlight the first item in the view
END highlight the last item in the view
PAGEUP scroll up
PAGEDOWN scroll down
CTRL+PAGEUP scroll left
CTRL+PAGEDOWN scroll right
LEFT ARROW (book view only) go backward one page
RIGHT ARROW (book view only) go forward one page
 
Calendar views provide additional mouse controls on the control
panel located under the monthly calendar. Keyboard equivalents
to using these controls are listed in the following table. These
keys change the active date and may change the information
displayed in daily, weekly, and monthly display modes (see
Customize-Design View).
 
CTRL+T Return to daily display mode for today.
 
LEFT ARROW move the outline box in the monthly calendar to
the previous day; change the active date to that
day's date
 
 
RIGHT ARROW move the outline box in the monthly calendar to
the next day; change the active date to that day's
date
 
CTRL+LEFT ARROW change the monthly calendar to display the
previous month; change the active date to one
month previous to the current active date
 
CTRL+RIGHT ARROW change the monthly calendar to display
the next month; change the active date to one
month after the current active date
 
CTRL+PAGEDOWN change the monthly calendar to display the
current month for the following year; change the
active date to one year after the current active date
 
CTRL+PAGEUP change the monthly calendar to display the
current month for the previous year; change the
active date to one year before the current active
date
 
CTRL + 1 display in Day mode
 
CTRL + 2 display in Two Day mode
 
CTRL + 3 display in Two Week mode
 
CTRL + 4 display in Month mode
 
CTRL + 6 display in Six Week mode
 
CTRL + 7 display in Week mode
 
CTRL + 9 display in Grid mode
 
CTRL + Y display in Year mode
Window Control Keys
The following keys allow you to control the windows displayed by
Commence. Most of them apply to all windows in the Windows
environment.
 
Note that document windows are identified by the short dash
displayed in a box in the upper left hand corner of the window.
Application windows are identified by the long dash displayed in a
box in the upper left hand corner of the window.
ALT+- Select the control menu box for a document window.
ALT+SPACEBAR Select the control menu box for an application
window.
<underlined letter>
ARROW KEYS+ENTER Once you select a control menu, press
the underlined letter of a command to execute that
command, or use the arrow keys and ENTER to
select a command.
CTRL+F4 Close a document window.
CTRL+F5 Restore a maximized document window to its previous
smaller size.
CTRL+F6 Switch to the next document window.
SHIFT+CTRL+F6 Switch to previous document window.
CTRL+F7 Move a document window.
CTRL+F8 Size a document window.
CTRL+F9 Minimize a document window.
CTRL+F10 Maximize a document window
Mouse Shortcuts
For mouse users, selecting means pointing at something on the
screen (like a time slot in a calendar view, a button in a dialog
box, a name or label in a list box, etc.) and double-clicking the left
mouse button.
In dialog boxes where there are several command buttons,
pointing and double-clicking on a list box entry with the mouse
defaults to an edit operation, though not all dialog boxes have an
edit operation (in this case, there usually is no default command).
Pointing and double-clicking the left mouse button on an item in a
view invokes the Edit-Edit Item command and displays the item
detail of the item.
Pointing and clicking the left mouse button on a view icon on the
view bar or on a command button on the tool bar opens the view
or invokes the command.
As you pass the mouse pointer over a button on the view bar, tool
bar, or the control panel of a calendar view, the message box on
the status bar displays a short description of the button's purpose.
A short command description is displayed in the status bar's
message box when you point at a menu name on the menu bar
with the mouse, hold down the left mouse button, and move the
mouse pointer over the command names on the menu. Releasing
the button while pointing at a command executes the command.
Calendar Items Dialog Box This dialog box is
displayed when you double click on a Day Title arrow
or on the date in a weekly, monthly, or yearly
calendar display mode.
 
This dialog box displays all of the appointments for the selected
day. Highlight the name of the item from the list box and then
select the Add, Edit, or Delete button. Selecting Add causes an
Add Item to screen appear. Selecting Edit causes an Item Detail
screen to appear. This screen lets you view or edit the item. You
can move the appointment by editing the date or time values.
Selecting Delete deletes the highlighted item. Select OK to exit
the dialog box without choosing an item.
 
Alternatively, you can double-click the left mouse button while
pointing at the name of the item you want to select. This causes
an Item Detail screen to appear (as does the Edit button).
Active Date
The calendar view initially displays today's date in the center of
the Calendar Tool Bar at the top of the calendar window; this is
called the active date. As you select different dates and calendar
Display Modes using the Calendar Tool Bar, the active date
displayed changes to match the dates you select. The date is
displayed in the format you set through the Windows Control
Panel.
Display Modes
There are a variety of display modes for the calendar view. They
are Day, Two Days, Week, Two Weeks, Month, Six Weeks, Grid
and Year modes.
 
Item names associated with the active date are displayed in the
calendar view window in a Day, Two Days, Week, Two Weeks,
Month, and Six Weeks display mode. The Year display mode
shows all the dates for the active year by displaying twelve
monthly calendars.
 
The default display mode is the Day mode. To change the display
mode, select a display mode from the drop down list on the left of
the Calendar Tool Bar.
 
Select one the buttons below to see a description of the features
and controls in each display mode:
 
Day Day Display Mode
Two Days Two Days Display Mode
Week Week Display Mode
Two Weeks Two Weeks Display Mode
Month Month Display Mode
Six Weeks Six Weeks Display Mode
Grid Grid Display Mode
Year Year Display Mode
Calendar Tool Bar
At the top of every Calendar view there is a tool bar that lets you
change certain aspects of the calendar display. Use the control
buttons to change the display mode, move to a different active
date or to show/hide the color code legend.
DISPLAY MODE
Select the calendar format you wish to display. Choose from
day, 2 days, week, 2 weeks, month, 6 weeks, or year.
LEGEND
Selecting this button or selecting the Customize-Design View
command ,click on the Display tab and mark the Show Legend
checkbox, toggles the display of the legend. When the legend is
displayed, a small window appears that explains the color
coding used in the busy bars. You can change the color coding
used in a Calendar view by selecting a color coding template
with the Customize-Design View command and clicking on the
Colors tab. You can create new color coding templates using
the Customize-Database command and selecting the Color
Code tab.
PREVIOUS DAY OR INTERVAL
Selecting this button displays the previous day, 2 days, week, 2
weeks, month, 6 weeks, or year, depending on the current
display mode.
CURRENT DAY OR INTERVAL
Selecting this button lets you change the date, week, month or
year that is displayed. This displays the Select Date dialog box
where you can display monthly calendars to select a date and
then select the OK button to return to the calendar view.
NEXT DAY OR INTERVAL
Selecting this button displays the next day, 2 days, week, 2
weeks, month, 6 weeks, or year, depending on the current
display mode.
RETURN TO TODAY
Selecting this button displays today's appointments. This is a
quick way to return to today's appointments after examining
past or future appointments.
PREVIOUS DAY WITH ITEMS
Selecting this button displays the first day (before the currently
displayed day or interval) for which items are scheduled.
NEXT DAY WITH ITEMS
Selecting this button displays the next day (after the currently
displayed day or interval) for which items are scheduled.
 
Select Date Dialog Box
The Select Date dialog box is displayed when you click on the
CURRENT DAY OR INTERVAL button on the Calendar Tool
Bar or when you double-click on any date field in an item detail or
dialog box. In this dialog box you can display monthly calendars
to select a date and then select the OK button to return to the to
the previous screen with the selected date.
 
The elements of this dialog box are:
YEAR BUTTONS
Select a button for the year you want to see in the Monthly
Calendar. If the year you wish to select is not displayed, press
the Left Arrow button to scroll to previous years or select the
Right Arrow button to scroll the years ahead and then select a
year button.
MONTH BUTTONS
Select a button for the month you want to see in the Monthly
Calendar.
MONTH CALENDAR
This displays the month selected by the Year and Month buttons.
The initial month displayed is the month of the date field from
where you started this dialog box. Click on a day in the Month
Calendar to change the selected date.
OK
Select the OK button to return to the previous screen and have
the selected date replace the date field from that screen.
CANCEL
Select the Cancel button to return to the previous screen without
changing the date.
Working with Detail Form Tabs
A detail form is organized into a series of “pages,” with each page
displayed as a separate folder tab across the top of the opened
detail window. Not all detail forms require multiple pages in order
to display all pertinent information. In those situations, a single
folder tab will appear rather than the multiple tabs shown above.
By grouping related areas of a detail form, and including them on
their own, dedicated page, completing a complex form can be
done easily by switching between pages, which is as easy as
clicking the left mouse button on the desired folder tab. Up to 25
individual tabs may be defined per Detail Form.
 
TAB NAME
The Tab Name identifies the tabbed window that will appear
across the opened Detail Form and must be unique within a
detail form. It can be up to 16 characters.
 
CUT
Select an area of the detail form or a group of fields, then use
Edit-Cut or Right click-Cut. This copies the selected objects into
the buffer and deletes them from the visible tab. .
 
COPY
Select an area of the detail form or a group of fields, then use
Edit-Copy or Right click-Copy. This copies the selected objects
into the buffer.
 
PASTE
Pastes the buffer contents into the active tab.
 
DELETE
Deletes the selected objects. CAUTION: if nothing is selected
then all objects on the form including all tabs are deleted. Ifyou
wish to cancel this command, select File-Exit from the menu
without saving your changes.
 
ADD TAB PAGE
Adds a new page to the detail form. Fields can be displayed on
the page using drag & drop, or using the copy/paste commands
from another tab page.
 
DELETE TAB PAGE
This will delete the active tab. Be sure to click the tab you wish
to delete so that the fields on that tab are visible before using
this command. Commence asks for confirmation before deleting
the tab and will not let you delete the only tab for a detail form.
 
SWAP TABS
This allows you to rearrange the order in which the tabs will
appear across the opened Detail Window. The active tab is
replaced with the tab you choose from the list, and the active tab
moves to the other tabs position. The tabs (and ultimately the
corresponding pages of the Detail Form) should be arranged in
the order in which the pages will be completed when entering
data.
 
PROPERTIES
Lets you control the appearance of the form. Refer to Form
Properties or Chapter 5 of the Commence User’s Guide for more
details.
Tab Order Dialog Box
Commence automatically sets the tab order based on each field’s
position on the detail form. The tab order starts with the field in
the upper left corner, then tabs to the next field(s) in that row (if
any). Then Commence tabs to the beginning of the next row,
similar to hitting the carriage return key on a typewriter. You can
determine which fields are in the same row by aligning the tops of
the boxes.
 
Select Format-Tab Order the Detail Form Design dialog box to
change the tab order for data entry, use the Up and Down
buttons to reorder the fields.
 
UP OR DOWN BUTTONS
Highlight a field, then repeatedly click on the Up or Down
buttons until the field is in the postion you want. Repeat this step
for the associated field label to keep the field and its label
together for design purposes. Note the label will not be included
in the tab order for the finished form. This will be used to
determine the tab order of fields when entering data.
 
OK
Select the OK button to save the tabbing order for the fields on
the detail form tab.
 
CANCEL
Select Cancel to cancel your changes.
 
AUTO TAB ORDER
Select this button to base the tabbing on the arrangement of the
fields on the detail form tab.
View Types
See Also
When creating a new viewterms_view, you select a view
typeterms_view_type to tell Commence how to structure the
window used to display view data.
There are several view types delivered with Commence. Each
provides a distinct display format. They are briefly described
below; select the name of the view type below to see a detailed
description of its capabilities:
Book displays items alphabetically by the item name field of
each item (up to 20 fields can be included); each page of the
book displays items whose item name fields begin with the
letter appearing at the top of the page
Calendar displays items on a daily, weekly, or monthly basis in
an appointment calendar format; each item must have start
time, end time, and date values
Gantt Chart displays items in a traditional Gantt Chart format;
each item must have start date and end date values
Grid displays fields from one or more categories in a
row/column format. It can be used in two modes: read-only and
update mode.
Document diaplays a Web page as a view window on the
Commence desktop.
Multi-View Displays a set of related views in one window frame.
The view is split to show the primary and secondary views.
Report displays user-selected fields of an item in a multi-row,
multi-column format; the graphic format of the display (fonts,
labels, etc.) is also user-selectable
Report Viewer displays data from your Commence database in
WYSIWYG format as a print preview. Data can be viewed,
printed, and exported to various file formats.
 
You can use prefix matching to find a particular item in a long list
of items in a report view, Gantt chart view, Book view, or Grid
View.
Once you choose an appropriate view type and display a view,
you can refine how the view displays data items by selecting a
command from the Customize-Design View menu.
Choosing a View Type and View Layout
A view layout specifies required information for the view type
used in a view. This information may be required field mappings
(for a calendar or Gantt chart), the list of fields to display (for a
book or report), or various display aspect of the view type such as
fonts (for a report).
Calendar and Gantt chart views require specific field types in the
category used by the view. A calendar one time fields (one for
start time) and one date field (the due date field; i.e., the date the
item takes place). A Gantt chart view requires two date fields (one
for start date and one for end date of each item). Commence
needs to know the names of the fields from the active category to
use for these required pieces of information.
When you create a view, Commence picks appropriate defaults
for the view layout, and displays the resultant view. Commence
always successfully picks defaults for book and report views.
It is possible that Commence may not be able to pick appropriate
defaults for a calendar or Gantt chart view, either because the
view does not have required field types or has more than one
field of the required type(s). If Commence cannot pick reasonable
defaults, a dialog is displayed that informs you of this fact.
If the view does not have fields of the required type(s), the view
cannot be displayed using the view type you selected. You must
Cancel and try to create the view again using a different view
type.
You can display and change the view layout for a view using the
Customize-Design View command and clicking on the Fields tab.
Multi-View Properties Wizard
See Also
When you create a new multi-view or edit an existing one, you
are prompted for the various properties of the view. Select the
appropriate values and then press the Next button until all options
are selected.
 
 
PAGE 1: Setting the view properties
A multi-view window can be split into two panes, either
horizontally or vertically. You can choose your preference based
on what works best with the data you are displaying or how you
like to arrange views on your desktop.
 
Enter a new view name to describe the multi-view as a whole.
This unique “View Name” is used to identify the view and cannot
be a duplicate of an existing view name in the Open/Manage
Views list. All the required properties have now been set. You
may click Next> to continue setting up the multi-view or configure
the optional settings below.
 
To add a shortcut on the View Bar for the multi-view, set the
following properties:
 
1 Type a brief View Icon Label.
2 Choose an appropriate icon to represent the multi-view in
general.
3 Choose whether you wish to display the multi-view shortcut
on the View Bar and/or Views Menu, and for which
desktops.
 
PAGE 2: Setting the view pane size/position
 
The option to Save Position, Size, and Panes on Exit is turned
on by default. This allows you to resize the multi-view window as
a whole and retain that size and position on the desktop.
 
The splitter bar separates the primary window from the secondary
window in a multi-view. The window panes are initially split with a
default value, however the splitter can be reset to any proportion
totaling 100%. If the option to Save Position, Size, and Panes
on Exit is also enabled, then you can adjust the proportions any
time after the multi-view has been created by simply dragging the
splitter bar on screen.
 
Enabling the option to Save Position, Size, and Panes on Exit
is the only way to save changes to the multi-view window; the
View-Save View commands will continue to work on the active
view within the multi-view. However the multi-view window as a
whole will not be affected by the standard view commands.
 
PAGE 3: Setting the Primary View(s)
The primary view for a multi-view window can be any of the
following view types:
. Book View
. Calendar View
. Gantt View
. Report View
. Grid View
 
When selecting the primary views, you may choose a view-linked
view. Linked views in the primary pane are able to interact with
views outside the multi-view window or with the filtering toolbar in
the primary pane of the multi-view. However, linked views in the
secondary pane of a multi-view window will only respond to the
primary view within the same multi-view window.
 
The list of views can be sorted by View Name, Type, or by
Category. Double-click on the column heading to resort the list.
Once you have found the view of your choice, click the Select
View button or click on the handle to the right of the View Name.
Confirm that the view you chose is shown in the “Selected
Primary Views” box below. You may click Next> to continue
setting up the multi-view.
 
PAGE 4: Form Option
If the detail form option is left unchecked, double-clicking an item
in the primary window of a multi-view opens the form in a new
window. Because the new window is outside the multi-view frame
it is not linked to the secondary views in the multi-view window.
For example, browsing to the next item in a new form window will
not update the secondary views in the multi-view window.
 
When the form option is turned on, double-clicking an item in the
primary window opens the form within the primary window of the
multi-view frame. The primary view will use the filters and sorts
defined for the view you selected to determine which items to
display.
 
When you select the form option, you must also choose a default
detail form layout from the list of forms in the category. The form
layout you select will be used to display all items as you traverse
through the items in the view using the browse buttons on the
Detail Form toolbar. For this reason, it is recommended that you
select a general form displaying all the necessary fields,
regardless of the item data. Filtered detail forms would typically
not be appropriate.
 
To save your choice, click the Select Form button. Confirm that
the view you chose is shown in the “Selected default form view”
box below. Click Next> to continue setting up the multi-view.
 
PAGE 5: Setting the Secondary Views
At least one secondary view must be defined before you can save
the multi-view. Secondary views in a multi-view window can be
any of the following view types:
. Book View
. Calendar View
. Gantt View
. Report View
. Grid View
 
The list of views can be sorted by View Name, Type, or by
Category. Double-click on the column heading to resort the list.
When selecting secondary views, keep the following guide-lines
in mind:
Including a view in a multi-view window does not limit you
from re-using that view elsewhere in the application. Views
that are part of a multi-view window can still be opened
independently outside the multi-view window.
Multi-views can support up to 100 view tabs in the
secondary window.
Multi-views are optimized to handle numerous large views
in the secondary window. Large views are those displaying
hundreds of thousands of items or views approaching the
maximum number of fields.
Each view in the secondary window is not loaded until
after the view tab has been opened, not when the multi-
view window is opened.
Linked views in the secondary pane of a multi-view
window will only respond to the primary view within the
same multi-view window.
Unlinked views and views that are linked to different
categories can also be added as view tabs in the
secondary window. However, there is no dynamic
relationship between the data in unlinked views and the
data in the primary window of the multi-view.
 
Once you have chosen a view, click the Select View button or
click on the handle to the right of the View Name. Confirm that the
view you chose is shown in the “Selected Secondary Views” box
below. Repeat this step to select as many secondary views as
needed. To undo a selection, click the {X} button. This will remove
the view tab from the multi-view window. De-selecting a view in
this manner will not delete the view from the database.
 
After you have selected the secondary views, you can customize
the tab label for each view as needed. The tab label can be up to
15 characters. This label is used for display purposes only, to aid
you in locating a particular view in the multi-view window.
Duplicate tab labels are allowed but are not recommended to
avoid confusion. The tab labels are not used to determine the
order of the tabbed windows.
 
The tabbed windows will be arranged in the secondary window
from left to right in the order shown here, not alphabetically. If you
would like to rearrange this order, use the [up arrow] and [down
arrow] buttons to move the tabs up or down in the list. Views
accessed most often should be moved to the top of the list.
 
Click Finish to save the multi-view configuration. The completed
multi-view can now be accessed from the list of views defined
under the View - Open/Manage Views dialog. The multi-view is
now ready to use.
Document View Options
The Document view provides a way to integrate Internet and
intranet based applications and pages directly on your
Commence desktop.
 
Document views are created using the same command as regular
views. When in Document Options:
 
1 Enter the URL pointing to the HTML document you wish to
display, using the following examples.
Type the Internet address - for example,
www.commence.com
Type the complete path and file name to an HTML file - for
example, c:\program files\commence
workgroup\Web Companion.htm
Type the complete path and file name to a Microsoft Office
document - for example, c:\my documents\test.doc
(assumes test.doc is an existing file in this directory)
2 Click OK to open the view.
 
Tip Keywords can be used when specifying the URL - for
example, (-ProgramDir-)\Web Companion.htm
 
For more information on the Document View, see Document View
Type.
Customize-Design View-Fields Tab (Book Views)
See Also
Selecting the Customize-Design View command while a report
view is the active view displays the Design View tab.
This tab allows you to choose the fields to appear in the book and
set field options as explained below. You can also select the
name type field of items from connected categories to display in
the book. A book view can display up to 20 fields per item.
Any changes you make to the design are temporary unless you
use Views-Save View or Views-Save View As to save the
changed view.
The Fields in Book and Fields Not In Book list boxes show the
list of fields that appear in the book and those that don't appear in
the book, respectively.
To append a field to the list of fields in the book (making the new
field appear last in the book), highlight the field name in the
Fields Not In Book list box and select the Append Field button.
Shortcut: Double-click the mouse on a field in the Fields Not In
Book list box to append it to the Fields In Book list without
selecting the Append Field button.
To insert a field into the list of fields in the book, highlight a field
name in the Fields Not In Book list box and highlight the position
where you want the field inserted in the Fields In Book list box.
Then, select the Insert Field button.
To remove a field from the book, highlight the field name in the
Fields In Book list box and select the Remove Field button.
Shortcut: Double-click the mouse on a field in the Fields In Book
list box to remove it without selecting the Remove Field button.
The Fields in Book list box displays field names in the order in
which the fields appear in the book. If a field is in the the incorrect
position, you must remove the field from the report, then place
the field in the correct position by using either the Insert Field or
Append Field button.
You can also combine fields in the report by selecting the
Combine Fields button and then selecting the desired fields.
Customize-Design View-Display Tab (Report and Grid
Views)
 
In addition to the print title and paper layout options, Report and
Grid views also allow you to set the following display options:
FORMAT
REPORT VIEW
Select the format for a report by choosing the Horizontal or
Vertical radio buttons from the Format group box. Horizontal
format prints one item per row with fields listed from left to right;
field labels appear across the top of the report. Vertical format
displays one item per column, with fields listed from top to
bottom; field labels appear on the left side of the report. Fields
are listed in the order in which they appear in the Fields In
Report list box in the Fields tab.
 
GRID VIEW
Select the format for a grid view by choosing Detailed or
Summary from the Format group box. Detailed displays the
groups with the items. Summary displays just the group heading.
 
ALLOW UPDATE MODE (GRID VIEW ONLY)
If checked, this option allows updating of items in the view without
opening a detail form.
 
SPACE BETWEEN FIELDS (REPORT AND GRID VIEW)
Set the space between columns and rows in either format by
changing the values in the Space Between Fields group box. In
the Horizontal text box, type the amount of space between
columns. In the Vertical text box, type the amount of space
between rows.
NUMBER OF COLUMNS
REPORT VIEW
Specify the number of columns for a vertical format report by
typing in the appropriate number in the Number of Columns text
box. The default is 1; the maximum is 20.
 
GRID VIEW
This option is not customizable in a grid view.
 
SHOW LABELS
REPORT VIEW
Include labels for fields in a view by turning on the Show Labels
check box. Labels are included on the left side of a vertical format
report and across the top of a horizontal format report.
 
GRID VIEW
Include labels for fields in a detailed grid view by turning on the
Show Labels check box. Turning on this option displays the view
with group headings. Note, to show the groups, the sort order for
the view must at least have group headers on for the primary sort
field.
 
REMOVE BLANK LINES BETWEEN ITEMS
REPORT VIEW
By default, all items in a report are assumed to be of the same
height as the largest item. For horizontal reports, this is the height
of the largest field in the report; for vertical reports, this is the
height of the largest item in the report. If the largest item is
significantly bigger than other items in the report, this can lead to
a great deal of wasted space on the report. To eliminate this
space for items that are smaller than the largest item, turn on the
Remove Blank Lines Between Items check box.
 
GRID VIEW
This option is not customizable in a grid view.
 
COMPRESS LINES BETWEEN FIELDS
REPORT VIEW
Check to remove any extra blank lines between fields. This option
does not affect the blank space inside a field.
 
GRID VIEW
This option is not customizable in a grid view.
 
SHOW GRID LINES (GRID VIEW ONLY)
Displays grid lines on the view.
 
CONNECTION FIELDS (REPORT AND GRID VIEW)
Connection fields are fields from a connected category. When you
select a connection definition name on the Fieldstab, you specify
the field from the connected category to be displayed on the view.
Since an item in your report may be connected to more than one
other item, you must specify the maximum number of connected
items that can be displayed, and the number of lines needed to
display each connected field. These options are global settings
for the view; they apply to all the connection fields in the report.
 
Number of Items specifies the maximum number of connected
items for which the connection field will be displayed. The default
number is set to 2. You may enter any number between 1 and
750. For example, say your view is defined for the
Person/Company category, and you wish to display Notes
connected to each person. A person may be connected to one
note or hundreds of notes. The default setting would display only
the first 2 connected notes per person, even if they are connected
to more notes.
 
Lines Per Item specifies the amount of space allocated on the
view for each instance of the connection field in the view. The
default is a height of 1 line per item. You may enter any number
between 1 and 750. Using the example above, only the first line
of each note would be displayed in the view.
 
If you select values that are too small to display all the connected
items for a particular connection field, the word (more) is
displayed at the bottom of the connection field.
Finding Items with Blank Number Fields
To filter for a blank number field, you need to obtain all the items
with a zero value.
1. Display the view you wish to filter. For help, see Opening a
View.
2. Select the Customize-Filter View command from the menu
bar. To set the filter, select the Filter1 tab.
If you are creating more than one filter select the Summary tab.
3. In the Filter for All Items list box, select the number field to
filter on.
4. Select the Equal To comparison operator from the drop down
list.
5. Enter the number 0 into the Field Value text box.
6. Select the OK button.
This returns you to the active view and the filter will be in effect.
Finding Items with Blank Connections
To filter for a blank connection, you need to obtain all the items
except those which have the connected Name field filled with any
characters. To do this:
1. Display the view you wish to filter. For help, see Opening a
View.
2. Select the Customize-Filter View command from the menu
bar. To set the filter, select the Filter1 tab.
If you are creating more than one filter select the Summary tab.
3. Next to the Filter for All Items list box, turn on the except
checkbox.
4. In the Filter for All Items list box, select the connection field
to filter on.
5. Highlight the Name type field for the connected item in the
Filter by list box.
6. Select the Contains comparison operator from the drop down
list.
7. Enter the ? wildcard character into the Field Value text box.
8. Select the Save button.
This returns you to the active view and the filter will be in effect.
Edit-Preferences
Calendar Choose calendar view type preferences.
Database Allows you to password protect your database.
Dialer Choose a dialing device.
Event Logs Choose the categories to use for logging letters and
phone calls.
Letters Choose preferences for writing letters, including which
word processor to use for formatted letters.
Meeting Select the category, fields and connection definitions
used when scheduling a meeting.
Other Choose miscellaneous preferences.
Other Apps Configure Commence to be used with other
applications, such as your E-Mail application,
Outlook Calendar, or handheld.
Personal Info Designate an item that contains information about
yourself and select which connection definitions
will default to this item.
Tool Bars Display or hide the tool bar, the status bar, and the the
view bar. Specify a directory of icon files to be
used on the Tool bars.
View Choose preferences for saving views.
Change Database Password Dialog Box
This dialog box is displayed when you select the Set Password...
button on the Database Preferences dialog box. This option lets
you set a password for your copy of the database, whether it be a
personal, local database, or a shared client database. This
password is different from the login ID and password which must
also be entered when you attempt to open a shared database
which has been password protected by the Commence
Administrator.
To change or create a password follow these steps:
1. If this is the first time this database has been password
protected, the Old Password field is grayed out. Otherwise,
type your current password in the Old Password field.
2. Type the password you want into the New Password text
box. The characters you type are echoed with asterisks in
the text box.
3. Type the password again into the Verify Password text box.
4. If you correctly entered the Old Password (if any), and
entered the same password into the New and Verify
Password text boxes, Commence returns you to the Other
Preferences dialog box. If you made a mistake, Commence
displays a message box asking you to try again. Select OK
and go back to Step 4.
5. Select Save to close the Database Preferences dialog box.
Select the Cancel button to return to the Database Preferences
dialog box without changing the database password.
Print Letter Dialog Box
This dialog box is displayed when you select the Print button on
the Send Letter dialog box with a text template highlighted.
COPIES
In the Copies text box, enter the number of copies of the letter
that you want to print.
DRAFT QUALITY
Check the Draft Quality check box if you want a lower resolution
printout and your printer supports this print mode.
FONTS
If you want to select a different font for the letter, chose the Fonts
button. Commence shows you the Select Font dialog box. The
specific fonts available depend on the configuration of your
system. Keep in mind that the font you select will be applied to all
the characters in the letter.
OK
Select OK in the Print Letter dialog box to start printing the letter.
The letter prints on the default printer selected by Commence
(see Printer Setup).
CANCEL
As with other printing done by Commence you can cancel the
printing operation by selecting the Cancel button from the dialog
box displayed while printing.
Letter Template Tab
See Also
This tab is displayed when you select the Customize-Database-
Letter Template command. Select one of the Add buttons to add
a Text or Formatted template, or choose a letter template from
the list and select either the Edit or Delete buttons.
 
The Letter Template tab contains these elements:
CATEGORY NAMES
Use the Category Names drop-down list box. category to select
the category containing the data for your letter.
LETTER TEMPLATES LIST BOX
This list box shows the names and types of all letter templates
available for use with the category you selected.
ADD TEXT BUTTON
Select this button to add a text template for the selected category;
Commence displays the Letter Template dialog box.
ADD FORMATTED BUTTON
Select this button to add a formatted template for the active
category; Commence displays the Add Formatted Template dialog
box.
EDIT BUTTON
Select this button to edit the template highlighted in the Letter
Templates list box; Commence displays the Letter Template
dialog box or Edit Formatted Letter Template dialog box,
depending on the type of the letter template.
 
Shortcut: You can double-click the left mouse button while
pointing at a template name to edit the template without selecting
the Edit button.
DELETE BUTTON
Select this button to delete the template highlighted in the Letter
Templates list box. Commence asks for confirmation before
deleting the template. Select Yes from the confirmation message
box to delete the template; No to cancel the deletion.
CLOSE BUTTON
Once you are done working with letter templates, select OK to
return to the work area.
Home Menu Commands
Use commands on the Home menu to:
 
Message Log View a log of error messages, agent messages,
and other informational messages.
Send Letter Create a letter using fields
from a highlighted item or the active view. A letter template
(which specifies the fields to use in the letter) must be
defined for the active category in order to write a letter
using fields from that category.
Dial Phone Dial the phone number of the highlighted item; if
there is more than one number displayed, a dialog box is
displayed so that you can choose the number to dial.
Send Generate a report or calendar view in HTML format and
attach to an email message.
Print Print views, item details, and labels.
Printer Setup Setup printers.
Print Preview Preview the printout prior to sending it to the
printer.
Page Setup Define Page layouts; set printed page margins;
choose whether or not to print a title and date on each
printed page
Agents Menu 1, 2, 3 ... Launch user-defined agents with menu
pick triggers; also reviews the pre-defined menu pick and
tool bar pick agents..
Launch Views 1, 2, 3 ... Open a view whose name appears at
the bottom of the Views menu.
 
Commence Application button Commands
Commands to manage your database are available by clicking
the Commence application button in the upper left corner.
 
Open/Manage Database Open a database, add, edit, delete,
backup, and restore databases.
Exit Exit Commence.
New/Edit Agent Dialog Box
See Also
If you select the Add, Edit or Copy buttons on the Agent tab, you
get a dialog box that lets you setup an agent’s components.
 
The title of the dialog box will be either New Agent (if you are
adding or copying an agent), or Edit Agent (if you are editing an
existing agent). The elements within the New Agent and Edit
Agent boxes are identical.
 
If you are adding a new agent and just want to review the options
for conditions and actions, first press the T button to select any
trigger. Then select either 1 or A to view the possible conditions
and actions, respectively. You can make selections for both
conditions (1 and 2) and still go back and change the trigger.
However, you should not make a final selection of the agent
actions until after you have decided on the trigger and conditions
for the agent since different selections can change the available
options for some actions.
 
The elements of this dialog box are:
AGENT NAME TEXT BOX
If you are adding or copying an agent, this text box is blank; you
must fill in an agent name before you can save an agent. If you
are editing an agent, the Agent Name text box contains the name
of the agent you are editing; you can change the name by typing
new text into the box.
 
ACTIVE CHECKBOX
Turn this checkbox on to activate the agent. Uncheck the box to
deactivate the agent. Another way to temporarily deactivate all
agents is to use a command line option on /noagents.
 
Note: When you save a new agent, the status of the new agent
will be set to activate so you can start using your new agent
immediately.
IF THIS TRIGGER OCCURS GROUP BOX
This box shows the current trigger selection for the agent. If there
is a label of (none) to the right of the button, then there is no
trigger currently set for that agent.
 
If you want to view or change the trigger, select the button labeled
T to bring up the Add Trigger dialog box; if a trigger is already
defined, the Edit Trigger dialog box is displayed. The Edit
Trigger dialog box is identical to the Add Trigger dialog box,
except it is filled in with the details of the existing trigger.
 
AND THESE CONDITIONS ARE TRUE GROUP BOX
This box shows the current condition selections for the agent. If
there is a label of (none) to the right of the button, then there is
not a condition currently set.
 
To view or edit a condition, select one of the buttons labeled 1 or
2 to display the Add Condition dialog box; if a condition is already
defined, the Edit Condition dialog box is displayed. The Edit
Condition dialog box is identical to the Add Condition dialog
box, except it is filled in with the details of the existing condition.
 
You cannot select either of the 1 or 2 buttons unless a trigger has
been selected.
 
THEN PERFORM THESE ACTIONS GROUP BOX
This box shows the current action(s) selected for this agent. If
there is a label of (none) to the right of a button, then there is not
an action specified for that position. To view or edit one of the
actions, select one of the buttons A, B, C, or D to display the Add
Action dialog box; if an action is already defined, the Edit Action
dialog box is displayed. The Edit Action dialog box is identical to
the Add Action dialog box, except it is filled in with the details of
the existing action.
 
You can look at the selection of triggers by selecting the T button
under If this Trigger occurs. However, you will not be able to
select a condition button (1 or 2) or an action button (A to D)
unless a trigger has been selected.
 
OK AND SAVE BUTTONS
If you are editing an agent in the Edit Agent dialog box and have
not made any changes, select OK to return to the Agent tab. If
you have made any changes to the agent, the OK button changes
to a Save button. If you select Save, Commence saves any
changes that you make to the agent (its name or the
specifications of its components).
 
CANCEL BUTTON
Select the Cancel button to return to the Agent tab without
saving a new agent or to undo changes to an edited agent.
Time Conflict Layout Dialog Box
In order to enable time conflict checking for the selected category,
you must specify the name of a date field, and the names of two
time fields, and select the Report Time Conflicts check box.
Once you do so, Commence will warn you if a new item conflicts
with the time and date of an existing item.
 
Another feature of time conflict checking is checking start time
and end time fields to make sure you specify an end time that is
later than the start time. To enable this checking for the active
category, you need only select fields for Date, Start Time, and
End Time, and select the Save button (you don't need to turn on
the Report Time Conflicts check box).
 
If you cannot select fields for one or more of the parameters, you
will not be able to enable time conflict checking for the category.
You can, however, add date and/or time fields to the category and
then enable time conflict checking. See Adding and Editing Fields
 
The date field specifies the date on which the item, occurs, and
the time fields specify the time span of the event described by the
item. You can specify the same field for Start Time and End
Time, indicating that items in the category have no time span
(i.e., they occur at a particular time, not for a duration of time).
 
Commence uses the date field to select items from the category
with which to compare the new item; it uses the time fields to
calculate a time span that is compared to the time span of other
items occurring on the date in the date field. If the date field of the
new item matches the date field in an existing item and the time
spans of the items overlap in whole or in part, a time conflict is
reported.
 
 
If the Connection to use for conflict checking connection
definition is not set and you have time conflict checking turned on
(the default), you are warned whenever you add an appointment
to a time slot with an existing appointment, regardless of who is
involved in the appointment.
 
The fields you choose for time conflict checking can be changed
at any time.
Enabling Time Conflict Checking
1. Select the Customize-Database-Category command. You
will see a Category Names list box. Select the category for
which you want to enable time conflict checking.
2. Press the Time Conflict button. The Time Conflict dialog
box is displayed.
3. Select the arrows on the drop-down list boxes for Date,
Start Time, and End Time to display a list of all the date
and time fields (as appropriate) in the active category. If
there are no fields of a particular type, only the name (none)
will appear in the associated drop-down list when you select
it. Select a field from each list box, or Cancel if appropriate
fields don't exist.
4. Turn on the Report Time Conflicts check box.
5. Select the name of the connection definition you want
Commence to use when checking for time conflicts by
highlighting that connection definition in the Connection to
use for conflict checking drop-down list box.
6. Select the Save button.
If one of the field boxes is set to (none) when you select the
Save button, Commence attempts to pick an appropriate
field. If it cannot, time conflict checking will not be enabled
for the category.
Disabling Time Conflict Checking
1. Select the Customize-Database-Category command. You
will see a Category Names list box. Select the category for
which you want to enable time conflict checking.
2. Press the Time Conflict button. The Time Conflict dialog
box is displayed.
3. Turn off the Report Time Conflict check box.
4. Select the Save button.
Print Items Action A Print Items action can print
single items or sets of items. You can chose to print
the trigger item, the condition items, or a saved view
by selecting one of the radio buttons under Print
Items In. If you select View, the drop-down list box
under View Name lets you select from your existing
set of views.
 
Selecting the Print Options button will allow you to specify
details about the printing.
 
The print action is useful for agents that will periodically print
reports. For example, you can create an agent that will print out
your calendar for the week every Monday morning at 9 AM.
Add/Edit Action Dialog Box
To add, edit or delete an action in your agent, select one of the
four action buttons (1 to 4) on the New Agent or Edit Agent dialog
boxes. You then see the Add Action dialog box (if you are adding
a new action) or the Edit Action dialog box (if you are editing an
existing action). The controls and operation of the two boxes are
identical.
 
The same dialog box is used for all actions, but its contents vary
depending on what action you highlight in the Action Type list
box. A brief description of the action you have selected is shown
under the list box. The right hand side of the dialog box is used to
set the options for the action type you select. As you select
different action types, this side of the box will change to show you
the appropriate options.
 
Every agent can have up to four actions. Actions define the work
that an agent does when its trigger has fired and its conditions (if
any) are true. You can specify some actions independently of the
agent's trigger or conditions, but in most agents actions use the
trigger or condition item(s). Thus it is necessary to define an
agent's trigger and conditions before defining the actions. In fact,
you cannot even view the condition and action options until you
make a trigger selection. (The trigger can be changed later, but if
any actions were specified using the trigger item from the first
trigger, they also have to be changed.)
For complex agents, it is important to know that an agent's
actions are performed in the order that they appear in the agent's
definition. This is significant because if one action edits the trigger
item or the condition items, then a subsequent action will receive
the edited items, not the original ones.
 
Actions can range in sophistication from displaying a simple
message on the status line to editing multiple fields in an
incoming set of data items. One action, the message box, even
lets you define up to four sub-actions that can be performed in
response to the selection button on the message box.
 
If you select Clear in the Add Action or Edit Action dialog box,
the options for the current action are cleared. The OK command
saves the current options, and the Cancel command restores the
previous values (in an edit situation).
 
If an action is changed or cleared before selecting Cancel, you
can select the original action type and the original values will
appear.
 
Option specification for individual agent actions is described with
each action type; choose an action from the list below.
 
The full set of actions available for Commence agents is the
following:
 
Add Item
Adds a new item and optionally displays the item
Application Launch
Starts an application with command line parameters Backup
Make a backup of the open database
Create E-Mail
Create and send an electronic mail message
Delete Items
Deletes an existing item
Edit Item
Edits an existing item and optionally displays the item Export
Exports to a pre-selected file using a pre-selected template
Icon Modification
Changes the appearance of an icon on the icon bar
Import
Imports from a pre-selected file using a pre-selected template
Manage Desktop
Cascades, tiles, or closes all views. Also, opens a desktop.
Message Box
Displays a message dialog box (and optionally presents user
selectable sub-actions) New View
Displays a new view
Open View
Displays an existing view
Outlook Sync
Starts a synchronization of data with Microsoft Outlook Play
Multimedia
Play a Windows multimedia file.
Print Items
Prints a view or an item
Print Letter
Generates and prints a letter using a template
Process E-Mail
Deletes or moves electronic mail message into a folder Send
DDE
Sends a DDE message to another application
Set Agent Status
Makes another Agent active or inactive
Status Bar Message
Displays a message on the status line
Sync Link In
Apply Sync Link In updates using a pre-defined file Sync Link
Out
Generate a Sync Link Out to a pre-defined file
Use Telephone
Will answer a telephone call, dial the telephone, or hang up the
telephone
 
All the actions (except Icon Modification, Open View, and Set
Agent Status) allow you to use the trigger item or one of the two
condition item sets (if you have selected conditions for the agent)
as part of the action specification. See Using Trigger and
Condition Data.
 
Using Field Codes, you can also insert values from a trigger or
condition item into your action specifications. See Using Field
Codes In Actions.
Select Directory Dialog Box This dialog box appears
when you choose the Select button from a dialog
box that requires a directory path in a text box. The
Select Directory dialog box lets you search through
the available disks on your system for an
appropriate directory path.
 
The elements of this dialog box are:
DIRECTORIES
This element contains two items a Directory Line to display the
selected path and a list box of the directory structure.
DRIVES
This is a list of available drives for your system. Select the drive
where the directory your are selecting is located.
OK
Use this button to accept the directory specified. The path shown
on the Directory Line is the resulting path used by the dialog box
to which you return.
CANCEL
Select the Cancel button to cancel the select directory operation.
HELP
Select the Help button to provide help for the Select Directory
dialog box.
CREATE DIRECTORY
If you cannot find the directory or you would like to create a new
directory select the Create Directory button.
Using Transfer Commands
See Also
The general procedure for transferring definitions from one
database to another is:
 
1. Use the Open/Manage Database command to open the
database containing the definitions you want to transfer.
2. Select the Transfer Out command. This command copies
selected categories, views, agents, and data (if desired) from
your database to a DOS file.
3. In the Transfer Out Options dialog box, select either
Category or View. Then select the specific categories or
views and agents to transfer.
4. Turn on the Items check box to transfer data as well as
definitions from the selected categories or views. If you
select the Items box, you may also choose to transfer any
DOS files pointed to by data file fields in transferred items.
Turn on the Related Data Files check box to do so.
5. Select OK. The Select Transfer File dialog box appears.
6. Type a file name for the transfer file in the Filename text box
and select OK.
The transfer out begins. Commence keeps you informed of its
progress and tells you when it is finished. Select OK when
the message box indicates that the transfer is completed.
7. Using the File-Open/Manage Database command, open the
database into which you want to read the transfer file just
created. If this database resides on a computer other than
the one on which you created the transfer file, be sure to
make the transfer file accessible to this computer via floppy
disk, a shared LAN drive, etc.
8. Select the Transfer In command. This command copies the
definitions (and data, if any) from the DOS file that was
created with the File-Transfer Out command into the
currently open database.
9. In the Select Transfer File dialog box, enter the path name
of the transfer file in the Filename text box and select OK.
10. If the transfer file contains data, the Transfer Rules dialog
box appears. Make the appropriate selections and select OK.
The transfer in begins. Commence keeps you informed of its
progress and tells you when it is completed. Select OK when
the message box indicates that the transfer is completed.
Customize-Design View-Display Tab (Calendar and
Gantt Views)
In addition to the print title and paper layout options, Calendar
and Gantt views also have one or more of the following display
options:
DISPLAY MODE
For calendar views, selecting Display Mode opens a cascade
menu from which you choose one of the mode options, Image,
Day, 2 Days, Week, 2 Weeks, Month, 6 Weeks, Year and Grid
modes. The currently selected option has a check mark to the left
of its entry on the cascade menu.
 
Shortcut: As an alternative to using the Display Mode command,
you can use the mouse to click on the display mode drop down
list in the calendar view window (see Calendar Tool Bar).
TIME SCALE
Allows you to change the time scale for a calendar or Gantt chart
view. The time scale determines the time interval used in the view
and also affects how many items can be viewed on one screen.
 
For calendar views, selecting Time Scale opens a cascade menu
from which you choose one of the time scale options, 5, 6, 10, 15,
20, 30, or 60 Minutes. The currently selected option has a check
mark to the left of its entry on the cascade menu. The time scale
specifies the number of minutes between the appointment slots
(default: 30 minutes) in Day display mode (see Display Modes).
 
For Gantt chart views, selecting Time Scale opens a cascade
menu from which you choose one of the time scale options, Day,
Week, Month, Quarter, Year, or Auto. The currently selected
option has a check mark to the left of its entry on the cascade
menu. The time scale specifies the time gradation to be used for
displaying task intervals (default: auto -- Commence examines
the range of data being displayed and selects an appropriate
scale).
START TIME
Allows you to change the starting time of a calendar view (i.e., the
time associated with the first time slot in day display mode).
 
In day display mode, a calendar view displays multiple time slots,
each of which displays the names of the appointments scheduled
for the time associated with the slot. By default, the first time slot
is associated with 8:30AM.
 
You can change the time associated with the first time slot of a
calendar view by typing in a new Start Time. The time specified
must be in the same format that you have selected in the
Windows Control Panel.
 
Commence validates your chosen start time according to the
currently set time scale. That is, you cannot specify a start time
that, given the currently set time scale, would result in empty time
slots in day display mode. Commence will display a message box
and ask you to choose another time if your start time is invalid;
the message box also tells you the latest possible valid start time
given the currently set time scale.
Filter Tabs
When you select one of the Filter tabs on the Filters tab, it
displays the fields and connections from the active category,
along with the buttons described below.
 
FILTER FOR ALL ITEMS (By field value)
To filter by field value, first highlight a field Filter for All Items list
box. The available filter options on the right half of the screen will
change, depending on the field type of the highlighted field. See
Filtering by Field Value for more information.
 
Note: If you are filtering for all items based on the value in a field
and not a connection, the Filter By list box will be grayed out,
and the words (Field value) will appear in this list box.
FIELD VALUE
Enter the field value to filter for in this text box. You may use
wildcards to keep the filter broad based. In the case of date and
time fields, you may also use natural language date and time.
FILTER BY
To filter for all items connected to a particular item name, first
highlight a connection name in the Filter for All Items list box.
Notice that the words (Connection to item) appear in the Filter by
list box and the right half of the screen now displays a list of all
the item names and clarify fields (if any) for the connected
category. Then select an item name from the list of items in that
category.
 
If you prefer, rather than selecting a particular item name, you
can turn on the checkbox to Use highlighted item in open view.
The latter option filters the view so that the only items shown are
those connected to whatever item is currently highlighted in an
open view for the connected category. For more information about
setting up view links, see Customize-Link Views.
 
You are not limited to filtering on the name type field only. To filter
on any field within the connected category, highlight the field in
the Filter by list box and select a field value on the right. To filter
on a connection within the connected category (selected in the
Filter for All Items list box) you can highlight a connection name
in the Filter by list box and select the connected item name from
the list box on the right half of the screen.

add a new color code template, or highlight an existing template
and choose the Edit or Delete buttons.

This list box shows all the categories in the active database.

This list box shows all the currently defined color templates for all

on which to base your color code template, then the Color Code

EDIT BUTTON
Selecting this button displays the Color Code Template dialog box
filled in with the definition of the color template highlighted in the

Shortcut: Mouse users can double-click the left mouse button
while pointing at a template name to edit it without selecting the
Edit button.
DELETE BUTTON

Connection Dialog Box
Selecting the Customize-Database-Connection command
displays the Connection dialog box, which lists the names of the
connections from the active category to other categories. Use this
dialog box to establish connections between categories.
 
See Connections Overview for a discussion of the basics of
connections and connecting items together.
 
See Connecting an Item to Another Item for how to connect items
using connection controls on item detail forms.
 
Use the buttons on the right side of the dialog box to add, edit,
and delete connection definitions as described below.
ADD BUTTON
To add a connection definition for the active category, select the
Add button to display the Add Connection dialog box. From this
box, you can add a connection definition between any two
categories (or between any category and itself) in the currently
open database.
EDIT BUTTON
When editing connection definitions, you are allowed to rename
both directions of the connection definition; no other changes
are allowed.
 
To edit a connection definition shown in the list for the active
category, highlight the connection definition whose name you
want to edit and select the Edit button. The Edit Connection
Names dialog box is displayed, with the current connection
definition names filled in. You can then edit the name(s) for the
connection definition.
 
Shortcut: Double-click the left mouse button on the connection
definition name you want to edit.
 
Caution: Changing certain connection definitions may cause
unintended effects in your data. An example would be changing
the name of the simple connection (Related Date) between the
Calendar category and itself; changing the name of this
connection definition would remove the associations among
recurring date items in the category and these items would not
be able to be changed or deleted as a group.
 
Commence detects when you choose such a connection
definition for editing and displays a warning message box that
informs you of the potential problems in changing the name of
the connection definition you've selected for editing. Select the
OK button on the warning message box to continue. If you want
to abort the editing of this connection definition, select OK and
then select the No button on the next dialog box displayed.
DELETE BUTTON
To delete a connection definition shown in the list for the active
category, highlight the connection definition in the list box and
select the Delete button. Commence asks for confirmation
before deleting the connection definition. Select Yes from the
message box asking for confirmation to delete the connection
definition.
 
Typically, you will want to delete a connection definition that you
are not using to free up space in a category for more field
definitions. A connection definition adds two fields to the total
number of fields in the category. You should be careful, however,
when choosing a connection definition to delete.
 
Caution: Deleting certain connection definitions may cause
unintended effects in your data. An example would be deleting
the simple connection (Related Date) between the Calendar
category and itself; deleting this connection would remove the
associations among recurring date items in the category and
these items would not be able to be changed or deleted as a
group.
 
The same is true for agents. If you delete a connection definition
on which an agent depends, the agent will no longer function as
intended. You will have to update any agents that depend on the
deleted connection definition yourself using the Customize-
Database-Agent command.
 
Commence detects when you choose such a connection
definition for deletion and displays a warning message box that
informs you of the potential problems in deleting the connection
definition you've selected for editing. Select the OK button on
the warning message box to continue. If you want to abort the
deletion of this connection definition, select OK and then select
the No button on the next dialog box displayed.
ADVANCED BUTTON
The list of items in the connected category can be sorted and
filtered to make items easier to locate. For example, you may
want to filter your connection to To-Do’s to only show
uncompleted To-Do’s.
 
To filter the connection list, press the Select Filter button. The
filter can be toggled on and off by the end-ser by clicking the
filter button on the connection toolbar. Checking theFilter by
default box will apply the filter automatically when the
connection list is opened. (See Filter Tab for more information
about how to filter a list of items)
 
To sort the connection list, press the Select Sort button. (See
Sort Fields for more information about the Sort Fields dialog
box.) For example, you may want to sort connections to the
Calendar in date descending order so the most recent items
appear at the top of the list.
 
To layout the columns of data displayed in the connection field,
press the Field Option button. (See Connection Field Options
for more information about the Field Options dialog box.)
 
By default, connections allow as many related items as needed
to be selected. For example, an Appointment can be related to
multiple attendees, and likewise a contact may be related to
many appointments. This is known as a “many-to-many”
relationship. If necessary, the connection can be configured to
allow “many-to-one”, “one-to-many” or “ one-to-one”
relationships by turning on the option to Allow At Most 1
Connected Item. This option must be enabled in both directions
of the connection for a true one-to-one relationship.
CLOSE BUTTON
Select Close when you are finished working with connection
definitions.
Connections Overview
A connection is a bi-directional link between two categories or
between a category and itself. Use the Customize-Database-
Connection command to add, edit, and delete connections
between any two categories.
Once you establish a connection, you can link items from the
categories participating in the connection together to create
relationships among your data, and reduce the amount of
redundant data in your database.
A connection is named when you create it, and the name should
reflect the reason that the categories are linked together.
For example, let's say you have a Contacts category and a
History category, as in the tutorial data. A link from the Contact
category to the History category called "Relates to" can be used
to connect a Contact item to the History item for the notes that
refer to that person in the Contact item. Likewise, the History item
would have a connection called “Relates to” that links the person
to the note in the History category.
You must specify a name for both directions of a connection
between two categories, as in the example in the previous
paragraph. A connection that has two directions is called a paired
connection.
For a connection between a category and itself, you give the
same or different names to both sides of a connection. A
connection between a category and itself that describes only one
direction is called a simple connection.
An example of a simple connection would be one between the
Contact category and itself that is given the name "Works with". It
would be used to link together people who work together without
qualifying the relationship in the way a paired connection does.
For example, a paired connection between items in the Contact
category that expresses the same relationship could have the
names "Works for" and "Manages", indicating a staff/supervisor
relationship.
Linking items together using connections is a two-step process.
1. Set up the simple or paired connection at the database level
using the Customize-Database-Connection command. This
specifies the categories for both sides of the connection and
allocates connection controls on category detail forms.
2. Connect an item to another item by displaying an item detail
for one of the items you want to connect and choosing the
name of the other item from the connection control for the
connection to the other item's category. Each item-to-item
connection can be thought of as an instance of the simple or
paired connection.
In the case of the "Relates to" paired connection between the
Contact and History categories, an individual item in the Contact
category is linked to an item in the History category by selecting
the name of the History item in a connection control on the
Contact item. Thereafter, you can display the item detail of either
item and display the other item by selecting its name from the
connection control.
Connection controls are displayed only in an item detail. When
you create a connection definition, a connection control is
allocated automatically on every detail form of the category or
categories involved in the connection.
image/confield_shg.gif
The label above the connection control is the connection name
and contains the name of the connected category, in the case of
our example, Has History indicates a connection to the Contact
category.
The connection control shows the item name of the first
connected item in the text portion of the box (connected item
names are listed alphabetically in a connection control). The
fields displayed in the connection control can be customized
using the the Customize-Database-Connection command and
clicking the Advanced button. On the right side of a connection
control, there are three control buttons.
Use the up and down arrows 
image/connarrs_shg.gifto scroll
through the list of connected item names; there is more than one
name if one or both of the arrows is darkened . The middle
control, the connection button 
image/conndiag_shg.gif, is used
to connect and disconnect items from the connected category.
The add item button 
image/connadd_shg.gifon the extreme right
is used to add a new item to the connected category and
automatically connect it to the currently displayed item detail.
(See Dialog Box and Detail Form Keys for how to use these
controls without a mouse.)
See Connecting an Item to Another Item for detailed instructions
on using connection controls on item detail forms.
Connecting an Item to Another Item
Individual items are connected using the connection control
boxes that appear on detail forms. This procedure shows you how
to connect two items together using an existing connection
definition.
 
If an appropriate connection definition does not already exist
between the categories in which the items reside, you must add
the connection definition before starting this procedure (see
Customize-Database-Connection).
 
1. Open a view of one of the categories in the connection
definition and display an item detail of one of the items you
want to connect. The detail form chosen for the item detail
must display the connection control for the connection you
want to use to connect the items.
2. Select the connection diagram mage/conndiag_shg.gifon the
connection control labeled with the name of the connection
definition you want to use to connect the displayed item to
another item.
For example, if you wanted to connect the item in the tutorial
data's Contact category to the item in the mailingType
category that describes the various mail lists to which the
person has subscribed or shown interest, you would select the
connection diagram on the connection control labeled
Mailing/Event Type. A list box displaying the names of all the
items from the connected category appears.
3. Select the items from the list that you want to connect to the
contact item. You can select as many item names as you want.
Each time you select an item name, a check mark appears to
the left of it. The check mark indicates that a connection
instance exists between the Contact category item and the
checked item in the mailingType category.
If an appropriate item in the connected category does not exist,
you can create a new item in the connected category by
selecting the add item icon mage/connadd_shg.gifin the
connection control. The plus button may not be visible if the
connection has been designed to 'HidePlusButton' or the
button may be disabled if you do not have write (add and
modify) permissions to the category.
The buttons on the connection toolbar are as follows:
