4. After selecting all the item names that you want from the
connected item list, select the save button on the connection
toolbar to return to the detail form.
Once you connect items to the displayed item, the connection
control displays the connected items in a list. You can scroll
through the list of connected items using the up and down
arrow buttons mage/connarrs_shg.gifto the left of the
connection diagram.
5. Select the Save or Cancel button on the detail form to return
to the view you opened in Step 1.
Detail Form Tab
The Customize-Database-Detail Form command displays the
Detail Form tab. From this tab, you can select the Add button to
add a new detail form, or highlight an existing detail form and
choose the Edit, Copy, or Delete buttons. At the bottom of the
tab, the Detail Form Description displays a description of the
highlighted form.
 
CATEGORY NAMES
This list box shows all the categories in the active database.
 
DETAIL FORM NAMES
This list box shows all the currently defined detail forms for the
selected category. Commence automatically creates a default
form called Initial Form for each newly created category. All the
fields in a category appear on the Initial Form at creation, though
you can edit the initial form like any other form.
ADD BUTTON
Selecting this button displays the Design Form dialog box. This
allows you to add a new detail form for the category.
 
EDIT BUTTON
Selecting this button displays the Design Form dialog box filled in
with the definition of the detail form highlighted in the Detail Form
Names list box. This allows you to make changes to the existing
form.
 
Shortcut: Mouse users can double-click the left mouse button
while pointing at a template name to edit it without selecting the
Edit button.
 
COPY BUTTON
Add a new form by Copying the form highlighted in the Detail
Form Names list box and saving it to a new name; choosing the
Copy button displays the Design Form dialog box.
 
DELETE BUTTON
Delete the existing form highlighted in the Detail Form Names
list box (if you have more than one); Commence asks for
confirmation before deleting the form and will not let you delete
the only form for a category.
 
SET DEFAULTS BUTTON
Set Defaults for adding and displaying items; choosing the Set
Defaults button displays the Set Default Detail Forms dialog box.
 
CLOSE BUTTON
Select Close when you are finished working with detail form
definitions.
Importing Connections
If you attempt to import data from a file, and the template you are
using maps information from the file into connection definitions in
your category, the connected data must be present in your
database, or the connections will not be made. If this occurs, a
message box telling you that connections have not been imported
is displayed at the completion of the import operation.
For example, let's say you received an exported file that contains
Project data for various customers. Let's also assume that the
exported Project category and your Project category are exactly
the same and that this file contains valid connection data for each
item in the file. Each item in the exported file, then, contains the
names of the connected items in the Person/Company category
at the time the category was exported.
In order to import this connection data, the exact same connected
items in the Person/Company category must exist in your
database, or the connections specified in the export file will be
ignored. In other words, the Projects will be imported into the
Project category, however the connection to the Person/Company
will be left blank.
To import connection data contained in an exported file, we
recommend the following procedure. We'll use the example of
importing a Project category with connection definitions to the
Person/Company category to illustrate the procedure.
1. Create two import templates:
A. one for the Person/Company category that imports the items
required for the Client Person/Company connection. This
data should contain information about the client who
contracted the project. Only fields should be mapped (and
no connections).
B. one for the Project category that imports all the project-
related fields you want including the connection to the
Person/Company category
2. Import the Person/Company category using template A above.
3. Import the Project category using template B above. During
this second import, the newly added Projects will automatically
be connected to the existing Person/company items that were
imported previously.
Note: If one or more of the connection definitions in the category
you want to import point back to the category itself, you have to
import the category twice; once to import the items (without
connections) and the second time to import the connections.
Template Definition Dialog Box
This dialog box is displayed when you are adding, editing, or
copying an import/export template from the Import/Export dialog
box.
 
Type or edit the template name in the Template Name text box.
The name can be up to 30 characters and must be unique.
 
Choose your target category by selecting a category name from
the Category Name drop down list box.
 
You have to tell Commence three more things before you are
done defining a new import/export template:
 
1. How to read the external file. You do this by selecting a file
format from the File Format radio group.
2. Which fields you want to update in your target category and
how those fields relate to the external file. You do this by
selecting the Map button, which displays the Template
Mapping dialog box.
3. How to process incoming data with new and duplicate item
names. You do this by selecting the Import Rules button,
which displays the Import Rules dialog box.
4. How to process outgoing data to an external OLE DB/ODBC
database that may contain duplicate item names. You do
this by selecting the Export Rules button, which displays
the Export Rules dialog box.
 
Select the Save button to save your template definition. Select
Cancel to discard the template you are working on.
File Format
In order to read your external data file, Commence needs to know
how this file is formatted. Commence can read three different
formats: ASCII, dBASE, and DIF. Commence can also read/write
data directly from/to an OLE DB/ODBC database.
 
ASCII formats are very simple: each item appears on a separate
line and fields are separated by a pre-defined delimiter, such as a
comma or tab, or by having fixed column positions. Commence
supports all three of these ASCII formats: comma, tab, and
column delimited.
 
dBASE and DIF are more sophisticated file formats that include,
besides the data, descriptive information about the file's field
names, sizes and data types. Because dBASE and DIF formats
are self-describing, if you provide a sample of the file you want to
import, Commence can read the format and use that information
to help you define your import/export template.
 
In all, Commence supports seven choices of file formats: three
ASCII formats, dBASE and DIF without sample files, and dBASE
and DIF with sample files. These choices are displayed in the File
Format radio group on the Template Definition dialog box and are
described below. The default format is ASCII, comma delimited.
 
ASCII, comma delimited
Each field is separated by a comma and surrounded by quotation
marks. Each item starts on a new line. For example: "Jones,
Jim", "Red Bank, NJ","(908)530-5221"
"Sands, Sally", "New York NY","(212)226-3345"
ASCII, tab delimited
Each field is separated by a tab and surrounded by quotation
marks. Each item starts on a new line. For example: "Jones, Jim"
"Red Bank, NJ" "(908)530-5221"
"Sands, Sally" "New York NY" "(212)226-3345"
ASCII, column delimited
The fields start and end at specified positions. Each item starts
on a new line.
Jones, Jim Red Bank, NJ (908)530-5221
Sands, Sally New York, NY (212)226-3345
dBASE, sample file
dBASE IV files ending with a .DBF extension, and memos stored
in a .DBT file, are common file formats accepted and created by
many different database programs. If you have a sample dBASE
file appropriate to this category, Commence can help create your
template definition by reading the field names and the size of the
field. Commence can also do type checking on the field mapping
you define.
Note: To import a dBASE-format file that contains memo fields,
your target category must have fields that are capable of
containing the data in the dBASE file, namely text fields with a
size of Large (which holds up to 30,000 characters).
dBASE, no sample file
dBASE IV files ending with a .DBF extension, and memos stored
in a .DBT file, are common file formats accepted and created by
many different database programs.
DIF, sample file
Data Interchange Format (DIF) is a file format used primarily by
spreadsheet applications. If you have a sample DIF file
appropriate to this category, Commence can help create your
template definition by telling you how many fields are imported or
exported.
DIF, no sample file
Data Interchange Format (DIF) is a file format used primarily by
spreadsheet applications.
OLE DB/ODBC data source
Many applications support ODBC including Microsoft Access,
Oracle, and SQL.
(Edit) Authorization Defaults Dialog Box
This dialog box is displayed when you choose the Default
Permissions... button on the Database Options dialog box. This
screen lets you set the default permissions for one or more
categories in the shared database. The default permissions are
applied to all new users when they enroll to the shared database.
A similar dialog box is displayed by pressing the Edit
Permissions... button on the Permissions dialog box. From this
screen, you can override the default permissions for any category
and choose the level of access to a category for each user
individually. The Edit Authorizations dialog box will have an
additional option of (Default) in every drop-down list to accept the
default permissions.
USER(S)
The names of the user(s) whose permissions are being modified
will be displayed here. This is only shown on the Edit
Authorizations dialog box.
DATABASE:
The name of the active shared database will be displayed here.
DEFAULT USER LEVEL
Select the default user level for the active shared database. The
choices are Author, User, or None.
CATEGORIES AND DESKTOPS
Select one or more categories or desktops from the list.
Categories are listed as Type C and desktops are listed as Type
D. The default permissions will be shown to the right of the
category name. To change the default permissions for all
categories and desktops at once, press Select All.
Note: The only permission that needs to be set for desktops
is the Read permission. When setting permissions, if the
selection includes one or more desktops, only the Read
permission may be set. For full control over all applicable
permissions, it may be helpful to sort by the Type column to
group similar objects together.
READ PERMISSION
To change the default read permission for the selected
categories or desktops, select All, Selective, or None from
the list choices.
For a category, if the read permission is set to None, the
user(s) will not receive any shared items for the selected
category. In this case, it is not necessary to set the write and
delete permissions.
WRITE PERMISSION (for CATEGORIES ONLY)
To change the default write permission for the selected
categories, select All, Selective, or None from the list
choices.
DELETE PERMISSION (for CATEGORIES ONLY)
To change the default delete permission for the selected
categories, select All, Selective, or None from the list
choices.
SAVE
Select Save to accept any changes you have made on this
screen.
CANCEL
Select Cancel to exit without saving your changes.
Assign Users to Commence Items Dialog Box
This dialog box is displayed when you choose the Assign
Users... button on the Database Options dialog box. Selective
Permissions lets you decide which users will have access to the
items in a shared category, simply by connecting the user to the
item. If the user is not connected, they will not be able to read,
write, or delete that item.
This screen lets you select the “Person” category in your
database containing the names of all Commence users in your
workgroup. This category may be called Person, as in the Main
Database, or you may have your own custom category, such as
Employees, where only the names of Commence users are
entered.
Note: You must enter the names of all your users (Commence
clients) into your Person category before you can complete
this dialog box.
USERS
The login IDs (as they were entered in ComAdmin) of all users of
the shared database will be listed on the left side of the screen.
Select one user name at a time from the list.
PERSON ITEMS
The names of all your Commence users (as they were entered in
the Commence Person category) will be listed on the right side
of the screen. Select the one item name from the list that
matches the login ID of the user selected on the left side of the
screen.
ASSIGN PERSON
Press this button to match up the selected items on the left and
right sides of the screen. The word (none) to the right of the
login ID will be replaced by the item name of the Commence
user you selected.
UNASSIGN PERSON
Press this button to undo your selection. The word (none) will
appear to the right of the login ID.
OK
Select OK to accept any changes you have made on this screen.
CANCEL
Select Cancel to return to the Database Options dialog box
without saving your changes.
Select Connections for Selective Permissions Dialog
Box
This dialog box is displayed when you choose the Select
Connections... button on the Database Options dialog box.
Selective Permissions lets you decide which users will have
access to the items in a shared category, simply by connecting
the user to the item. If the user is not connected, they will not be
able to read, write, or delete that item.
If you have not done so already, you must first assign users to
items in your Person category. Then on this screen, you must
choose which connection to the Person category should be used
to determine selective permissions. This is useful if you have
more than one connection to the Person category, for example
“Appointment Coordinated by Person” can be used to allow only
the person who added the Appointment item to have write and
delete permissions to the item. “Appointment With Person” can be
used to allow only the attendees (i.e., any user whose name
appears in this connection) to have read permissions to the
appointment item.
CATEGORY:
Select a shared category in the active shared database. This
category will be configured to use selective permissions.
CONNECTION FOR READ PERMISSIONS:
Select a connection to the “Person” category. Only those users
who are connected to the item via this connection will receive
the item.
CONNECTION FOR WRITE PERMISSIONS:
Select a connection to the “Person” category. Only those users
who are connected to the item via this connection will be able to
edit the item.
CONNECTION FOR DELETE PERMISSIONS:
Select a connection to the “Person” category. Only those users
who are connected to the item via this connection will be able to
delete the item.
OK
Select OK to accept any changes you have made on this screen.
CANCEL
Select Cancel to return to the Database Options dialog box
without saving your changes.
Workgroup Communications Dialog Box
This dialog is displayed when you choose the Configure button
(in the Server Communications group box) on the
Communications dialog box.
SYNCHRONIZE WITH CLIENTS VIA...
Select a method of synchronization from the list. The default
method of syncing with clients is via a shared LAN directory.
All three methods can be configured at the server. This
offers each client the most flexibility in choosing a
convenient method of synchronization. For example, on-site
users who are typically connected to a network may choose
the shared LAN method, while remote users may prefer
FTP.
EDIT
After selecting a method of synchronization, press the Edit
button to enter the communication parameters. The Server
Communication dialog box will be displayed.
CLEAR
If all your clients will be using the same method of
synchronization, and you want to ensure that no users
attempt to sync via any other method, then select the
method of syncing you no longer want to use and press the
Clear button to clear the parameters for that option.
OK
Select OK to accept any changes you have made on this
screen. Click Next to continue.
CANCEL
Select Cancel to return to the Communications dialog box
without saving your changes. Note you will be allowed to
continue only after the communications setup has been
filled out properly.
 
Registered User Dialog Box
This dialog box is displayed when you choose the Details button
from the Register dialog box.
Use this command to get more information about the registered
user you’ve selected. The user’s Full Name, Login ID, and
Authorization Key are displayed. Other options on this screen
include:
CREATE INSTRUCTIONS
Use this command to generate an instruction sheet for the
registered user you’ve selected. The instruction sheet will
include the user’s information displayed above, and any
additional information the user will need to know to enroll to the
shared database. For more information about how to customize
the instruction sheet, see Registering Clients.
RE-REGISTER
Use this command to resend initialization information to the
selected user. This must be done if an error occurs during the
enroll process.
RESYNC
Use this command to resend database information to the user.
OK
Press the OK button to close this dialog box and return to the
Register dialog box.
Type a Correction or Select a Suggestion Dialog Box
This dialog box, displayed when you select the Suggestions
button from the Spell Checker, allows you to correct a misspelled
word found by the Tools-Spell Check command by either:
 
-- editing the text in the Correction text box, or
-- selecting an alternative spelling for the misspelled from the
Suggestions list box. If you choose a suggested spelling, it
appears in the Correction text box.
 
Once you are satisfied with the text in the Correction text box,
select one of the buttons explained below to tell the spell checker
how to perform the substitution of the new spelling for the
incorrect one.
CORRECTION TEXT BOX
This text box originally contains the misspelled word found by the
spell checker. Change its contents using the mouse and
keyboard, or by selecting one of the spell checker's suggestions
from the Suggestions list box. Use one of the buttons on the
right side of the dialog box to use the contents of the Correction
text box to replace the misspelled word or return to the Spell
Checker without correcting the word.
SUGGESTIONS LIST BOX
Lists suggested spellings for the misspelled word. Selecting one
of these suggestions places it in the Corrections text box.
HERE
Substitute the contents of the Correction box for the currently
highlighted occurrence of the word within the field being checked
by selecting the Here button from the Apply box. Commence
then highlights the next misspelled word in the field (if any). (The
currently highlighted occurrence is the one that was highlighted
when you selected the Suggestions button from the Spell
Checker dialog box.)
EVERYWHERE
Substitute the contents of the Correction box for all occurrences
of the word within the field being checked by selecting the
Everywhere button from the Apply box.
CANCEL
To leave the misspelled word unchanged, select the Cancel
button. Commence returns you to the Spell Checker dialog box,
with the highlight still on the original misspelled word.
Find Time Dialog Box
This dialog box is displayed when you select the Find Time
button on the Schedule Meeting dialog box. The purpose of this
dialog box is to let you choose a meeting date, start time, and end
time for the Participants you selected in the Schedule Meeting
dialog box.
 
Time slots are color coded so that you can find free time at a
glance. White space indicates a time when all selected
participants are free. Red indicates a time when at least one
required Participant is busy. Blue indicates a time when none of
the required participants are busy but at least one Optional
Participant is busy.
 
The Find Time dialog box contains these elements:
HRS/MINS
The value in these boxes determines the size of the free time slot
you want Commence to find. When you first enter the Find Time
dialog box, these boxes display the meeting duration. If you set a
Start Time and End Time in the Schedule Meeting dialog box,
Commence calculates your meeting duration and displays the
value here. If no End Time is entered, this value defaults to 30
minutes. Use these boxes to change the duration of the meeting.
DATE
When you first enter the Find Time dialog box, this text box
displays either today's date or the Date specified on the
Schedule Meeting dialog box. Either type a new date in the text
box or use the Time/Date Grid (see below) to select a date.
START TIME
When you first enter the Find Time dialog box, this text box
displays either the current time or the Start Time specified on the
Schedule Meeting dialog box. Either type a new start time in the
text box or use the Time/Date Grid (see below) to select a start
time.
END TIME
When you first enter the Find Time dialog box, this text box
displays either the End Time specified on the Schedule Meeting
dialog box or, if no End Time was specified, 30 minutes after the
Start Time. If you change the Hrs/Mins value (indicating meeting
duration), Commence recalculates the End Time.
SKIP BY DAY
Select one of these buttons to move the Time/Date Grid
backward or forward by one day.
SKIP BY SCREEN
Select one of these buttons to move the Time/Date Grid
backward or forward by 14 days.
LEGEND
Shows the colors or shades used to identify which time slots are
free for all selected participants (None), busy for at least one
Required participant (Required), and busy for at least one
Optional participant (Optional).
TIME/DATE GRID
Displays 24 time slots for each day in a 14 day period. When this
box first appears, the day displayed on the left of the grid is either
today or the day selected as the Date for the appointment on the
Schedule Meeting dialog box.
 
Click the mouse button over a single time slot. The Date, Start
Time, and End Time boxes change to reflect your selection.
OK
Select this button to confirm your choices for Date, Start Time,
and End Time and return to the Schedule Meeting dialog box.
CANCEL
Select this button to cancel the currently specified Start Time,
End Time, and Date and return to the Schedule Meeting dialog
box without making any changes.
Resources Dialog Box
This dialog box is displayed when you select the Resources
button on the Schedule Meeting dialog box. The purpose of this
dialog box is to connect the appointment you are scheduling to
items in your Resource category. Connecting resources serves as
a reminder to the coordinator and attendees that particular
resources are required for the meeting and must be reserved in
whatever way is appropriate for your organization.
The Name, Located in and Date/Time text is copied from the
information in the Schedule Meeting dialog box to identify the
meeting.
The Needed and Not Needed list boxes in the middle of the
dialog box, and the buttons between them, are used to build a list
of required resources for the meeting.
To add a resource to the meeting, highlight one or more
resources from the Not Needed list and select the Add button. To
remove resources from the meeting, highlight them in the Needed
list box and select the Remove button.
To highlight multiple individual items in either of the list boxes,
press and hold down the Ctrl key as you click the mouse button
over them. To highlight a range of items, highlight the first item in
the range, move the list box using the scroll bars (if necessary) to
display the item at the other end of the range, then press and
hold down the Shift key as you click the mouse button over the
last item in the range.
Select the Save button to confirm your choices. Select Cancel to
return to the Schedule Meeting dialog box without choosing
resources.
Add/Edit Remote Database Dialog Box
These dialog boxes are displayed when you select the Add or
Edit buttons on the Sync Out dialog box. The Edit Remote
Database dialog box looks the same as the Add Remote
Database dialog box, except that the name of the database you
are editing appears in the Database Name text box.
 
This dialog box lets you give a descriptive name to the remote
database you are defining. The database name you specify must
be unique within the currently defined list of names and must
have 60 characters or less. It is advisable to use a database
name that identifies both the database and the PC on which it
resides. For example, if you want to use a database named
"Sales Contacts" as a base database and have a remote
database on your home PC, then enter a name like "Sales
Contacts on Home PC". This will help you to remember which
database you currently have open and the PC for which you are
creating an update file.
 
Enter or edit the database name and select OK to confirm your
changes. Select Cancel to return to the Sync Out dialog box
without making any changes.
Creating the Initial Update File
With the Sync Out dialog box displayed, follow the procedure
below:
1. The names of your remote databases are listed in the
Synchronize with Database drop-down list box. In the Sync
Filename text box, type in (or use the Select button to
choose) a filename to hold the updates for the selected
remote database, that is, the one displayed in the
Synchronize with Database drop-down list box.
If you have more than one remote database, you must select a
different filename for each update file. Choose an update file
name that identifies the remote database. You may be
synchronizing different remote databases at different times,
so the update files can contain different information for each
remote database. Commence writes the remote database
name in the update file and will not let you update a remote
database using the wrong update file.
2. Select the OK button to create the update file. The Sync Out
message box is displayed to show you the status of the
update.
When the Sync Out is finished, the button changes to OK.
Select OK on the message box.
3. Clearly label the diskette with the name of the PC and the
remote database for which the update file was created. (The
update file may be created on any drive; it often created on a
diskette or copied to a diskette to move to a remote PC.)
Now, you are ready to initialize the remote database(s). Select
the Browse >> button to see how to do this.
Running Multiple Commence Servers on One PC
See Also
It is frequently convenient to have more than one copy of
Commence running on a single PC. The following command line
options can be used to control Commence's behavior when it first
starts.
/Database
The /Database option controls the database that Commence
opens initially. For example,
"c:\program files\commence\commence.exe" /Database=Contact
Manager
will start Commence with the Main Database and
"c:\program files\commence\commence.exe"
/Database=c:\commence\data
will start Commence with the database that is in the
c:\commence\data directory.
/Prompt
The /Prompt option causes Commence to display a list of
available database when it starts. You can then select the
database to open. For example:
"c:\program files\commence\commence.exe" /Prompt
/Quiet
If Commence is already running you will get a warning message if
you try to start another copy of Commence. Adding the /Quiet
option will suppress this warning message. For example:
"c:\program files\commence\commence.exe" /Quiet
Note: Each server MUST use a separate shared LAN or FTP
directory. Open each database and use the Customize-
Workgroup command, then press Next >> until you get to
the Communications screen. Make sure you have selected a
different shared LAN or FTP directory for each server
database. Also, if you are synchronizing via e-mail, each
server can use the same e-mail ID.
Tip For each copy of Commence you start, you will get a message
telling you that Commence is already running and asking
you if you want to start another copy. You can bypass this
message by using the /Quiet command line option. To use
it, just append “/Quiet” to the end of the command line.
Setting Server Communications
Three sets of Server Communications Settings are provided (e.g.
one for each of the available synchronizing methods.) Depending
upon the method(s) used for synchronizing updates, one or more
of these sets of parameters must be configured. The methods
available for synchronizing data are:
Shared LAN Directory - used when a Local Area Network
(LAN) is used by the Workgroup; a directory which is
accessible by both the Server and Clients is used to
distribute updated information
E–Mail - uses electronic mail to send and receive updated
information for the Shared Database
Internet FTP Directory - uses File Transfer Protocol
(FTP) commands to send and receive updated information
for the Shared Database over the Internet.
It is not necessary for all three sets of parameters to be defined if
only one or two of the methods are going to be used for the
synchronizing of data. For example, if there is no Internet
connection available to the Server, then synchronizing via the
Internet will not be possible, and those parameters may remain
undefined.
 
Perform the following procedure to define Server
Communications Settings:
1. From the Customize Workgroup - Database Options Dialog
Box, click on Next > two times. The Customize Workgroup -
Communications Dialog Box will be displayed.
2. In the Server Communications section, click Configure. The
Workgroup Communications Dialog Box will be displayed.
3. Highlight each method that will be used, in turn, and set the
parameters:
E-Mail
Internet FTP Directory
Shared LAN Directory
Setting Client Communications
Once the Commence Server has been configured properly for
each of the methods to be used for synchronizing, each Client
must in turn be configured as well. Unlike the Commence Server,
which is capable of being configured for all three means of
synchronizing (i.e. Shared LAN Directory, e–mail, and/or the
Internet), a Commence Client is capable of using only one
method of synchronizing at any given time. The choice of
synchronizing method may be changed at any point by
reconfiguring the Client Communications Parameters, but a Client
cannot be configured for multiple methods of synchronizing
simultaneously.
 
Perform the following procedure to define Client Communications
Settings:
1. From the Customize Workgroup - Database Options Dialog
Box, click on Next > two times. The Customize Workgroup -
Communications Dialog Box is displayed.
2. From the Customize Workgroup - Communications Dialog
Box, highlight a user name in the Client Communications
box and select Configure.
3. Select the method that the client will use to synchronize.
Note, only one method can be used per client.
E-Mail
Internet FTP Directory
Shared LAN Directory
Repeat for each client.
Users’ communications parameters may need to be updated if the
methods available for syncing change. For example, if a user
usually works in the office, they would typically be connected via
a shared LAN directory. If they begin using Commence on a
laptop from outside of the office, e–mail communication would
allow the Client Database to communicate with the Server even
when away from the office. The server will then need to be
reconfigured so that it sends (and receives) synchronization data
from the Client via e–mail. Additionally, the user will need to
configure the Client Database to communicate via e–mail.
Add/Edit Field Dialog Box
When you are adding, editing, or copying a category (see
Category Definition dialog box), the Add and Edit buttons on this
dialog box allow you to add new fields or modify existing fields in
the selected category.
FIELD LABEL TEXT BOX
When adding a field, this text box is blank. Type a field label into
the box that is indicative of the field's purpose. You must label a
field in order to save its definition using the OK button.
 
When editing a field, this box is filled in with the current field
label. You can change the label by editing the box's contents and
selecting the OK button.
 
Field labels are limited to 20 characters.
FIELD TYPE LIST BOX
This list box shows Commence's field types (the name field type
is omitted unless you are editing the name field).
 
When adding a field, Commence highlights the Text field type by
default. Change the type by highlighting another in the list.
 
When editing a newly added field, the current field type is
highlighted and can be changed by moving the highlight to
another field type in the list. This can be done until the field is
saved.
 
When editing a previously defined (saved) field in an existing
category, the field's type is highlighted but it cannot be changed;
this also applies to the name type field of any category.
 
Note: If you have not yet entered any data into a field and you
want to change the field type, you can delete the field and add a
new field with a different field type. If you have entered data and
want to transfer this existing data to a new field with the type you
want, use the File-Export-By Category command to export the
data values from the old field, define a new field with the type
you want, and then use File-Import to import the data values.
 
FIELD OPTIONS
The options displayed on the right side of the dialog box depend
on the field's type, so the right hand side of the Add or Edit
Field dialog box changes when you select different field types in
the Field Type list box.
 
Since each field type has special capabilities and different
definition options, consider how you will use a field before you
add or edit it.
 
The following is a list of all the available field types. To see a
definition of the capabilities of the field, select the field type
name below. To see a description of the field's options as they
appear in this dialog box, as well as an explanation of valid field
values, select the word Options next to the field type whose
options you want to see.
 
Calculation Options
Check Box Options
Data File Options
Date Options
E-mail Address Options
Excel Cell Options
Image Options
Internet Address (URL) Options
Number Options
Selection Options
Sequence Number Options
Telephone Options
Text Options
Time Options
Editing a Radio Group
There are two considerations you should bear in mind when
editing radio button definitions using the Edit Field dialog box.
 
First, you cannot delete a radio button. For example, once you
save a definition that uses five radio buttons for the Status field,
you can't go back to using only three buttons. The fourth and fifth
will have to be labeled Unused or some such name if you no
longer want to use those labels. Each button will continue to be
counted towards the total number of fields and connections used
for your category.
 
Second, while you can change the radio button's label at any
time, it is easiest to do so before you enter any data into the
category. This is because data you enter is associated with the
radio button number, not its label.
 
For example, if you have data associated with a radio button
labeled Meeting and change the label to Phone Call, any items
in the category that previously had the Meeting button turned on
will now have the Phone Call button turned on.
Icon Modification Action
The Icon Modification action draws attention to a button on the
view bar to alert you to open the view.
 
Use the View Name drop-down list box to select a view from
among the views you currently have on the view bar. View bar
buttons have two appearances: normal and alert. The alert
appearance can be used as a reminder that the view should be
examined. The icon modification action can optionally sound an
audible alarm to further alert you.
 
The icon modification action does not use values from trigger or
condition item(s).
 
An agent using a timer trigger and an icon modification action is
an easy way to periodically remind yourself to check important
data.
Item Count Condition
An Item Count condition tests whether or not there are a
specified number of data items in a category, a filtered category
or a named view. The condition can be defined to look for an
exact match to the condition count or for an item count less than
or greater than the condition count.
Select either View or Category under For Items In... to specify
the items that are checked by the condition.
If you select View, a drop-down list box appears that lets you
select one of your current views. Note that selecting a view
determines the category of the view.
If you select Category, a drop-down list box appears that lets you
select one of your categories. If you want to limit the condition
test to a sub-set of the category, use the Select Filter button to
bring up the Filters tab. Now you can define a filter for that
category.
An Item Count can be defined to look for an exact match to the
item count or a connection count less than or greater than the
condition count. Use the radio group controls under Trigger If...
to set the style of comparison. Enter the condition count in the
text box to the left of items are visible.
An Item Count condition passes all the items in its test view or
category as condition items.
Item Count conditions are useful for passing several data items
at once to an action. Remember, if you want to design an agent
that processes many items at once you should use an Item
Count condition or a Field Summary condition since triggers
only pass a single item as trigger data.
Tools Menu Commands
This menu provides access to various utilities provided by
Commence.
Outlook Sync Synchronizes data with Microsoft Outlook.
Spell Check Check the spelling of words in a text field; either the
blinking cursor or some highlighted text must be present
in the field to be checked.
Schedule Meeting Find an available meeting time for a group of
participants and then add an appointment item to the
group calendar.
Field Summary Display a summary of all the calculation and
number fields selected for summary reporting (if any) in
the active view. This command is available only if the
active view is a report.
Sync Link In Read an update file for a particular database.
 
Sync Link Out Manage remote databases in the sync link group.
 
Import Import data from an external file into a category in the
open database.
Export-By View Export data from a category in the open
database to an external file; only those items appearing in
the active view are exported.
Export-By Category Export all data from a category in the open
database to an external file.
Transfer In Read data definitions from a transfer file into the
open database.
Transfer Out Copy data definitions into a transfer file from the
open database.
 
Save Item Trigger
The Save Item trigger is fired when an item is saved in
Commence. The specification of the trigger consists of identifying
which items activate the trigger when they are saved. All Save
Item triggers work with a single category (e.g., Account, Activity,
etc.). The Save Item trigger passes data from the item that was
saved (trigger data).
 
You can specify that the trigger fires when an item is added for
the first time, when an item is edited or both. Select these options
using the added and edited check boxes to the right of An item
is in the Trigger If group box.
 
You can set a Save Item trigger for items in a view or in a
category. Make this selection by picking one of the View or
Category radio buttons under Trigger If.
 
If you select View, a drop-down list box appears that lets you
select one of your current views. Note that selecting a view
determines the category of the view.
 
If you select Category, a drop-down list box appears that lets you
select one of your categories. You can just set the alarm for any
item in the category, or you can restrict it to a filtered sub-set of
the category. Press the Select Filter button to bring up the Filters
tab and define a filter for that category.
 
Pressing the Options button allows you to qualify the type of
Save Item that has occurred. An add/edit item can occur in a
number of ways. The table below summarizes the various options
available for modifying items in Commence.
 
Option Item was added/edited via…
Detail Form -an item detail in which information was manually
entered by a Commence user
Agent -an agent using an Add Item or Edit Item action
Transfer In -Transfer In from a Commence *.TFR file
Import -Import (or the Import action in an agent) from an ASCII,
dBASE, DIF, or ODBC file
DDE -an external program or a Commence agent using
Dynamic Data Exchange
Sync Link -Sync Link In (or the Sync Link In action in an agent)
from a remote database
Workgroup -Workgroup Sync or automatic synchronization
(only available in a shared database)
Internet/Intranet -an Allure-enabled web site
Pilot Link -HotSync with a Palm handheld device
Other programs -an external add-on using the Commence
Database API or from a Commence detail form
using the Database API in a VBScript
Outlook Sync -synchronization with Microsoft Outlook
By default, the Save Item trigger assumes that the agent will fire
only if an item was manually added/edited via a detail form. Every
one of these options, including the default detail form option, can
be turned on or off independently, allowing you to custom select
the types of save to suit your needs. For example, by turning off
the agent option, you can be sure that an agent will never trigger
itself.
 
The following steps describe how to access the options in the
Save Item trigger.
1. Select Customize-Database-Agent.
2. Select Add to create a new agent.
3. Press the T button in the If this Trigger occurs: section.
This will display the Add Trigger dialog.
4. Select Save Item from the list of Trigger types.
5. Select a view or category from either combo box.
6. Press the Options… button. The Updates screen will appear.
Check as many options as needed. The agent will run
whenever an item is added or edited via the selected options.
For example, if the “Other program” option is selected, the
agent will run when an Automation client adds an item to the
selected view or category.
7. Complete the remaining selections for the agent (e.g. define
any conditions and actions as needed).
8. Save the agent.
 
Note: The options also work with existing agents that already use
the Save Item trigger. Simply edit the agent and modify the
trigger. Then press the Options button and check the appropriate
boxes as needed.
Sync Link Concepts
See Also
If you are using several PCs and are not on a LAN, you can still
maintain identical databases by using the Sync Link feature.
Sync Link provides you with the benefits of information sharing
without using a network to interconnect your PCs. Using Sync
Link, you can compress a Commence database into a single file,
and read that file into a copy of Commence running on another,
unconnected computer. Thereafter, you can keep the two copies
of the Commence database identical by sync linking the
database changes from one PC to the other as you move
between them.
 
The text under the heading Establishing a Sync link Group
explains the general procedure for defining and maintaining a set
of two or more identical databases. The section Using Sync Link
details procedures for using the sync link group and for adding,
editing, and deleting remote databases.
Establishing a Sync link Group
A sync link group consists of two or more Commence databases,
usually located on different, unconnected computers.
Establishing a sync link group allows you to exchange files
between the databases in the sync link group and keep the
contents of the databases identical, or synchronized, as work
and travel take you from computer to computer. The following
diagram depicts a typical sync link group scenario.
 
image/synclink_shg.gif
 
Using Sync Link to update databases is much more efficient than
backing up and restoring entire databases. After the initial
synchronization (which occurs during the establishment of the
sync link group), only the changes are sync linked from PC to
PC.
Steps to Establish a Sync link Group
Before you can use Sync Link to synchronize databases, you
must define your sync link group, create the remote databases
on your remote PCs, and then populate the remote databases
with the information in the base database. The general
procedure is as follows:
1. Open the database that you want to sync link between
computers. This database will be the base database for the
sync link group.
2. With the base database open, define one or more remote
databases for the sync link group.
3. Create an initial update file for each of the remote databases.
This is called a "complete update" since it consists of all
definitions and data in your base database.
4. For each remote, add the new remote database on the
remote PC and read in the update file you created. This
makes the base and the remote databases identical.
 
 
 
 
 
Defining the Sync link Group
Before beginning, decide which PC you want to use as the base.
Select whichever PC you use most frequently. If the data is not
already on the base PC, copy the data directory to the base PC
and use File-Open/Manage Database to add that database to
Commence. Then follow these steps:
margin-bottom: 6.00pt;
text-indent: -18.00pt;">1. Start Commence on the PC you
want to use as your base, and open the database that you want
to sync link. This database will be your base database.
If this database is not opened automatically when you start
Commence, use the Open/Manage Database
command to open it.
2. Select the Tools-Sync Link Out command. The Sync Out
dialog box
is displayed. It should be empty. This dialog box lets you define
the remote PCs that will be in your sync link group. There should
not be any databases defined in the Synchronize with Database
list box. If there are, you should follow the steps in the section
Adding Another Remote Database in the description of the Sync
Out dialog box.
3. Select the Add
button from the Define Remote Database
group box. The Add Remote Database
dialog box is displayed. This dialog box lets you give a descriptive
name to the remote database you are defining. The database
name you specify must be unique within the currently defined list
of names and must have 60 characters or less.
It is advisable to use a database
name that identifies both the database and the PC on which it
resides.
For example, if you want to use a database named "Sales
Contacts"
as a base database and have a remote database on your home
PC, then enter a name like "Sales Contacts on Home PC". This
will help you
to remember which database you currently have open and the PC
for which you are creating an update file. Select OK.
A dialog box will appear while Commence initializes the database
in preparation for the first sync link out. As soon as the
initialization is finished, you will be returned to the Sync Out
dialog box.
4. The Sync Type should be set to Incremental. Because
you have added a new remote database, Commence will
automatically detect that an initial sync file containing the
entire database must be created. Do not
select OK yet.
Now, you are ready to create the initial update file.
Select the Browse >> button to see how to do this.
 
 
 
 
Initializing Remote Databases
Perform the following procedure for all of the remote databases in
the sync link group:
1. Open Commence on the PC on which the remote database is
to be located.
2. Create a new, empty database by using the Open/Manage
Database command and selecting the Add button. You can
give this database any name you wish, but to prevent
confusion, you may want to use the same name that you
gave to the base database when you defined the sync link
group.
3. Insert the update diskette intended for this remote database
into your diskette drive. (The update file may exist on any
drive, but is commonly on a diskette.)
4. Select the Sync Link In command. The Select File dialog box
is displayed.
5. Select the name of the update file in the Files list box that
matches the one you selected for this database's Sync
Filename in step 1 of the procedure for creating the initial
update files above. Then select OK.
6. The Sync In message box is displayed to show you the
status of the update.
When the Sync In finishes, the button changes to OK. Select
OK on the message box.
Now, you are ready to use the sync link group you've established.
Select the Browse >> button to see how to do this.
Using the Sync link Group
See Also
This section describes a common scenario for using your sync
link group. It assumes that you have a base database called
Sales and two remote databases: "Sales on Home PC" and
"Sales on Laptop". This scenario also assumes that you have
followed the steps in the section Sync Link Concepts and have
initialized both of your remote databases.
1. You are using your base database and are about to leave for
a business trip. Just before closing Commence, you select
Sync Link Out to create an incremental update of your
database to take with you on your laptop.
2. Select the database named "Sales on Laptop" from the
Synchronize with Database drop-down list box and make
sure that the Sync Type radio button is set to Incremental,
which is the default. Enter a name for the update file in the
Sync Filename text box. If you selected a diskette drive for
the file, make sure that there is a diskette in the diskette
drive. Then select OK.
Commence creates the incremental update file for the Sales
database on your laptop PC. This file only contains the
changes you have made since the last time you
synchronized your Sales on Laptop database.
3. On your laptop PC, start Commence and open the Sales
database.
4. Select Sync Link In and select the filename that contains
your incoming updates. Then select OK.
Commence applies all of the updates from your base database
to your remote database. When the synchronization is
finished, this database is identical to your base database.
If you made any changes to your "Sales on Laptop" database
during your trip, you would perform the same sequence of steps
when you return, this time from your laptop and to your base PC.
After you have re-synchronized with your base PC, you can follow
the same steps to create an update file for your other remote
database, "Sales on Home PC." Then you can take this file home
and synchronize your home PC so that it is identical to the others.
Some important things to remember:
You must perform a Complete update (from the Base
database) the first time you add a new remote database to a
sync link group (see the following section). After you do this,
you need only perform Incremental updates between
databases in the sync link group.
If a diskette containing an update file becomes unreadable,
or you otherwise lose the update file, you will need to go
back to the last PC you used and perform a File-Sync Link
Out with the Sync Type radio button set to Complete. This
file, like the one you first created on the base database, is a
complete copy of the database. After you read it into the
targeted database, you can use an Incremental update file
again.
Update files are targeted for a particular database in a sync
link group. After you establish a sync link group, Commence
will not let you read an update file into the wrong database.
If you cancel a synchronization in progress, you must restart
the synchronization from the same update file if you want to
continue using the open database without any loss of data.
Alternatively, you could go back to the database from which
you created the update file and create a new complete (not
incremental) update file for the open database.
Restore Database Dialog Box
The Restore Database dialog box is displayed while the
database is being restored. If you select the Cancel button to
cancel the backup, nothing is written to the database directory
you specified. Once the backup is complete, select the OK button
from the Restore Database dialog box to return to the
Open/Manage Database dialog box.
 
Restore will read from multiple floppy diskettes if the backup file
spans diskettes. Commence will prompt you for additional
diskettes as needed.
Template Mapping Dialog Box
See Also
After selecting a file format for a template on the Template
Definition dialog box, you must tell Commence which fields in
your target category correspond (or "map") to the fields in the
external file.
 
To define your field mapping, select the Map button on the
Template Definition dialog box. Commence displays the Template
Mapping dialog box, the contents of which depend on whether or
not you are using a sample file, which is an option for dBASE and
DIF file formats.
 
If you are not using a sample file, you must select field names
from your target category in the same order that the fields appear
in the external file.
 
If you are using a sample file, Commence will give you a list of
the external field names so you can match each external name
with the corresponding field in your target category; Commence
then puts the fields in the correct order for you.
 
The following help topics discuss template mapping without and
with sample files.
 
Template Mapping Without Sample Files
Discusses using the three ASCII file formats; the dBASE, no
sample file format; and, the DIF, no sample file format.
 
Template Mapping With Sample Files
Pertains to the dBASE, sample file format; the DIF, sample file
format; and, the OLE DB/ODBC data source format..
 
The process of mapping fields is identical for import and export
with three exceptions.
 
1. A special <Skip Field> entry appears in the Fields Not in
Template list box. This is used as a placeholder when
mapping templates without a sample file. It instructs
Commence to disregard the field data in that position of the
import file. When you are importing data or exporting using a
sample external file, the <Skip Field> does the same
function as the Unassign Field button; it clears the selected
field mapping causing Commence to skip over that field
during the import/export.
2. There is no need to set Import Rules when a template will
only be used for exporting data from a category. Export
Rules apply only to OLE DB/ODBC exports.
3. Exporting a sequence number or calculation field may be
useful, but these values cannot be imported since those field
values are automatically generated and/or computed by
Commence.
Setting up Windows Telephony
See Also
Select the Edit-Preferences-Dialer command to tell Commence
what type of dialer you are using. If you select Windows
Telephony as the dialing device, you can specify default dialer
settings for a modem connected to your PC. This will enable
Commence to autodial a phone number from your PC.
 
1. Select the Control Panel button on the Dialer preferences
tab. This will run the Telephony applet in Windows Control
Panel.
2. The Telephony Properties dialog box will be displayed.
Select the appropriate telephony driver for your modem. You
will typically be given a choice of “Unimodem Service
Provider”. If the manufacturer of your dialing device has
supplied you with a TAPI Service provider (*.TSP) file, it will
be listed here after it has been properly installed.
3. Select the Setup button, then press Properties. The
Modems Properties dialog box will be displayed. Select the
correct communications Port and Speed for your modem and
configuration. Click OK.
4. Select the Dialing Properties button. Select “Default
Location” or press New to add a new location.
Enter your local area code (e.g. “908” for central New
Jersey). Select the Country you are dialing from (e.g. “United
States of America (1)” if you are in the United States).
If dialing through a PBX enter the appropriate prefixes to
access an outside line. Typically, you would enter “9,” for
both local and long distance calls. Otherwise, leave both
boxes blank. Click OK to save the dialing properties for this
location.
5. Click Close to save the data.
 
Note: You will have to close Commence and restart it for Control
Panel changes to take effect.
 
Once you have setup your dialing device you can use the Tools-
Dial Phone command or the Dial button on the Home menu to
dial any phone number displayed in the active view or item detail:
 
Note: Windows Telephony (TAPI) is a built-in component of
Microsoft Windows.
Configuring E-Mail Client
This dialog box is displayed when you select the Edit-
Preferences-Other Apps command.
 
This preference allows you to configure your Commence
database to receive e-mail.
 
The Commence agent trigger Incoming Communication: E-Mail
can detect when you receive new E-Mail. It can then perform
actions on the e-mail such as deleting the mail message, marking
it as Read, or do any number of other agent actions such as
adding the e-mail message as a new item in Commence.
 
Before Commence can detect that you have new e-mail, you
must first configure Commence as an E-Mail client. To do so:
1. From the Configure Commence as: combo box, select E-
Mail client.
2. From the Mail Program: combo box, select the appropriate
protocol for your E-Mail application. For example, if you use
Lotus cc:Mail, select Lotus cc:Mail or VIM compatible (16-
bit). If you use MS Outlook or another MAPI application such
as Eudora, select MS Outlook or MAPI-compatible (32-bit).
3. If you selected VIM compatible, you must also fill in the
location of your Post Office Path. For Lotus Notes, enter the
directory where VIM.DLL or VIM32.DLL can be found.
4. Enter your e-mail User Name and Password. (If you are
using MS Outlook, enter your Profile Setting instead. The
default is usually ‘Microsoft Outlook’.) . Re-enter the
password in the Verify Password box.
5. When completed, select Done to save the configuration.
Configuring Mobile Link and Microsoft Outlook
See Also
Commence can synchronize data with supported handheld
devices and also with Microsoft Outlook.
 
Use the Preference screens in Commence to match each field in
Commence with the corresponding field in the other application.
These field mappings will be used during the synchronizations to
ensure that the correct data is stored in the appropriate field in
each application. Only those fields that will be used need to be
mapped.
 
Refer to the following topics for detailed steps:
Mapping fields for Mobile Link and Microsoft Outlook Calendar
Mapping fields for Mobile Link and Microsoft Outlook Contacts
Mapping fields for Mobile Link and Microsoft Outlook Tasks
Mapping fields for Mobile Link Notes
 
MOBILE LINK SYNCHRONIZATION
You can begin to use Mobile Link once you have completed the
mappings for your Commence database. For more details about
how to use Mobile Link, please see the Mobile Link Guide
(mobilelink.pdf) provided with your downloaded software.
 
OUTLOOK SYNCHRONIZATION
Step 1
To use the Outlook synchronization feature, Outlook must be
installed on the same PC as Commence RM. Outlook should be
setup using either of the following options:
Corporate Workgroup: provides full MAPI compliance
Internet Mail Only: provides services to connect to an Internet
Service Provider (ISP).
To determine which setup option is installed, startup Outlook then
select Help -> About Microsoft Outlook.
 
Step 2
Commence must also be configured as a MAPI 32-bit E-Mail
client. This will enable Commence to access Outlook without
prompting for your login information each time a synchronization
with Outlook is initiated. Refer to Configuring E-Mail for detailed
setup information.
 
Step 3
Before your first synchronization with Outlook, please backup
your data both in Commence and in Microsoft Outlook. Refer to
the Microsoft Office Integration Guide (msoffice.pdf) for more
information about how to prepare your database for
synchronization with Outlook.

This dialog box is displayed when you select the Open button on
the Open/Manage Database dialog box if your Administrator has
required a Login Password for the shared database you are
opening.
LOGIN ID
Type in the Login ID you were assigned by the Commence
Administrator.
LOGIN PASSWORD
Type in your Login Password. The characters you type for your
password are echoed to the screen as a series of asterisks to
maintain the privacy of your password.
Note: Only the Commence Administrator can change your Login
Password.
SAVE LOGIN ID ON DISK
The Save Login ID on disk check box tells Commence to
remember your Login ID and automatically supply it whenever
you open a shared database. If you turn on this check box, the
next time you open this shared database, Commence will
display your Login ID in the edit box and you will have to type in
only your Login Password.
OK
Select OK to open the shared database.
Using Field Codes in Templates
See Also
By using Field Codes, you can insert values from a data item into
your letter. For example, if you have chosen to associate a
template with the Person/Company category, you can use values
from any direct field or connected field in the Person/Company
category in the template. When the template is used to send a
letter to a specific person, the Field Codes are replaced with the
actual values from the person's data item.
 
The format for field codes depends on the word processor that is
configured.
WORD 2000/XP
Field Codes use Word formatting and field code formats for
additional functionality. The Microsoft Office Integration Guide
(msoffice.pdf) includes more details on the use of Field Codes in
Word 2000 and XP.
WordPerfect
Field Codes are simply the name of the field enclosed in
parentheses and percent characters: (%Field Name%). For
example, to insert the name of the item in the letter, the field
variable (%Name%) would be used in the template. Data from
connected fields can also be represented with Field Codes. The
general format for a connected field is :
 
(%Connection%%Connected Category%%Field Name%)
 
For example, in a template for the Person/Company category, you
can use the following Field Code: (%Has
%%Notes/History%%Notes%). This represents the notes field in
the Notes/History category.
 
Note: If a formatted template references a connected field and
multiple items are connected to the data item for which a letter is
being written, data from all the connected items is entered into
the letter. Each connected item field is added on a separate line.
For example, if you specify a connection of
(%For%%Opportunity%Name%) in a letter template and this
letter template is used to send a letter to a person that is
connected to two opportunities, then fields from both connected
items will be inserted into the letter, one field per line. The fields
for each connected item appear in the order you specify in the
template, but the order in which the connected items are
displayed is determined by Commence.
Using Field Codes in Actions
See Also
Using Field Codes, you can insert values from the trigger or
condition item into your action specifications.
For example, if you have chosen to use a trigger item from the
Person/Company category in a Status Bar Message action, you
can use values from any field in that category in the action
specification. Thus you could display the name of a Person or
Company item in the status bar message.
The concept here is the same as that used in a letter template.
You define a letter template including Field Codes from a
particular category. When the template is used to create a letter
to a specific person, the Field Codes are replaced with the actual
values from the person's data item. The same kind of substitution
process happens when an agent operates. The agent definition
(in particular the action specifications) determines which
substitutions take place when the agent operates.
You can use a value from the trigger or condition item(s) in any
text field in an action specification. This includes Status Bar
Message text, Message Box text, text field values for Add Item
and Edit Item items, Application Launch command lines and
Send DDE messages. In all cases, inserting a Field Code in the
action specification will cause a field value substitution when the
agent operates.
Field Codes are simply the name of the field enclosed in
parentheses and percent characters: (%Field Name%). For
example, to use the name from an item in the text of a status line
action, the Field Code (%Name%) would be inserted in the text. If
you know the field names available from the trigger or condition
data you have chosen, you can just type in the Field Code.
However, it is easier and probably less error prone to select the
Field Codes from a list. You can display this list by selecting the
Insert Field button on an action specification. The list includes all
the fields that are available for the trigger or condition item being
used for that action.
If you have selected a trigger item from all categories, the Insert
Field button is not shown since Commence doesn't know the
category of the item that will be passed. However, you can still
type in Field Codes manually. If the Field Codes correspond to
fields in the trigger item passed when the agent fires, the value
will be substituted. For example, virtually all categories have a
Name field, so the Field Code (%Name%) usually produces a
useful value.
There are four special Field Codes that can be used with any
category. The (-Date-) Code will generate the current date (that is
the date at the time the agent operates). Similarly, the (-Time-)
Field Code will generate the current time. The (-Category-) Field
Code will insert the name of the category of the trigger or
condition item.
The (-TrigItemName-) Field Code is resolved to the item name of
the trigger item. If there is no trigger item, then the keyword
translates to a zero length string. This keyword is useful when
you want to create an agent that uses the trigger item name and
values of the condition item fields in the action. To do this, select
Condition 1 or Condition 2 in the Using Values From radio
group of the Add/Edit Action dialog box. Then select the Field
Values button to display the Field Values dialog box. You can
use the keyword (-TrigItemName-) in the Value text box along
with field names from the condition category.
For example, you can create an agent that has a Person item as
a trigger, with a condition
to select all overdue To-Dos. The action can delegate all of these
To-Dos to the highlighted person by selecting the Assigned to
Person connection and typing "(-TrigItemName-)" into the Value
text box.
If you are using an Add Item or Edit Item action, then Date fields
can be edited using Field Codes. You can also enter dates like
"Today" or "Next Monday" in a data item date field within an
action. When the agent operates, the appropriate date will be
entered into the fields. You can also use the dates from the
trigger or condition item(s) by inserting Field Codes for the date
field that you want to reference. Finally, you can even combine
date Field Codes with date operations to create new dates. For
example: "5 days from (%StartDate%)" will create a date that is 5
days after the date in the "Start Date" field of the trigger item.
The Agent Bar
The agent tool bar is reserved for displaying user-selected
buttons for tool bar agents. You can add your own buttons to the
tool bar by defining an agent with a Tool Bar Pick trigger; see
Customize-Database-Agent.
Help-Context-Sensitive
Use this command to access Commence's on-line help system for
help on a specific menu command or screen region without going
through the Help-Index command.
 
Context-sensitive help lets both keyboard and mouse users get help
on a specific command or screen region.
 
Context-sensitive help is available:
 
for menu commands and screen regions
for dialog boxes and message boxes
Getting Help on Menu Commands and Screen Regions
If there is no active dialog or message box, keyboard and mouse
users access context-sensitive help as described in the next two
subsections.
MOUSE USERS
If you have a mouse connected to your computer, you can get help
on menu commands and screen regions by:
 
Hover over the command with your mouse
Press SHIFT+F1
 
When you point at a screen object (such as a button on the
command bar) and then click Shift+F1, Commence displays a help
topic that explains the object or command.
 
Exit the help topic by selecting ALT+F4 or File-Close from the help
screen's menu bar.
Getting Help on Dialog and Message Boxes
If a dialog or message box is displayed, press F1 or SHIFT-F1 to
display a help topic from the Commence Help System that
describes the dialog box or message box (including error
messages) in detail. Exit the help topic by selecting ALT+F4 or File-
Close from the help screen's menu bar.
Day Display Mode
The Day display mode provides:
DAY TITLES
Day titles are displayed at the top of the daily calendar just
above the graphical display of time slots. Day titles are
appointments or events that are scheduled for a day but do not
have a start or end time. An example or a day title is
"Anniversary". An arrow immediately to the right of the day title
shows that there are more day titles for the day being
displayed.
MONTHLY CALENDARS
The mini-month calendars are displayed on the right side of a
calendar view. The current monthly calendar display changes
as you change the display using the calendar controls on the
Calendar Tool Bar. The active date is framed in a small
rectangle. The calendar weeks are arranged according to the
preference you set for the beginning day of the week using the
Edit-Preferences command and clicking on the Calendar tab.
LEGEND
A small window that explains the color coding scheme used in
the busy bars. You can toggle the display of the legend by
selecting the Legend button on the Calendar Tool Bar or by
selecting the Customize-Design View command, selecting the
Color tab, and marking the Show Legend checkbox. You can
change the color coding scheme used by a calendar view by
selecting a different color coding template on the Color tab
dialog box. You can create new color coding templates using
the Customize-Database command and selecting the Color
Code tab.
Appointment Controls
The rest of the day calendar displays appointments that are
scheduled for particular start times. The daily calendar shows as
many appointments as can fit in the current window size. If you
make the window larger, the number of time slots displayed
increases with the window size.
 
TIME SLOTS
Time intervals are labeled in this column by their start time. You
can change the interval between time slots, and the time
displayed in the top-most slot using the Customize-Design
View command and then selecting the Display tab; The time is
displayed in the format you set through the Windows Control
Panel. The number of time slots displayed depends with on the
window size (See above).
 
ITEM NAME SLOTS
The fields that are displayed for a scheduled item can be
changed using the Customize-Design View command and
selecting the Fields tab. These fields are displayed in the slot
corresponding to the start time of the item. To display the item
detail for an item in a time slot in Day display mode, double-
click the mouse on the item name.
SCROLL BAR
Allows you to browse through the items not in the currently
displayed time interval. Use the arrows and box in the scroll bar
to move through the day's scheduled items. You can also use
the Page Up and Page Down keys for the same purpose.
Using Appointments with Day Display Mode
This section describes how to add, view, move, copy, edit and
delete appointments using a Calendar view in Day display mode.
Note: For keyboard users, highlighting an item or date and
pressing the Space Bar is equivalent to double clicking the
mouse button on an item.
Adding Appointments
Double click on a time slot. Commence displays the Add Item
screen with the Date and Start Time set to the date and start
time you selected. 
For example, if you double click on the
5:00pm time slot for Tuesday, 5/6/09, the Date will be set to
5/6/09 and the Start Time will be set to 5:00 p.m. To change
one or both values, type over the information in the appropriate
edit box.
 
To add a Day Title (an appointment with no start or end time),
double click in the blank slot located at the top of the calendar
below the Date Toolbar. The Start Time will default to the
current system time; clear this value so that both the start and
end times are blank.
Note: To add an appointment to your calendar regardless of the
calendar mode: Enter Ctrl + A or select the Add button on the
tool bar.
Drag an item from a view of any other category over your
calendar. If you drop the item over a time slot, it will be
scheduled for that time slot. If you drop it over an area without a
time, it will be added as a Day Title. Commence will
automatically connect the newly added appointment to the item
you copied from another category. See Drag and Drop for more
information.
Viewing and Editing Appointments
To display the item detail for an item in a time slot in the Day
display mode, double-click the mouse on the item name, or
highlight the time slot and press the Enter key. If multiple items
are scheduled for the same time slot, the Select Item dialog box
is displayed from which you select the item name of the item
you want to display.
Moving and Copying Appointments
To move an appointment drag the item to another day or time
slot. To move an appointment using the keyboard, highlight the
item and select the Edit-Edit Item command.
To copy an appointment hold down the Crtl key when you
select the item and drag the item to another day or time slot. To
copy an appointment using the keyboard, highlight the item and
select the Edit-Copy Item command.
See Drag and Drop for more information.
Deleting Appointments
To delete an appointment drag the item to the Delete button on
the tool bar or highlight the item and select the Edit-Delete Item
command, the Delete button on the tool bar, or the Delete key.
Two Days Display Mode
Two days display mode shows as many of the day's items as
possible for the current day of the week and the next day. If a day
has more items than can be displayed in the window size, a down
arrow appears at the bottom right corner of the day's rectangle.
You can re-size the window to display more items per day.
 
To switch to the a different display mode of the Calendar view,
click the right mouse button over the day's date. Choose Display
Mode from the right-click menu that appears, then select another
display mode from the list. You can also left click on Display
mode drop-down list on the calendar tool bar.
 
Note: For keyboard users, highlighting a date and pressing the
Space Bar will switch to the day display mode. From there you
can select any item on that day.
Using Appointments with Two Days Display Mode
This section describes how to add, view, move, copy, edit and
delete appointments using a Calendar view in 2 days display
mode.
Adding Appointments
Double click the mouse on any blank space within the day
rectangle. An Add Item screen is displayed.
Note: To add an appointment to your calendar regardless of the
calendar mode: Enter Ctrl + A or select the Add button on the
tool bar.
Drag an item from a view of any other category over your two
day calendar, it will be added as a Day Title. Commence will
automatically connect the newly added appointment to the item
you copied from another category. See Drag and Drop for more
information.
Viewing and Editing Appointments
To display the item detail for an item in the 2 day calendar,
double-click the mouse on the item name, or highlight the item
name and press the Enter key.
Moving and Copying Appointments
To move an appointment drag the item to another day. If you
drop it on a time slot in day mode, Commence will change its
start time as well as its date. If you drop it on a date, its start
time remains the same. To move an appointment using the
keyboard, highlight the item and select the Edit-Edit Item
command.
To copy an appointment hold down the Crtl key when you
select the item and drag the item to another day. To copy an
appointment using the keyboard, highlight the item and select
the Edit-Copy Item command.
See Drag and Drop for more information.
Deleting Appointments
To delete an appointment drag the item to the Delete button on
the tool bar or double click on the item to display the Item
Detail, and then select Edit-Delete Item command or the Delete
button on the tool bar.
Week Display Mode
Week display mode shows as many of the day's items as possible
for each day of the week in which the active date occurs. If a day
has more items than can be displayed in the current window size,
a down arrow appears at the bottom right corner of the day's
rectangle. Click the arrow to switch to the day display mode
where more items can be displayed. You can re-size the window
to display more items per day.
 
To switch to another display mode of the Calendar view, click the
right mouse button over the day's date. Choose Display Mode
from the right-click menu that appears, then select another
display mode from the list. You can also left click on Display
mode drop-down list on the calendar tool bar.
