Grid Display Mode
Grid mode allows you to see the items in the calendar for a
particular day in a columnar format based on the time of the item
and a connection to the item. For example, you can set up your
calendar grid mode to show all the appointments that are
connected to you and up to 7 other people (for a total of 8).
 
Using Appointments with Grid Display Mode
This section describes how to add, view, move, copy, edit and
delete appointments using a Calendar view in Grid display mode.
Note: For keyboard users, highlighting an item or date and
pressing the Space Bar is equivalent to double clicking the
mouse button on an item.
Adding Appointments
Double click on an empty time slot. Commence displays the
Add Item screen with the Date and Start Time set to the date
and start time you selected and will automatically be connected
to the item at the top of the column. For example, if you double
click on Tuesday, 5/6/97, at 5:00 p.m., the Date will be set to
5/6/97 and the Start Time will be set to 5:00 p.m. To change
one or both values, type over the information in the appropriate
edit box.
Note: To add an appointment to your calendar regardless of the
calendar mode: Enter Ctrl + A or select the Add button on the
tool bar.
Drag an item from a view of any other category over your
calendar. If you drop the item over a time slot, it will be
scheduled for that time slot and connected to the item shown at
the top of the column. Commence will automatically connect the
newly added appointment to the item you copied from another
category. See Drag and Drop for more information.
Viewing and Editing Appointments
To display the item detail for an item in a time slot in the Day
display mode, double-click the mouse on the item name, or
highlight the time slot and press the Enter key. If multiple items
are scheduled for the same time slot, the Select Item dialog box
is displayed from which you select the item name of the item
you want to display.
Moving and Copying Appointments
To move an appointment drag the item to another day or time
slot. To move an appointment using the keyboard, highlight the
item and select the Edit-Edit Item command.
To copy an appointment hold down the Crtl key when you
select the item and drag the item to another day or time slot. To
copy an appointment using the keyboard, highlight the item and
select the Edit-Copy Item command.
See Drag and Drop for more information.
Deleting Appointments
To delete an appointment drag the item to the Delete button on
the tool bar or highlight the item and select the Edit-Delete Item
command, the Delete button on the tool bar, or the Delete key.
Year Display Mode
The Year display mode shows all the dates for the active year by
displaying twelve monthly calendars. If you re-size the year mode
window, the calendar will re-size also. To switch to a day
Calendar view, double click the right mouse button over the day's
date. You can also click the left mouse button over a date and
then select another display mode from the drop down list on the
tool bar.

Form Properties Dialog Box
This dialog box, accessed by selecting Edit-Properties on the
Design Detail Form dialog box, allows you to set attributes for the
active tab page of the detail form.
 
A form that has been created by Commence (i.e., one delivered
with Commence or one created by Commence for a new
category) will be set up to use the default form options.
 
CAPTION
Change the tab label by typing in a new description into the
Caption text box.
 
FONTNAME
To change the font style for the tab label, click the button next to
the FontName property. You can change the font, font size, font
style (bold, italic, etc.), color, or effects (underline, etc.) all from
this screen.
 
FONTSIZE
The font size for the tab label is easily set using the FontName
property. If the specific font size you want is not available when
setting the FontName property, you can override the size setting
by manually entering the point size in this box. (e.g. 8.25)
 
BACKCOLOR
The Background Color box lets you reset the color of the form's
background using virtually any color available on your PC. Click
the ellipse button and a color palette will be displayed. Choose a
system color based on your Windows Control Panel settings, or
choose a basic color from the palette. If the desired color is not
shown, it may be created by selecting the Define Custom
Colors >> button from the color palette. This color will be used
everywhere there isn't a label or a field. Select Apply to save
your changes and return to the Design Detail Form dialog box.
 
You can also customize the tab pages of a form by adding,
editing, and deleting tabs. See Working with Detail Form Tabs for
more information.
Customize-Design View-Fields Tab (Report and Grid
Views)
See Also
Selecting the Customize-Design View command while a report or
grid view is the active view displays the Design View tab.
Use the Fields tab to choose the fields to appear in the view and
set field options as explained below. If you select a connection
name to appear in the view, you can choose a field from the
connected category. The available options on the Fields tab for a
Report Viewer differ slightly.
The Fields in Report and Fields Not In Report list boxes show
the list of fields (including connection definitions) that appear in
the view and those that don't appear in the view, respectively.
The Fields in Report list box displays field names in the order in
which the fields appear in the view.
To append a field to the list of fields in the view (making the new
field the last field listed in the view), highlight the field name in the
Fields Not In Report list box and select the Append Field
button.
Shortcut: Double-click the mouse on a field in the Fields Not In
Report list box to append it to the Fields In Report list without
selecting the Append Field button.
To insert a field into the list of fields in the view, highlight a field
name in the Fields Not In Report list box and highlight the
position where you want the field inserted in the Fields In Report
list box. Then, select the Insert Field button.
If the field you select is a connection definition, you can choose to
display any of the fields from the connected item in the view. For
example, you could show the name and phone number of each
person assigned to a To-Do item by highlighting Assigned to
Person in the Fields Not In Report list box and selecting either
the Insert Field or Append Field button. Commence displays a
list box containing the list of fields in the connected category.
Select the name and phone number fields, one field at a time,
from the list. Note that connection definitions in the connected
category are not displayed in the list box. You can only display
items that are directly connected to the category the view is
defined for.
To remove a field from the view, highlight the field name in the
Fields In Report list box and select the Remove Field button.
Shortcut: Double-click the mouse on a field in the Fields In
Report list box to remove it without selecting the Remove Field
button.
You can also combine fields in the view by selecting the Combine
Fields button and then selecting the desired fields.
Any changes you make to the design are temporary unless you
use Views-Save View or Views-Save View As to save the
changed view.
Select Font Dialog Box
Type a font name and font size in the Font Name and Font Size
text boxes, or select from the associated drop-down list boxes.
The valid font names and sizes in the list box reflect the fonts you
have installed in Windows.
 
You can also change the font style to Bold, Italic and/or Underline
by selecting the appropriate check boxes.
 
The Font Appearance box shows you what the selected font and
style look like.
 
Select OK once you've made your selections; you are returned to
the dialog box from which this window was invoked.
Letter Template Dialog Box
This dialog box appears when you select either the Add Text
button or the Edit button (with a text template highlighted) on the
Letter Template tab.
 
Select the topic below that matches the button you pressed:
 
Adding a Text Template
Editing a Text Template
Adding a Formatted Letter Template
See Also
To add a new formatted letter template, select the Add
Formatted button on the Letter Template tab. This displays the
Add Formatted Template dialog box.
 
Type a brief description for the new template in the Template
Name text box.
 
Use the For Category drop-down list box to select the category
that you want the template to be used with.
 
Finally, select the Create Template button to start up your word
processor with the new (empty) template file.
 
If you are using Word 2000 or later version, you are prompted for
the type of letter template to be created. Select the proper format.
This determines how the template will look.
 
You can enter Field Codes into the template by using the
Commence toolbar. Again, this assumes that you have installed
the required letter macros for your word processor.
 
You can format the template file using any features that are
available in your word processor: multiple fonts, page numbering,
date fields, bookmarks, embedded graphics, etc. When you are
satisfied with the template, save it, being sure to keep the original
filename so that Commence can locate the template when it
needs it to send letters.
Editing a Formatted Letter Template
To edit a formatted letter template, select the Edit button on the
Letter Template tab with a formatted template highlighted. This
displays the Edit Formatted Template dialog box.
File Name shows you the filename of the letter template file. This
file is stored in the tmplts subdirectory of the current database.
Do not change the name of this file when editing the letter
template.
To change the description of the template, use the Template
Name text box.
If you want to use the template with a different category, select
the new category with the For Category drop-down list box.
Note: If you change the category, the old Field Codes may not
properly map into the new category specified (unless the field
names were identical in both categories). You will need to select
the View button to edit the template and run the appropriate
macro for your Word Processor to insert the field codes for the
new category. Then save the template. See Using Field Codes in
Templates for instructions on how to insert field codes in
templates.
If you select Save, then any changes you have made to the
category or template name will be saved. If you select Cancel,
the template will be left unchanged.
If you want to change the contents of the template file, select the
View button. If you have changed the category or name, these
changes will automatically be saved. Commence then launches
your word processor and opens the template file. Use the word
processor to make any changes to the template file (new text,
Field Codes, formatting, etc.).
Add/Edit Trigger Dialog Box
Selecting the T button under If this Trigger occurs in the New
Agent or Edit Agent dialog box brings up the Add Trigger dialog
box. If you are editing an existing trigger, then the title bar of the
dialog box says Edit Trigger and the details of the existing trigger
appear in the box. The controls and operation of the two boxes
are identical.
 
The same dialog box is used for all triggers, but its contents vary
depending on what you highlight in the Trigger Type list on the
left hand side of the box. As you select a different trigger type, the
right hand side of the box changes to present the options
appropriate for the trigger type you have highlighted. Also, when
you highlight a different Trigger Type a brief description of the
trigger you have selected is shown under the list box.
 
If you select the Clear button in the Add Trigger or Edit Trigger
dialog box, the options for the current trigger are cleared. The OK
button returns you to the previous dialog box (New Agent or Edit
Agent) and saves the trigger values you have defined. The
Cancel command restores the previous trigger options (if any).
 
These are the triggers that you can use in Commence agents:
 
Commence Event
Fires when Commence starts or exits
Incoming Communication
Fires when a telephone call or E-Mail message is received
Item Alarm
Fires before a data item becomes current Keyboard Hotkey
Fires when you select a specific keyboard hotkey combination
Menu Pick
Fires when you select the associated menu pick from the Tools
menu
Receive DDE
Receive parameterized data from other Windows applications
and pass this data to the agent's actions Save Item
Fires when a data item is saved (added and/or edited)
Timer
Fires at predefined times
Tool Bar Pick
Fires when you select the associated button on the tool bar
Add/Edit Condition Dialog Box
To add, edit or delete a condition for an agent, select one of the
two condition buttons (1 or 2) on the New Agent or Edit Agent
dialog boxes.
 
Every agent can have up to two conditions in addition to its
trigger. Conditions place further qualifications on the initiation of
an agent, or identify other items for the agent's actions to operate
on. The Time Interval condition is only useful in the former mode:
it simply provides an additional test to determine if the agent
should be invoked. Item Count and Field Summary conditions
can be used both to further define the agent initiation and to
select condition items.
 
If you are adding a new condition, the Add Condition dialog box
appears. If you are editing an existing trigger, then the Edit
Condition box will appear. The controls and operation of the two
boxes are identical. The same dialog box is used for all
conditions, but its contents vary depending on what you highlight
in the Condition Type list on the left hand side of the box. This
dialog box lets you set the options for any condition type.
 
As you select different conditions, a brief description of the
condition you have selected is shown under the list box.
 
If you select Clear in the Add Condition or Edit Condition box,
the values for the current condition are cleared. The OK button
saves the values, and the Cancel button restores the previous
values (in an edit situation).
 
The three conditions available for use in Commence's Agents are
the following:
 
Field Summary Condition
True based on a calculated value summarized across items
Item Count
True based on the number of item meeting its criteria Time
Interval
True for specified date/time intervals
Clear
See Also
You have cleared the trigger, condition, or action that you were
working on.
 
If you are editing an agent, select the same Trigger, Condition,
or Action Type again to see the original definition.
 
Otherwise, select a new Type from the list box, or select one of
the OK or Cancel buttons.
Add Item Action
The Add Item action creates a new item, optionally using values
in the new item from a trigger or condition item.
Select the category that the new item will be added to using the
Category drop-down list box under Add Items To.
When you select the Field Values button, you are presented with
a Field Values dialog box that lets you specify the field values for
the new data item using text that you supply, default values for
the category (if you leave the field blank), or Field Codes from the
trigger or condition item.
Note that if the values for an Add Item action are taken from the
trigger item, then the action creates a single data item. However,
if an Add Item action uses condition items, it produces a new
item for every item in the set of condition items passed to it.
If you select the Display Item(s) check box, then the item(s) that
you add are displayed when the agent operates. You can then
manually add or edit the fields of the new item before it is saved.
If you don't select Display Item(s), Commence does all the work
for you automatically. If you are displaying items, you can also
specify the form that is used for the item detail using the Form
drop-down list box. If condition data is being used (so that
multiple items can be generated), a view of the new items is
displayed. If you don't select the Display Item(s) check box, then
Form is grayed out.
If you select the Connect to Selected Item(s) check box, then
the new item is connected to the trigger or condition item using
any available connection definition. This check box is available
even in the case when the category of the incoming trigger item is
unspecified. (For example, when using a trigger item from a
Menu Pick trigger that is not associated with a specific category.)
Application Launch Action
The Application Launch action can start up another Windows
application and pass it command line data.
 
In the Run area, use the Program: text box to enter the
command line text (the command line specifies the application
executable). If the application executable is in your path, you can
just enter the filename (e.g., write.exe), otherwise enter the full
path (e.g., c:\program files\microsoft office\office11\winword.exe).
 
If there are any command line arguments that are to be passed to
the executable, enter them in the Argument: text box. The
command line text can contain Field Codes from the selected
trigger or condition item.
 
If you want to browse your PC file system to locate the application
path and insert it into the text box, use the Select button. Use the
Insert Field button to insert valid Field Codes from the trigger or
condition item(s).
 
By selecting the corresponding option in the Mode drop-down list,
the application can be started in Normal mode (a standard
window with the focus), Maximized (a full screen window with the
focus), or Minimized (an icon without the focus, useful for
background processing).
Backup Action
The Backup action creates a backup of your open database.
 
When this action is performed, it does a full backup of the
database as if the Backup button had been selected from the
File-Open/Manage Database dialog box.
 
When you highlight the Backup action type, the Backup to group
box appears on the right side of the dialog box. Type in the name
of the file to hold your backup data.
 
Note: If you would like to store the backup file on a diskette,
specify a:\ in front of the filename in the group box.
Create E-Mail Action The Create E-Mail action
creates and sends an electronic mail message.
Before defining the actual message, you must
choose whether the category containing the
recipient’s name will come from the trigger or
condition data. This selection is made using the "To
Recipients Specified Using:" radio group.
If your Create E-Mail action is defined with "To Recipients
Specified Using: Trigger", one e-mail message will be created
when the agent fires. If your Create E-Mail action is defined with
"To Recipients Specified Using: Condition 1 or 2", one e-mail
message will be sent for each item matching the condition.
Once this selection has been made, it is time to proceed to the
Define E-Mail dialog box. This dialog box appears when the
Define Message button is selected.
Delete Item Action The Delete Item action deletes
one item when using values from the trigger. This is
equivalent to the Edit-Delete Item command.
 
When using values from a condition item, the Delete Item action
deletes ALL the items which meet the condition. This is
equivalent to the Edit-Delete All Items command.
Use this action carefully. You will not be presented with a
confirmation message. The items will be deleted automatically.
Edit Item Action
The Edit Item action edits data items, either the single trigger
item or multiple items from one of the conditions. When you
select the Field Values button, Commence displays the Field
Values dialog box that lets you specify the field values for the
edited data item using text that you supply and/or Field Codes
from the unedited values.
 
If you select the Display Item(s) check box, then the item(s) that
you edit are displayed when the agent operates. If you are
displaying items, you can also specify the form to use for the item
detail by using the Form drop-down list box. If you do not select
the Display Item(s) check box, then the Form drop-down list box
will be grayed out.
 
If the values for an Item Edit/Display action are taken from the
trigger item, then the action edits a single data item. However, if
an Item Edit/Display action uses condition items, it edits all the
items in the condition set. When multiple items are edited, a
report view of the edited items is displayed if you select the
Display Item(s) check box.
 
Note that the Item Edit/Display action requires selection of either
a trigger or a condition from a specific category.
Export Action
The Export action exports data from your open database to a
pre-selected import/export file using a pre-selected template.
Note: Before defining this agent, make sure you already have an
appropriate export template defined. If you do not, use the
Customize-Database-Import/Export command to do so.
 
When you highlight the Export action type in the Add or Edit
Action dialog box, the Export Items From group boxes appear
on the right side of the dialog box. Use these group boxes to
choose the items to export, to select an import/export template
and to enter the name of an export file.
 
The DOS Text checkbox allows you to translate between the
Windows standard character set and the DOS character set. If
you are sharing information between Commence and other
Windows applications, leave this box unchecked. If you are
sharing information between Commence and other applications
that run in DOS but are not designed specifically for Windows,
you should check this box.
 
The Archive after Export check box allows you to delete all
items that have been exported from your database.
 
Caution: Use this option only to archive and delete items you
no longer need in your database.
Import Action
The Import action imports data from a pre-selected import/export
file to your open database using a pre-selected template.
Note: Before defining this agent, make sure you already have an
appropriate import template defined. If you do not, use the
File-Import command to do so.
 
When you highlight the Import action type in the Add or Edit
Action dialog box, the Import Using group box appears on the
right side of the dialog box. Use this group box to select an
import/export template and to enter the name of an import file.
 
The DOS Text checkbox allows you to translate between the
Windows standard character set and the DOS character set. If
you are sharing information between Commence and other
Windows applications, leave this box unchecked. If you are
sharing information between Commence and other applications
that run in DOS but are not designed specifically for Windows,
you should check this box.
Manage Desktop Action
The Manage Desktop action allows you to manipulate the views
on the active desktop. It also allows you to open another desktop.
 
Choose one of the following radio buttons:
 
Cascade Overlaps all normal and maximized views on the
desktop. The active view will be placed on top, and
all other views will be stacked behind it. Minimized
views are left minimized.
Resize All Resizes all views (except minimized views) so that
they fit on the active desktop while maintaining the
relative porportions and positions of each individual
view.
Tile Horizontally Re-positions all normal and maximized views
on the desktop. The active view will be placed on
top, and all other views will be arranged under the
active view. Minimized views are left minimized.
Tile Vertically Re-positions all normal and maximized views on
the desktop. The active view will be positioned left-
most on the desktop, and all other views will be
arranged side-by-side to the right of the active view.
Minimized views are left minimized.
Close All Closes all views, including minimized views.
Open Desktop Switches to the specified desktop.
 
If you select the Open Desktop option, you must also select the
desktop you wish to open from the drop-down list box.
Message Box Action
The Message Box action displays a Commence message box
that displays a message and up to four action buttons that allow
the user to perform additional actions when the box is displayed.
You control the text of the message and the buttons you want to
appear in the box (if any).
 
Enter the text of the message in the Display Message text box.
The text of the message can contain Field Codes from the
selected trigger or condition data. If you have selected trigger or
condition item(s) from a specific category, the Insert Field button
will be active. You can then select Insert Field to add a Field
Code to the Display Message text box. When the box is
displayed, if you have checked Audible Alarm. a tone sounds to
further alert you.
 
Three buttons may appear in any message box displayed by
agents: OK, Snooze and Snooze All. If you select the OK button
in the message box the message box disappears.
 
Selecting the Snooze or Snooze All button displays a Set
Snooze Time dialog box that asks for the number of minutes after
which you want the message box to reappear. Snooze affects
only the currently displayed message box, while Snooze All
affects the current message box and all currently pending
message boxes. If you use the Snooze button and there are
pending messages, the next pending message is displayed after
you select the snooze time.
Note: The Message Box snooze alarm works only within a
Commence session. That is, if you set a snooze time for a
message box and then exit Commence, the snooze time is lost
and the message box will not reappear when you reopen
Commence.
 
The message box can optionally display up to four Action
Buttons. Each of the Action Buttons can in turn invoke an
additional sub-action (the full set of actions are available as
possible sub-actions, except for Message Box). These actions
are specified just like other agent actions, but only the action
corresponding to the button you select when the agent executes
is performed (i.e., you select one button, the corresponding action
is performed, and then the message box exits). If you select the
OK button in the message box, no action is performed and the
message box disappears. If you select one of the action buttons,
the corresponding sub-action will be performed.
 
If the Message Action is not the last action for the agent, a
Cancel button is also displayed in the message box under the OK
button. Selecting Cancel tells Commence to perform none of the
sub-actions and cancel the remaining actions not yet performed
by the agent.
 
When you select the Action Buttons button in the Edit Action or
New Action dialog boxes, Commence displays the Message Box
Actions dialog box.
New View Action
A New View action creates and displays a new view. The view
can show a new view of the trigger item, the condition items, or
any category. Select the radio button under View Items In to
make your choice.
 
If you select Category, a drop-down list box appears to the right
that lets you choose the category you want to view. If you select
Category, you can also use the Select Filter button to bring up
Filters tab and define a filter for that category.
 
Select the view type for the new view using the View As drop-
down list box. If you select report, you can choose to view the
item names only, or all fields by choosing the corresponding
button in the Report Style radio group.
 
The new view action is a convenient way to display condition
items in an agent. In addition to being useful in a production
agent, it is helpful to check out the operation of new agents.
Open View Action
An Open View action opens an existing saved view. The view to
be opened can be selected using the same category as the
trigger item, or the condition items. Select the radio button under
Using Values From to make your choice.
 
If you prefer, you may select a specific category to be used every
time, regardless of the trigger or conditions. If you select
Category, a drop-down list box appears to the right that lets you
choose the category you want to view.
 
Use the View Name drop-down list box under Open View to
select the view.
 
Open the view if another copy is already open.
Outlook Sync
This action allows you to automate the synchronization of data
with Microsoft Outlook. The Outlook Sync action lets you select
Contacts, Calendar, or Tasks for synchronization. If for example
Contacts is the only selected application, then only the Contact
information will synchronize with Outlook. If the agent is designed
to synchronize just one application, this setting is enforced even if
other Outlook Application preferences are mapped.
Play Multimedia Action
This action allows you to play multimedia files including sound
and video files. If you have the proper hardware and Windows
multimedia drivers installed, you can play a special sound or
video clip. For example, you can play a special sound to warn you
about pending appointments. To define a Play Multimedia action,
file in the File text box with the path of the multimedia file to be
played. You can use the Select button to search your PC for the
path to a multimedia file. The type of multimedia file is identified
by the file's extension. Common multimedia file types include:
.WAV (sound files), .AVI (video files), .MID (MIDI files). See the
Drivers section in your Windows Control Panel to determine
which multimedia capabilities your system has installed.
Print Letter Action
A Print Letter action will generate a new letter based on one of
Commence's text letter templates (formatted letter templates
cannot be used with this action). It is equivalent to using the
commands that create and print letters.
The action requires the use of either a trigger item or condition
items. If you use a trigger item, a single letter is printed for the
trigger item (similar to using Send Letter and selecting the
Merge/No Preview option). If you use condition items, a letter is
printed for each of the condition items (similar to Send Letter).
Select the template to be used for the letter with the Templates
drop-down list box. The text templates available will be those for
the category of the trigger or condition item(s) that you have
selected. Thus you must select trigger or condition item(s) from a
specific category to use this action.
You can select the Log Item check box if you wish to have a
document log item created for the letter. If you select Log, use the
Item Name text box to enter a name for the Log Item. You can
insert a Field Code into the Log item name using Insert Field.
(Keep in mind that Item names are limited to 50 characters: the
name is truncated if it exceeds this limit.)
See Letter File Storage for an explanation of where on your hard
disk letters are stored.
If you select the Send to Printer radio button, then the letter will
be printed. You can enter the number of copies that you want
printed in the Copies text box. If you select the Fonts button,
then you will see the Select Font dialog box that will let you
specify the font to be used when the letter is printed.
If you select the Send to File radio button, then the letter is
saved in a file instead of being printed. Enter the full path and
name of the file in the text box below the button. You can use
Field Codes in the text box, but be aware that the action will fail if
the generated filename is invalid when the agent operates. If you
select both Send to File and Log Item then the document log
item will point to the file that you have specified. This option is
useful if you want to create text files that will be passed on to
other applications (e.g., electronic mail packages).
The Send to File radio button can only be selected if you are
using a trigger item (single letters) since condition items would
generate multiple letters that could not be saved in a single file.
Thus, if you select condition items the Send to File radio button
is grayed out.
Process E-Mail Action
When used with the trigger, Incoming Communication, this agent
action can either delete the incoming E-Mail, or mark the E-Mail
as Read. These actions can be performed whether the E-Mail
application is running in the background or not.
Send DDE Action
See Also
A Send DDE action sends a DDE message to another Windows
application. Press the Define Message button.
PROGRAM. . . GROUP BOX
If you want Commence to start up the other application if it is not
already active, turn on the Run If Necessary check box and
enter the path of the receiving application in the File Name text
box. You can browse your PC's file system to find the application
and insert its path in the File Name text box by using the Select
button.
 
CONVERSATION. . . GROUP BOX
 
Enter the DDE name of the other application in the Application
Name text box. For Commence, the Application name is
Commence. Enter the DDE Topic in the Topic Name text box.
This information can be found in the manual for the other
application.
 
DDE MESSAGE. . . GROUP BOX
You can send a DDE Execute message or a DDE Poke message
with data to a Windows application.
 
To send a DDE Execute message, turn on the Execute radio
button in the DDE Message group box and enter the text of the
DDE message in the Message Text text box.
 
To send a DDE Poke message, turn on the Poke radio button in
the DDE Message group box. The message must consist of two
parts: the data and an item name . Enter the item name (or
similar data identifier, such as row-column number for
spreadsheets) into the Item Name text box. Then specify the text
of the Poke message in the Message Text box.
 
The DDE message can contain Field Codes from the selected
trigger or condition item(s). Use the Insert Field command to
include field codes from the trigger or condition data in the data
identifier and/or the data. See Using Trigger and Condition Data
Notes: If you select to send DDE Poke message, make sure that
the application expects this command instead of a DDE Execute
message. DDE Request messages are not supported in
Commence agent actions.
Set Agent Status
A Set Agent Status action is used to activate and deactivate
other Commence agents. Select an agent name from the Agent
list box and use the Active and Inactive radio buttons to select
the action.
 
The Set Agent Status action does not use values from trigger or
condition item(s).
Status Bar Message Action
A Status Bar Message action displays a message on
Commence's status bar. Enter the text of the message in the text
box under Display Status Bar Message. The message text can
contain Field Codes from the selected trigger or condition item(s).
 
Use the Insert Field button to select from a list of available Field
Codes for the selected trigger or condition category. If you select
the Audible Alarm check box, an alarm will sound as the
message is displayed. Messages on the status bar will clear after
six seconds, or sooner if another message is written to the status
bar.
 
All Status Bar Messages are added to the log of status bar
message that can be viewed using the Help-Message Log
command.

Use Telephone Action
The Use Telephone action can be used to answer an incoming
phone call, hang up the phone, or dial the phone. This action can
be used with the Incoming Communication trigger.
Using Trigger and Condition Data
All actions (except Icon Modification, Open View, and Set
Agent Status) allow you to use the trigger item or one of the two
condition item sets (if you have selected conditions for the agent)
as part of the action specification.
 
One of the actions (Edit Item) requires this selection, since the
item(s) to be edited must be either the trigger item or one of the
two sets of condition items. You make the selection of trigger or
condition item(s) by picking the appropriate radio button in the
Using Values From section. If you have not selected conditions
for the agent, the condition radio buttons will be grayed out. Also,
if you select a trigger or condition that is not capable of passing
items, then the corresponding button will also be grayed out. By
default, the Trigger radio button is selected for new actions.
 
Whichever button you select, the corresponding category name
will be shown after Selected Category. The category shown is
determined by the category of the trigger item or the condition
items. If no specific category is implied by your choice, then you
will see Selected Category: (All Categories).
 
Note that for any particular trigger or condition, the item(s)
passed to an action will always come from one category.
Furthermore, except for the case of a trigger item from (All
Categories), when you define an agent, you will know exactly
what fields are available from the trigger or condition item, since
the fields are determined by the category of the item. You can use
data from these fields in the action by inserting Field Codes in the
places where you want the data to appear. Whenever you have
selected a specific category, an Insert Field button appears to let
you select Field Codes from the category. When the agent
operates, the Field Codes are replaced with the corresponding
values from the trigger or condition item. See Using Field Codes
In Actions.
 
You can use trigger data when filtering a category in an Item
Count condition. If you create an agent that has trigger data, then
a condition in that same agent can contain a filter using field
codes representing values from the trigger item. This lets you
pass data from the trigger to the condition. An example is an
agent that passes the Close Date to the connected Support Call
when a Support Followup item is closed. . For this example, the
condition filter must isolate the Item Name of the connected
Support Call in order to accurately determine which Support Call
item must be modified. The following filter on the Support Call
category accomplishes this task.:
has - Support Followup - Name - Contains - (-
TrigItemName-)
Filtering By Field Value
See Also
The available filter options are different for each field type.
 
For a calculation, number, or sequence number field type, the
comparison operators are Equal To, Greater Than, Is Between,
Less Than, and Not Equal To. Choose the comparison you want
and type a numeric comparison value into the Field Value text
box. When filtering for a range of numbers (Is Between), type the
low number into the Minimum Value text box and type the high
number into the Maximum Value text box.
 
A check box field type can only be Checked or Not Checked.
 
For a date field type, the comparison operators are After, Before,
Is Between, Is Blank, and On. Choose the comparison you want
and type a comparison value (in the appropriate date format
selected via the Windows Control Panel) into the Field Value text
box. When filtering for a range of dates (Is Between), type the
start date into the Minimum Value text box and type the end date
into the Maximum Value text box. You can specify a natural
language date instead of a literal date.
 
For an e-mail address, internet address, or text field type, you
can select from the following comparison operations:
 
Contains You want to see items whose selected field contains
the value you typed into the edit box, but other
text can appear in the field.
Does Not Contain You want to see items whose select field
does not contain the value you typed into the
edit box.
Equal To You only want to see items whose selected field
exactly matches the value you typed into the
text box. No other text should appear in the
item's field value.
Is Between You only want to see items whose selected field
contains values ranging between the Minimum
and Maximum Value.
Is Blank You only want to see items whose selected field
contains no text.
 
The name uses all the same comparison operators as text fields
and also allows the following:
 
Has Duplicates You want to see items whose name fields
contain the same value.
 
After selecting a comparison operator for the name, e-mail
address, internet address, or text field types, you can then enter a
single field value, or press the Options button to enter 4 or more
field values (up to 10). See the topic titled Advanced Filter
Options. The Match Case checkbox allows you to perform either
a case-sensitive or case-insensitive operation as needed.
 
Note: Telephone fields can be filtered using all the same
comparison operations as text fields except Is Between.
Advanced filter options are not available for telephone fields.
 
You can also use wildcards in a comparison value for a name,
telephone, e-mail address, internet address, or text field type.
 
For a selection type field, you select one of the Equals or Does
Not Equal comparison operators, and then select a value from
the list box displayed.
 
For a time field, the comparison operators are After, At, Before,
Is Between, and Is Blank. Choose the comparison you want and
type a comparison value into the Field Value text box. When
filtering for a range of times (Is Between), type the start time into
the Minimum Value text box and type the end time into the
Maximum Value text box. The time value entered must be in the
appropriate time format set via the Windows Control Panel. You
can specify a natural language time value instead of a literal time.
 
Select OK to confirm your choice or Cancel to return to previous
screen without selecting a field value.
Wildcards
Simple and multiple filters allow you to use wildcards that match
one, or one or more, instances of a character. Wildcards can be
used only when filtering by a name, text, or telephone field.
 
"?" is a special character that matches a single instance of any
ASCII character in a name or text field. For example, "070??"
would match any zip code beginning with 070 followed by two
characters. To filter for blank fields, you can use the Contains
qualifier as the comparison operator and search for "?".
 
"*" is a special character that matches zero or more instances of
any ASCII character. For example, searching for "Smith,*" in the
Person's name field would select all people with the last name of
Smith.
 
Note: In telephone field-based filters using the Does Not Contain
or Contains qualifier, only "*" can be used as a wildcard as
explained above. Neither a "?" nor a "*" can be used as a
wildcard in a filter on a telephone field using the Equal To
qualifier.
Select Color Field Dialog Box
When you select the Add button on the Color Code tab, the
Select Color Field dialog box is displayed.
 
You should choose a field on which to base your color code
template. When the field value changes, a different color will be
used for that item. Highlight the name of the field and press the
Select button to choose a field for the template. Only selection
fields, check box fields, and connection definitions can be used in
color templates, so those are the only fields listed to choose from.
 
Shortcut: Mouse users can double-click the left mouse button
while pointing at a field name to choose it without using the
Select button.
 
Once you choose a field, the Color Code Template dialog box is
displayed.
Color Code Template Dialog Box
COLOR CODE NAME TEXT BOX
 
If you are adding a new color code template, fill in a short
description of the template here. You must type in a name before
you can save the template. Template names are limited to 24
characters.
 
If you are editing a color code template, you may edit the name if
you wish.
UP TO EIGHT FIELD/COLOR BOX SLOTS
Beneath the Color Code Name text box, up to eight colored
boxes (depending on the field type) are displayed.
 
When you are adding a template, a number of blank slots are
displayed, each with a color box and a drop-down list box. You
must select field values and color for each slot.
 
When you are editing a template, each slot displays a field value
and the color assigned to that value. You can change the color
assigned to a slot at any time.
 
To change a color, click on the colored box. Selecting one of the
color boxes opens a drop-down color list box of 16 colors to
choose from. Select a new color from the list.
 
If the field on which the template is based is a selection field,
each slot corresponds to a radio button in the group (radio groups
contain at most 8 radio buttons) or a selection in the combo box
drop down list..
 
If the field on which the template is based is a check box field,
two slots are displayed (check box fields have two possible
values: checked or unchecked).
 
If the field on which the template is based is a connection
definition, eight slots are displayed, each with a drop-down list
box initially set to Unused. If you select the arrow at the right of
one of the list boxes, you'll see a list of all the connected items'
names. You can select item names for the first six slots. If there
are less than six connected items in the drop-down box, you
should leave extra slots set to Unused. The last two slots are
reserved for the special entries None and Default Color. None is
used when the connected item has not been associated with a
color. The Default Color is used when the connection on which
the template is based is blank (unconnected) or if multiple items
are connected.
 
SAVE AND CANCEL BUTTONS
Once you are satisfied with the template, select the Save button
to save the template; select the Cancel button to cancel the add
or edit operation.
Add Connection Dialog Box
This dialog box is displayed when you select the Add button on
the Customize-Database-Connection dialog box.
 
The active category's name is highlighted in the From text box
automatically. Highlight a category name for the other side of the
link in the To box and select OK.
 
The Names for New Connection dialog box is displayed in which
you specify one name for a simple connection or two names for a
paired connection. The connection name(s) are used to label
connection fields on item detail forms.
Edit Connection Names Dialog Box
Names for New Connection Dialog Box
These identical dialog boxes are displayed when adding or
editing connection definitions from the Customize-Database-
Connection dialog box.
 
For a paired connection, two text boxes are displayed, labeled
with the two categories participating in the connection definition.
If you are editing a connection definition , the boxes contain the
existing connection definition names.
 
For a simple connection, one text box are displayed, labeled with
the category participating in the connection definition. If you are
editing a connection definition , the box contains the existing
connection definition name.
 
For a new connection definition, type a name into the text
box(es). The name(s) you pick should reflect the purpose of the
connection definition .
 
Select the Save button to save the names you choose. Select
Cancel to cancel the add or edit operation.
 
If you're editing a connection definition name, Commence
changes the connection definition name(s) in all items in the two
categories once you select Save. This may take some time,
depending on the number of existing category items. When it is
finished, you are returned to the Connection dialog box, which
now reflects the new connection name(s).
Connection Fields Dialog Box
This dialog box is displayed when you select the Advanced
button on the Customize-Database-Connection dialog box, then
click Field Option. You can choose the fields to appear in the
connection pop-up window and in the connection field on the
detail form.
 
The Fields in Report and Fields Not In Report list boxes show
the list of fields (including connection definitions) that appear in
the connection and those that don't appear in the connection,
respectively.
 
The Fields in Report list box displays field names in the order in
which the fields appear in the view.
 
ADDING COLUMNS
To append a field to the list of fields in the view (making the new
field the last field listed in the view), highlight the field name in
the Fields Not In Report list box and select the Append Field
button.
 
Shortcut: Double-click the mouse on a field in the Fields Not In
Report list box to append it to the Fields In Report list without
selecting the Append Field button.
 
To insert a field into the list of fields in the view, highlight a field
name in the Fields Not In Report list box and highlight the
position where you want the field inserted in the Fields In
Report list box. Then, select the Insert Field button.
 
ADDING RELATED COLUMNS
If the field you select is a connection definition, you can choose
to display any of the fields from the connected item. Commence
displays a list box containing the list of fields in the connected
category. Select the fields, one field at a time, from the list. Note
that connection definitions in the connected category are not
displayed in the list box.
 
REMOVING COLUMNS
To remove a field from the connection, highlight the field name in
the Fields In Report list box and select the Remove Field
button.
 
Shortcut: Double-click the mouse on a field in the Fields In
Report list box to remove it without selecting the Remove Field
button.
 
COLUMN HEADINGS
If needed, you can change the label that appears at the top of
the column for each field in the connection. Highlight the field
name in the Fields In Report list box and select the Field
Options button. Then enter the new field label in the label box.
Design Form Dialog Box
If you select the Add, Edit, or Copy buttons from the Detail Form
dialog box, the dialog box named above is displayed.
 
From the Display tab, you can choose a name for a detail form
and select the fields to appear on the form. You can also invoke
dialog boxes that let you drag fields to new positions in the form,
set the field attributes like color and font.
 
The Advanced tab lets you set printing and auto-selection options
for the form.
 
From the Scripting tab, you can create Visual Basic scripts for
each form. This enables such operations as setting default
values, filling in fields, and navigating within the form, as well as
performing validation checks before an item is saved.
 
SAVE
Select the Save button to save the detail form you are working
on.
 
CANCEL
Select Cancel to cancel the new, edited, or copied detail form
and return to the Detail Form dialog box.
Set Default Detail Forms Dialog Box
Use the Set Defaults button on the Detail Form dialog box to
define which detail form to always use for adding an item (Default
for Add Item) and which detail form to use when you edit or view
an item detail (Default for Item Detail).
 
If you have more than one form defined for a category and do not
select defaults, Commence will ask you to choose a detail form
when you display or add an item detail.
 
If you have only one form for a category, Commence will
automatically display that form for adding and displaying items,
even if you have not set defaults.
 
Choose default forms for adding and editing items by selecting a
form name from the drop-down list boxes on the Set Defaults
dialog box. The same form name can be used for both defaults.
 
The Auto Select Detail Form When option on the Design Form
dialog box overrides the default form for viewing or editing an
item detail set using this dialog box.
Import Rules Dialog Box
When you select the Import Rules button from the Template
Definition dialog box, Commence displays the Import Rules
dialog box.
 
The New Items radio group tells Commence how to insert items
with new names into your category. Select the Add radio button
to automatically add each item with a new name into the
category. Select the Ask before adding radio button to have
Commence prompt you for confirmation before adding each new
item.
 
The Existing Items radio group tells Commence how to handle
incoming items that have the same name as one or more existing
items in your category. How you want to handle these items will
depend in part on whether your category allows duplicates. The
radio button choices are described below.
 
Delete all existing items before input
Deletes all items in the target category before adding the new
data from the import file.
 
Replace duplicates, all fields
Deletes each item in the target category whose name matches
that of an incoming data item and replaces it with the data item
from the import file.
 
Replace duplicates, imported fields only
For duplicate items, replaces only the field values being
imported. Other field values in the item remain unchanged.
 
Ask on duplicates, then replace all fields
For each item in the target category whose name matches that of
an incoming data item, display a message box showing the item
name and you if it should replace the existing data item. If you
select OK, the existing data item is deleted and the incoming
data item is added.
 
Ask on duplicates, then replace imported fields only
For each item in the target category whose name matches that of
an incoming data item, display a message box showing the item
name and you if it should replace the data fields for this item. If
you select OK, the existing data fields in the item is deleted and
the incoming data item is added.
 
Add duplicates as new item
If the category allows duplicates, items with the same names as
existing items will simply be added to the category.
 
If an incoming item has a blank name field, Commence
automatically assigns the value (none) as the item name. To use
this feature, make sure that your category allows duplicates.
Turn on the Add duplicates as new items radio button, so that
all of the items so named are imported into the category.
 
Skip duplicates
Ignore an incoming item that has the same name as an existing
item.
 
When you have chosen the rules you want, select OK to return to
the Template Mapping dialog box.
Export Rules Dialog Box
When you select the Export Rules button from the Template
Definition dialog box, Commence displays the Export Rules
dialog box.
 
The New Items radio group tells Commence how to handle items
with new names that are not found in the external data source.
Select the Add radio button to automatically add each item with a
new name into the data source. Select the Ask before adding
radio button to have Commence prompt you for confirmation
before adding each new item.
 
The Existing Items radio group tells Commence how to handle
exported items that have the same name as one or more existing
items in your external data source. How you want to handle these
items will depend in part on whether the table allows duplicates.
The radio button choices are described below.
 
Delete all existing items before export
Deletes all items in the target data source before adding the new
data from Commence.
 
Replace duplicates, all fields
Deletes each item in the target data source whose name
matches that of an exported data item and replaces it with the
data item from Commence.
 
Replace duplicates, exported fields only
For duplicate items, replaces only the columns in data source
that have been mapped to field values being exported. Other
columns in the item remain unchanged.
 
Ask on duplicates, then replace all fields
For each item in the target data source whose name matches
that of an exported data item, display a message box showing
the item name and you if it should replace the existing data item.
If you select OK, the existing data item is deleted and the data
from Commence item is added.
 
Ask on duplicates, then replace exported fields only
For each item in the target data source whose name matches
that of an exported data item, display a message box showing
the item name and you if it should replace the data fields for this
item. If you select OK, the mapped fields in the item are cleared
and the field data from Commence is added.
 
Add duplicates as new items
If the table allows duplicates, items with the same names as
existing items will simply be added to the category.
 
Skip duplicates
Ignore an exported item that has the same name as an existing
item in the data source.
 
When you have chosen the rule you want, select OK to return to
the Template Mapping dialog box.
Server Communications Dialog Box
This dialog box is displayed when you choose the Edit button
from the Workgroup Communications dialog box on the Server.
Fill in the necessary information for the selected method of
syncing. All methods of syncing may be configured to
accommodate each individual user’s needs.
Setting Server Communications
OK
Select OK to accept any changes you have made on this screen.
CANCEL
Select Cancel to return to the Workgroup Communications
dialog box without saving your changes.
 
 
 
 
Configuring E–Mail Parameters
Perform the following procedure to configure the Server to use E–
Mail
to exchange synchronizing information:
 
1. From the Workgroup
Communications Dialog Box, highlight E-Mail
and select the Edit
button.
Note: It is assumed that the e–mail application is already
installed
and operational.
2. Configure the E-Mail
parameters as follows:
. Mail
Program - select the appropriate mail program/protocol from
the
pull–down menu.
. Post
Office Path - only necessary if using cc:Mail
or similar VIM–based mail system.
. Profile
Name [only applies to Microsoft Outlook or MAPI compatible
programs]
- the name used to log into E-Mail program.
. Login
Name [only applies to CMC–compatible programs] - the
name used
by the Server to log into the E–Mail Account. This is the
account that
the Server will be using to send/receive sync packets to and
from clients.
. Password/Verify
Password - the password associated with the Profile
Name/Login Name defined above.
Note: Although the same mail account can be used for the
Server
and Clients, it is strongly recommended that the Server and
each Client
be assigned separate mail accounts.
3. When the Server’s e–mail login information is
correct, select OK.
4. Next, the e–mail address for the Server must be
specified. This is the address to which Clients mail their
update information during a synchronization.
5. Indicate how often (in minutes) the Server will
check the mail server for Client synchronization requests.
This number
should not be very large in order to assure the timely receipt
of updated information by the Server. A recommended value
is ten (10) minutes.
6. Select OK
when done. The Workgroup Communications
Dialog Box will be displayed.
 
 
 
Configuring Internet FTP Directory Parameters
Synchronizing via the Internet is much the same process as
synchronizing via a shared LAN directory, only instead of the
directory’s being located on a LAN, it is now located remotely and
is accessed via dial–up service (e.g. Internet Service Provider) or
a dedicated Internet connection. The transferring of sync files
between Client(s) and Server is then done using File Transfer
Protocol (FTP) procedures.
Perform the following procedure to configure the server with the
Internet FTP Directory information:
1. From the Workgroup Communications Dialog Box, highlight
Internet FTP Directory and select the Edit button.
2. Enter the Internet FTP Directory information. The format of
this address is ftp://DIRECTORY_NAME. If the Internet FTP
Directory is unknown, it should be available from the
Network Administrator responsible for the FTP site.
3. Enter the User Name under which the Server will log into the
Internet FTP Directory. It is recommended that the Admin
login be used.
4. Enter the Password for the Server’s Login ID.
5. Enter the Password again to Verify Password.
6. Indicate how often (in minutes) the Server will check the
Internet FTP Directory for Client synchronization requests. A
recommended value is fifteen (15) minutes.
7. After the Internet FTP Directory parameters have been
configured as desired, select OK. The Workgroup
Communications Dialog Box will be displayed.
After all desired Server Communications Parameters have been
successfully configured, select OK from the Workgroup
Communications Dialog Box. The Customize Workgroup -
Communications Dialog Box will again be displayed.
 
 
 
 
Configuring Shared LAN Directory Parameters
Perform the following procedure to configure the Server to use a
Shared
LAN Directory for exchanging synchronizing information:
 
1. From the Workgroup
Communications Dialog Box, highlight Shared LAN
Directory and select the Edit
button. See the following figure.
2. Set the path for the Shared LAN Directory. Enter this path
as it would be seen by the Commence Server. It is through
this directory that the Server will communicate
with the Clients by sending and receiving data files. The
directory selected should be accessible to all users, and
should be a new directory that
is not used by any other program or Commence. All users
require full access (read/write/create/delete/etc.) to this
directory.
Note: If unsure of the path to the Shared LAN Directory, or to
create a new directory, click on Select.
If the desired directory exists, navigate to the directory,
highlight
it, and click OK, then click OK
again. To create a new directory, click Create Directory,
provide the name of the new Shared LAN Directory, click OK,
then click OK
again.
3. Indicate how frequently (in seconds) the Server
should check the shared LAN directory for data updates sent
by Clients.
A very small number will guarantee that updates are
processed in a timely manner, although the Server may be
busy for extended periods of time.
This limits the Server’s ability to run other tasks. The default
value
for this is every 15 seconds. This value should be adjusted to
meet the specific needs for the Workgroup being established,
as well as the hardware specifications.
4. When the Shared LAN Directory parameters have
been configured as desired, select OK.
The Workgroup Communications
Dialog Box will be displayed.
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Configuring E–Mail Parameters
Perform the following procedure to configure a Commence Client
to use e–mail for synchronizing:
 
1. Select the Communicate with Server using: E–Mail radio
button.
2. Click OK.
The Client Communications Dialog Box is displayed.
3. Complete the User Address for the Client. This is the
address to which the Server sends synchronization
information that is intended for this Client.
4. Click OK
to return to the Customize Workgroup - Communications
Dialog Box.
 
 
 
Configuring Internet FTP Directory Parameters
Perform the following procedure to configure a Commence Client
to use an Internet FTP Directory for synchronizing:
1. Select the Communicate with Server using: Internet FTP
directory radio button.
2. Click OK. A message indicating that no further configuring of
parameters is necessary is displayed.
3. Click OK to return to the Customize Workgroup -
Communications Dialog Box.
 
 
 
 
Configuring Shared LAN Directory Parameters
The default method for synchronizing data within a Commence
Workgroup is using a Shared LAN Directory.
Perform the following procedure to configure a Commence Client
to use a Shared LAN Directory for synchronizing:
 
1. Select the Communicate with Server using: Shared LAN
directory radio button.
2. Click OK.
A message indicating that no further configuring is necessary
is displayed.
3. Click OK
to return to the Customize Workgroup - Communications
Dialog Box.
 
 
 
Category Tab
Categories store your data. Sometimes you may want to add a
new category or change one to meet your needs. To do this,
select the Customize-Database-Category command. You will see
a Category Names list box. Select a category from the list, then
press the appropriate button, depending on what customization
you need to do. Use the Edit button to customize your existing
category definitions (i.e., number and types of fields, field labels,
default field values, and other attributes that vary between field
types). You can add your own categories by adding entirely new
definitions (Add button) or copying an existing definition and
editing it (Copy button). You can also delete any category (Delete
button). For more information about deleting categories, see
Deleting Categories .
 
When you press the Time Conflict button, Commence displays
the Time Conflict Layout dialog box. Enabling time conflict
checking for a category means that Commence will check each
item added to the category for a time conflict with items already in
the category. If a conflict is detected, Commence asks you if you
want to add the conflicting item anyway.
 
You can change a category definition at any time, even if the
category has data in it. This means you can try out category
definitions and fine tune them as you work, without having to re-
enter any of your data. Use the Tutorial data to experiment with
new category definitions (see Help-Tutorial).
 
The Add, Edit, and Copy dialog boxes are identical except for the
following:
Pressing the Add button opens a dialog box titled Add New
Category; it is blank except for a Name field (of type name),
which is required in every category. ). You can edit the field
to change its label. Regardless of how you label it, the value
contained in this field is used by the various view types as a
key for accessing items.
Pressing the Edit button opens a dialog box titled Category
Definition; it is filled in with the definition of the selected
category. You can change the name and the definition of any
of the fields, but not a field's type.
Pressing the Copy button opens a dialog box is titled Copy
Category; it is filled in with the definition of the selected
category, except that the Category Name is blank.
 
Note: Copy Category does not copy items or the connections to
the new category, just the category definition (i.e., number and
types of fields, etc.).
 
Once you have chosen to add, edit, or copy the selected
category, the Fields list box will be displayed.
FIELDS LIST BOX
This list box displays the labels of the currently defined fields for
the category. Highlight a field label in the list box and select one
of the Add, Edit, or Delete buttons (described below) to add,
edit, or delete the definition of the highlighted field.
 
Shortcut: Double-click the left mouse button while pointing at a
field in the Fields box to edit a field's definition without selecting
the Edit button.
ADD BUTTON
Select this button to add a field label and definition to the
category. The Add Field dialog box is displayed.
 
Note: After you Add a new field, you can select the Edit button
to change that field's type (regardless of whether you are
adding, editing, or copying a category). After you select the Save
button to save the category definition, however, the field type is
made permanent and you cannot change it in subsequent editing
sessions.
EDIT BUTTON
Select this button to edit a field label of an existing field, or the
field label and/or type of a newly added field. The Edit Field
dialog box is displayed.
 
You cannot change the field type of a field unless the field was
just added using the Add button (see above). Once you select
the Save button (see below), field types become permanent.
DELETE BUTTON
This button deletes the highlighted field from the category. You
can delete a field of any type except a name field type.
 
Caution: If you are editing an existing category, the field is
deleted from every existing item and any data in the field is lost
once you select the Save button (see below). If you want to
transfer this existing data to a new field with the type you want,
use the File-Export-By Category command to export the data
values from the old field, define a new field with the type you
want, and then use File-Import to import the data values. After
you do this, you can safely delete the old field.
 
Similarly, Commence deletes a field from any detail forms and
views that use the category.
 
If you have any agents that depend on or use the field you
deleted, you should edit the agent and ensure that it still
functions as intended (this includes the predefined agents
delivered with Commence). See Customize-Database-Agent.
SAVE BUTTON
The changes you make to category definitions are not saved
until you select this button. Once you select the Save button,
Commence saves the new category definition or changes the
definition of the edited category; it also updates an existing
category's detail form(s) and any views that use the category
with the changed category definition. Commence creates an
initial form and default report view for a new category definition.
ADVANCED BUTTON
This button displays the Advanced Category Options dialog box,
from which you can specify a clarify field and create secondary
indices.
Calculation Field Using the Fields button and the
arithmetic operators + (add), - (subtract), * (multiply),
and / (divide), insert an equation for the calculation
field into the Equation text box.
 
Calculation fields can contain a formula using up to 256
characters, including the +, -, *, and / operators, parentheses ( )
for grouping, and the currency symbol selected via the Windows
Control Panel (in the United States and some other countries, this
is the dollar sign $).
 
The Fields button displays the Select Field dialog box, from
which you select an existing currency, number, date, or time field
to insert into the equation. All fields for the equation must already
be defined to be used in the equation. All inserted fields must be
separated by one of the arithmetic operators.
Check Box Field
When adding or editing a category, you can select the Initial
State of a check box field by selecting one of the Checked or Not
Checked radio buttons. The default is Not Checked.
 
Agents with Edit Item or Add Item actions can be used to check or
uncheck a checkbox. When inserting a Value for a checkbox field
on the Field Values dialog box, the entire contents of the Value
text box must contain values of On, 1, True, or Yes to indicate a
Checked condition; anything else is interpreted as a Not
Checked condition.
 
Note: In Agents, a value of Checked does not indicate a Checked
condition; it will be interpreted as Not Checked.
Data/Excel Cell/Image File Field These three field
types have identical options.
 
Options are limited to a Mandatory Field check box. If you turn
on the Mandatory Field check box, Commence will not let you
save an item detail on which this field appears unless there is a
value in this field.
Date Field
If you enter a value into the Default text box, this value will be
displayed as the default value for this field whenever you add a
new item to the category. Default values for a date field must be
either a date in the format set via the Windows Control Panel, or
a natural language date less than 255 characters. See Natural
Language Dates for more information.
 
The valid date range accepted by Commence extends from
January 1, 1600, through December 31, 2999. This makes it easy
to add the birthday of a favorite classical composer to a musical
library database.
 
With the expanded range of valid dates available, the Short Date
Range preference (Edit- Preferences - Other - Short Date
Range) takes on greater importance when evaluating a two–digit
year within a date field. For example, if the Short Date Range
preference is set to be 1930–2029, then the date 25 Jul 49 would
be evaluated as July 25, 1949. However, if the Short Date
Range preference is set to be 1950–2049, then the date 25 Jul
49 would be evaluated as July 25, 2049.
 
If you turn on the Mandatory Field check box, Commence will
not let you save an item detail on which this field appears unless
there is a value in this field.
 
If you turn on the Recurring Date check box, a Recurring Item
check box will appear on any detail form that includes this field.
See Date Field for a description of how the Recurring Item check
box and the recurring date field are used.
E-Mail Address Field
This field type that is used specifically for electronic mail (E–Mail)
addresses. Double clicking on an address within this field will
open up a blank mail note (using the default e–mail software) with
the To: field automatically filled in with the selected address.
Commence allows up to eighty (80) characters for each e–mail
address
In order for Commence to automatically address an electronic
message, a compatible electronic mail program must be installed
and properly configured. Some examples of supported e–mail
applications are Microsoft Exchange, Lotus Notes, and Lotus
cc:Mail.
Once Commence has been configured properly with the e–mail
information, the next step is to include e–mail address information
in a database. If there are multiple mail system protocols in use, a
prefix can be used in order to identify which protocol is to be
used. For example, entering MAPI:fred will use MAPI protocol to
send an e–mail message to address fred. In addition to MAPI,
Commence recognizes VIM and CMC. The corresponding mail
programs must be installed and configured properly in order to
take advantage of this feature.
Complete the following information on the E-Mail Address Add
Field Dialog Box:
Field Label - this is the name of the field and will be used in all
instances where field labels are displayed.
Default - if there is a default address used, enter that address in
this area. This address will be stored in the E-Mail field in the
event no other e-mail address is entered into the field.
Mandatory Field - if an e-mail address is required information,
checking this box will assure that an item is not saved with an
empty E-Mail field.
The new E–Mail Address field is automatically added to all Detail
Forms associated with the category. If there are multiple tabs for
any of the Detail Forms, the field is automatically added to the
appropriate tab.
Internet Address (URL) Field
Commence includes a field that is used specifically for Uniform
Resource Locator (URL) addresses. Double clicking on a URL
address will launch the Web Browser (if not already open) and go
directly to the indicated web site. Commence allows up to 1024
characters for each URL address.
In order for Commence to automatically display a homepage at a
specific URL address, a compatible Internet Browser must be
installed and properly configured. Two examples of supported
browsers are Microsoft Internet Explorer and Netscape Navigator.
Note
Commence uses the web browser that is defined as the default
browser.
 
For proper launching of any web browser, the full URL address
must be specified. For example, http://www.commence.com
must be specified, not just commence.com.
 
Complete the following information on the URL Address’ Add
Field dialog box:
Field Label – this is the name of the field, and will be used in all
instances where Field Labels are displayed. In this example, the
Field Label will be Home Page.
 
Default – if there is a default URL address to be used, enter that
address in this area. This address will be stored in the Home
Page field in the event no other URL address is entered into the
field.
 
Mandatory Field – if an Internet (URL) address is required
information, checking this box will assure that an item is not
saved with an empty Home Page field.
Number/Telephone/Time/E-Mail Address/Internet
Address Field
These five field types have identical options.
 
If you enter a value into the Default text box, this value will be
displayed as the default value for this field whenever you add a
new item to the category. Default values are limited to 255
characters, but are also limited by the type of the field (i.e a time
field must contain a time in the currently selected Windows
Control Panel format, or one of the time keywords listed in
Natural Language Times).
 
If you turn on the Mandatory Field check box, Commence will
not let you save an item detail on which this field appears unless
there is a value in this field.
Selection Field
A Selection Field may be displayed in one of two possible ways.
In situations where there are eight (8) or fewer choices, a Radio
Group can be used. For situations where there are more than
eight (8) choices, or when space within a detail form is limited, a
Combo Box should be used.
RADIO GROUP
The Options for a radio group field include a label for each of
eight possible radio buttons, and a Default radio group from
which you select the radio button you want selected by default
upon display of an add item detail that shows this field.
Type a label into the text box for each button that you want to
define. Only the buttons you label will appear in the radio group.
Caution: When you are editing radio group fields, special care
must be taken when attempting to change the number or order of
radio buttons, or the meaning of a radio button that already has
data associated with it. See Editing a Radio Group.
Agents with Edit Item or Add Item actions can be used to select
any button in a radio group. When inserting a Value for a radio
group field on the Field Values dialog box, the entire contents of
the Value text box must contain the label of one of the radio
group's buttons, or the value will be ignored. The button label is
case sensitive. If the value is ignored, the default button will be
used. Note that you can specify a field code from the trigger data
to be used as a value for the radio group, but the trigger data
field's value must translate into a button name or it will be
ignored.
COMBO BOX
Each combo box can have up to 4000 selections from which to
choose. Each individual entry may be 60 characters long. Define
the Combo Box Selections as follows:
Add - adds a Combo box selection to the list of current
selections.
Edit - permits the Combo Box selection currently highlighted to
be modified.
Move Up - moves the currently selected Combo Box selection up
a position within the list of current selections. Used for
customizing the order in which all selections are positioned.
Move Down - moves the currently selected Combo Box selection
down a position within the list of current selections. Used for
customizing the order in which all selections are positioned.
Caution: Once a selection has been defined within a Combo Box,
it cannot be deleted later. If a selection becomes unnecessary at
a later date, the text associated with the location can be edited
(for example, to read Unused or Unavailable). Modifying the text
for a single location does not affect the text for any other location.
The obsolete selection (now labeled Unused) may then be
moved down to the bottom of the list so that it is “out of the way.”
Sequence Number Field
The Options for a sequence number field are limited to an Initial
Value. This value is placed in the field for the first item added,
and is incremented by one with each subsequent item added. The
default Initial Value is 1. When adding a new sequence number
field, you can change the Initial Value to any numeric value less
than 2,000,000,000.
 
Note: You change the initial value for an existing field by deleting
the sequence number field and readding it. Any existing items in
the category will be resequenced starting with the new initial
value.
Text Field
The Total Size of a text field is, by default, Small (eighty
characters or fewer). Alternatively, you can select a Large text
field (up to 30,000 characters), or select the Limit to option and
type a number of characters in the text box provided. Commence
will limit text input for the field to the number of characters you
specify.
 
If you enter a value into the Default text box, this value will be
displayed as the default value for this field whenever you add a
new item to the category. Default values are limited to 255
characters or the Total Size you specify, whichever is smaller.
 
If you turn on the Mandatory Field check box, Commence will
not let you save an item detail on which this field appears unless
there is a value in this field.
Timer Trigger
A Timer trigger fires at a given date and time, or at a given
frequency. The Timer trigger does not depend on data in
Commence, so its specification is limited to selecting the date and
time parameters. Use the Timer Frequency drop-down list box to
